Romania

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive has been implemented in the Romanian legislation through Title
VII of the Romanian Fiscal Code, starting | January 2016, the Law no, 27/2015 regarding the Fiscal
Code and the related Methodological Norms, the Government Decision no. 1/2016 for approving the
Methodological Norms for the application of the Law no, 27/2015.

1 Taxable Persons

11 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Taxable persons established in Romania for VAT purposes, whose annual turnover is no higher than the
equivalent in national currency of EUR 88,500 at the conversion rate communicated by the National Bank
of Romania on the day of Romania’s accession to the EU (i.e. RON 300,000), are not liable to register for
VAT purposes in Romania, being subject to the special exemption regime for small entrepreneurs. However,
taxable persons may opt at any time for the application of the regular VAT regime.

Anewly set-up company may benefit from the special VAT exemption regime to the extent that, when starting
its economic activity, the taxable person declares an estimated tumover below the above-mentioned threshold
and does not opt for the application of the regular VAT regime.

‘The taxable person that applies the special VAT exemption regime and its annual turnover is equal or exceeds
the comparable threshold should request the VAT registration within the first 10 days of the month following
the one in which the threshold was reached or exceeded. The special VAT exemption regime will be applied
until the date when the VAT registration is obtained by the taxable person.

The VAT registration process is separate from the legal process of incorporating a company and separate VAT
registration forms need to be filed with the tax authorities, both when registering for VAT purposes at the
time of incorporation and when registering for VAT purposes later, once the above-mentioned threshold is
exceeded.

Depending on the moment in time when the domestic taxable person applies for a VAT registration (i.e.
upon incorporation with the Trade Registry or later on), the following forms must be submitted with the tax
authorities:

+ upon the incorporation with the Trade Registry: The fiscal registration request in accordance with
provisions of Order 2509/2022;

+ _ after the incorporation with the Trade Registry: Form 700 - Declaration for electronic registration/
amendments/de-registration subsequent to tax registration (which can be downloaded at the following
link: hitps://tinyurl.com/3u966vvz; and

+ registration for VAT purposes in the event that the VAT identification number of the taxable person has
been cancelled: Form 700 - Declaration for electronic registration/amendments/de-registration subsequent
to tax registration (which can be downloaded at the following link: hitps.//tinyurl.com/y53cc29x

Starting 14 May 2020, companies applying for a Romanian VAT number will no longer be subject to a risk
analysis prior to the approval of the VAT registration. Such analysis will be carried out afterwards. Ifa taxable
person has a high fiscal risk, the Romanian tax authorities can cancel the VAT number.

Please note thal as of | February 2017, Form 088 - Statement of own responsibility for the evaluation of the
intent and capacity to perform an economic activity involving VAT operations has been cancelled and it is no
longer required.

1.2 _ Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

There is no VAT registration threshold for foreign taxable persons who must register for VAT purposes in
Romania.

Romania | SOL

--- Page 2 ---
The legislative updates of the VAT Directive regarding the exemption for SME’s have not yet been transposed
into national legislation, No amendment draft of the national legislation was published up to 1 January 2025.

Depending on the type of transactions carried out in Romania, the foreign taxable persons are liable to
register for Romanian VAT purposes for instance prior to performing intra-~Community acquisitions of
goods or exempt intra~Community supplies of goods, or they could opt for registration in case of specific
transactions. The latter would apply in case of the importation of goods, rental and lease of immovable
property if the option to tax is exercised, the supply of buildings and land if the transaction is taxable by
default or if the option to tax is exercised.

Depending on the country where the foreign taxable person is established (i.e. within or outside the EU),

one of the following VAT registration procedures applies: direct registration or registration via a fiscal
representative. Thus, the forcign taxable person established within the EU can register in Romania directly or
through a fiscal representative, while the foreign taxable person established outside the EU can only register
by appointing a fiscal representative.

In practice, the direct VAT registration process can take approximately two weeks and three to four weeks
from the date of the application in the case of foreign taxable persons registered through a Romanian fiscal
representative.

Foreign taxable persons that register in Romania through the direct registration procedure must submit with

the Romanian tax authorities the following documents:

+ 015 VAT registration form, which can be downloaded at the following link: hétps://tinyurl.com/yf84cs7m:

+ documentation attesting that the foreign taxable person will perform taxable operations in Romania, for
which it is liable to pay VAT, such as agreements, purchase/sale orders;

+ notarised copy of the VAT certificate obtained by the forcign taxable person from the country where the
corporate seat is registered;

+ original or notarized copy of an excerpt from the Trade Registry from the country where the foreign
taxable person has registered his corporate seat; and

+ copy of the articles of incorporation.

Moreover, all of these documents must be accompanied by Romanian authorized translations and submitted
with the Tax Administration for non-established taxable persons within the General Directorate of the Public
Finance of Bucharest at the following address:

Piata Presei Libere no. 1, Building C3
District 1

Bucharest

Romania

Foreign taxable persons that register for VAT purposes via a fiscal representative must submit with the tax

authorities the following documents:

* statement of commencement of the activity that will include the date and the nature of the activity to be
performed on the Romanian territory;

+ an official request for the appointment of a Romanian company to act as their VAT fiscal representative in
Romania;

+ aspecific power of attomey attesting that the Romanian company was appointed to act as the VAT fiscal
representative of the foreign taxable person in Romania, to be issued by the non-resident directly in the
name of the Romanian company;

+ written acknowledgement of the Romanian company to act as the VAT fiscal representative for the foreign
taxable person and to fulfil, on its behalf, the VAT liabilities required by the law;

+ a copy of the foreign taxable person’s articles of incorporation;

* —acopy of the excerpt obtained from the Trade Registry where the non-resident is established;

= acopy of the non-resident’s VAT registration certificate or other fiscal registration certificate;

+ documents (agreements/purchase orders) as evidence of the transactions which create a liability to register
for VAT purposes in Romania; and
+ estimated turnover per year from transactions taxable in Romania.

All documents above must be accompanied by Romanian authorized translations.

13 VAT Grouping

Romania has implemented the optional clause of the EU VAT Directive concerning VAT grouping, but in a
form that is not fully compliant with the EU recommendations. According to the Romanian VAT rules, two.
or more taxable persons which are legally independent but are closely related in terms of organisational,
financial and economic purposes may choose to form a VAT group, provided that the following conditions are
met:

+ a taxable person may only be a member of a single VAT group;

+ the option should refer to a period of at least 2 years; and

+ all taxable persons should have the same fiscal period.

The concept of ‘a single VAT group’ is not the same as a single taxable person, since transactions carried out
among members of this group will still be treated as falling within the VAT scope, while the sole advantage of
the group is to offset their VAT positions at the end of each fiscal period (e.g. month, quarter).

Therefore, cach member of the group will keep its own VAT number. The VAT group does not have to
register for VAT purposes. Moreover, each member of the VAT group will file and send their VAT return
to the respective representative, while the representative of the VAT group will file a consolidated VAT
return, Please note that, each member of the VAT group is held jointly and severally liable for the VAT due
individually or by the other members of the group.

Taxable persons are considered closely linked from a financial, economic and organisational perspective if
their capital is directly or indirectly held for more than 50% by the same shareholders (as per the certificate
issued by the Trade Registry and/or other applicable supporting documents).

No specific provisions are provided by the Romanian VAT legislation with respect to the possibility of having
a holding as a member of the VAT group. Note that the members of the VAT group must be taxable persons

established in Romania, Thus, difficulties may be encountered in practice for VAT fixed establishments which,
although deemed as established for VAT purposes in Romania, are not also registered with the Trade Registry.

14 Fiscal Representative

Foreign taxable persons established in the EU who are liable to register for VAT purposes in Romania may
register either directly or indirectly (through a fiscal representative). Foreign taxable persons established
outside the EU can register in Romania solely by appointing a fiscal representative. VAT registration is
required for all transactions taking place for VAT purposes in Romania, when the non-resident qualifies as the
person liable to pay/account for VAT in Romania.

‘The requirements in order to obtain registration via a VAT fiscal representative in Romania are detailed in
paragraph 1.2.

Romanian taxable persons acting as fiscal representatives for foreign taxable persons are held jointly liable
with the foreign taxable person for all their VAT obligations arising in Romania.

Note that the appointment of a VAT fiscal representative in Romania occurs either as a result of the non-
resident's obligation to register for VAT purposes in Romania or as a result of its option to register for VAT
purposes in Romania for specific operations (e.g. imports). The VAT advantages which may be obtained
further to a VAT registration via a fiscal representative are similar to the ones applicable to taxable persons
established for VAT purposes in Romania, For instance it will be possible to defer payment of the import VAT
and apply, if additional conditions are met, the exemption on transactions in VAT/excise warehouses.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Romania has implemented the optional clause provided by Articles 19 and 29 of the EU VAT Directive with
respect to the transfer of a going concern.

According to the Romanian VAT legislation, the total or partial transfer of a business performed through the
transfer of assets or, as the case may be, liabilities as well, performed as a result of other operations than
spin-off or merger, such as sale or a contribution in kind to the share capital, is considered as outside the VAT
scope, provided that the transferee is a taxable person established in Romania. The total or partial transfer
of a business performed in connection with a spin-off or a merger is considered as outside the VAT scope,
provided that the transferee is a taxable person established in Romania. The transferee must be a taxable
person in order for the transaction to be seen as outside the VAT scope. Otherwise, the transferor should
account for VAT upon the transfer of the business to a non-taxable transferee.

The Norms to the Fiscal Code provide that the transfer of assets is deemed as a generic transfer of goods and/
or services, that are not treated individually but as a whole at the level of the transferor, irrespective of the
transaction’s representing a total or partial transfer of assets. Also, the partial transfer of assets is defined as
the transfer of the assets invested in a certain business unit that represent (from a technical perspective} an
independent structure able to perform separate economic activities.

The transferee shall be considered as the successor of the transferor, irrespective if it is registered or not for
VAT purposes. Therefore, the transferee is deemed to be the successor in respect of the rights and obligations
of the transferor, including those with respect to VAT adjustments and self-supplies.

In principle, as per the Romanian VAT rules, the transfer of assets qualifies as a transfer of going concern,

only to the extent that the following conditions are cumulatively met:

+ the assets that are subject to the transfer belong to a certain business line of a taxable person;

+ the transferred assets constitute (from a technical perspective) an independent structure able to perform
‘economic activities by itself; and

+ the transferee should be a taxable person, who contioues the transferred business line and does not
intend to liquidate it immediately. In this respect, an affidavit should be provided by the transferee to the
transferor in order to prove that such a condition is met.

The above requirements do not apply to spin-off and merger, which are in any case considered a transfer of
assets outside the VAT scope.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

The concept generally includes any action that takes goods or services out of a business, not charged for, to
the extent that input VAT on the acquisition of the goods and/or services was previously deducted in full or in
part. Such transactions include gifts or ‘self-supplies’, meaning the private use of business assets.

Self-supplies include:

+ Goods that staff take out of the business for personal use, either temporarily or permanently;

+ Services supplied to a business but then used privately; and

+ The taking over by taxable person of tangible goods acquired or produced for purposes that are not related
to the taxable person’s economic activity, if the VAT for such goods or for the component parts of the
goods was wholly or partially deducted,

However, the following operations are not deemed to be ‘supplies of goods’ under the provisions of the

Romanian Fiscal Code:

+ goods granted free of charge from the state reserve as foreign or domestic humanitarian aid;

+ goods granted free of charge for advertising purposes or to stimulate sales or, in general terms, for the
pursuit of the economic activity, under specific conditions provided by the law;

+ small value goods granted free of charge, within sponsorship (i.e. the total value during a calendar year

504 | Romania

--- Page 5 ---
is within the limit of 3%o of the turnover) or protocol actions (i.e. RON 100 excl. VAT), under specific
conditions provided by the law; and

+ transfer of food intended for human consumption, which is close to the expiry of the minimum durability
date, when carried out according to the legal provisions regarding the reduction of food waste.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

It is possible to apply a VAT exemption on the importation of goods into Romania if it is followed by an

intra-Community supply. In order to apply the VAT exemption, there are certain conditions that must be met,

as follows:

* VAT identification number of the importer;

+ VAT identification number of the beneficiary; and

+ proof that the imported goods will be transported or dispatched from the EU Member State of importation
to the other EU Member State in the same state as they were at the moment of import.

2.3.2 Pestponed accounting on importation

‘The payment of VAT on importation can be deferred only by importers registered for VAT purposes in

Romania under the normal registration procedure and in the following cases as per the last amendments of the

VAT legislation:

* taxable persons that obtained a VAT payment deferral certificate (which is valid for 6 months) and if the
foliowing conditions are met:

* no outstanding tax liabilities or other budget liabilities as identified in executory titles issued
according to law;

+ an own liability statement confirming that the respective taxable person has no outstanding tax
liabilities is submitted to the customs authorities;

= no outstanding debts due to customs authorities;

* recording an overall value of imports in the last 6 months before the one in which the request is
submitted, of at least RON 50 million (approximately EUR 10 million). Such taxable persons should be
registered for VAT purposes in Romania for at least 6 months before the application is requested; and

+ the respective taxable person is not subject to insolvency or compulsory liquidation or winding-up
proceedings;

* — imports carried out by taxable persons who submit customs declarations using the centralized clearance
procedure:

+ imports carried out by submitting a customs declaration as entry in the declarant’s records; and

+ imports of certain goods such as:

+ wood and wood products;

+ cereals and technical plants, including oilseeds and sugar beet, which are not in principle intended as
such for final consumers (goods falling within certain NC codes);

+ mobile phones;

+ — integrated circuit devices such as microprocessors and central processing units, before their
integration into end-user products; and

+ game consoles, tablet PCs and laptops.

The VAT payment deferral certificate may be revoked as soon as any of the above conditions cease to be
fulfilled or the VAT identification number is cancelled. The revocation is notified in writing to the certificate
holder, along with the reasons which led to such measure.

35 Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Romania has implemented simplification measures for consignment/call-off stock transactions through
Government Ordinance no. 6/2020 for the application of Law no. 227/2015 regarding the Fiscal Code.
The quick fixes for call-off stock arrangements/consignment stock arrangements conditions are different

Romania | 505

--- Page 6 ---
from those previously stated in Ministry of Public Finance Order no. 4120/2015. Under the new conditions, a
taxable established person established in one Member State which transfers goods to another taxable person
in another Member State, within a call-off stock arrangement, does not perform a transfer and, therefore, does
not have to register for VAT purposes in the territory where the goods are in stock if the following conditions
are cumulatively met:

+ the goods are shipped by a taxable person to another Member State in order to be supplied in that Member
State to another taxable person, based on a previous agreement;

+ the taxable person is not established and does not have a fixed establishment in the Member State to
which the goods are shipped;

+ the taxable person to which the goods are shipped is registered for VAT purposes in the Member State to
which the goods are shipped, with its identity and VAT identification number known to the taxable person
shipping the goods; and

+ the taxable person shipping the goods records the transfer of goods in a special ledger provided by
Regulation no. 1912/2018 and includes in the recapitulative statement both the identity and VAT code of
the taxable person who purchased the goods.

‘The taxable person has to register for VAT purposes and declare an assimilated intra~-Community acquisition
of goods in the following situations: if any of the above-mentioned conditions are not met, when goods stay
in stock for more than 12 months, goods are destroyed or stolen, or the goods are sent to another Member
State or exported.

Romania has applied the simplifications measures under the reciprocity conditions provided under Order
4120/2015 until 3 February 2020.

Previously, the simplification arrangements were applicable on a reciprocal basis (i.e. these can be applied
only if the EU Member State from which the goods are dispatched to Romania considers the initial movement
of goods as a non-transfer). No simplification measures are applicable in case of imports. The simplification
measures basically avoid VAT registration requirements in Romania for the consignor/supplier by considering,
such a transfer as falling outside the scope of Romanian VAT.

Apart from the reciprocity condition, the following criteria need to be cumulatively met in order to enable the

application of the simplification measures:

+ the consignor/supplier is neither established nor registered for VAT purposes in Romania;

+ the consignee/customer is registered for VAT purposes in Romania; and

+ the consignee/customer must be known to the consignor/supplier when the goods are transported from the
other EU Member State to Romania.

3.2 Installation supplies

Please note that no formal definition of the ‘supply with installation or assembly” is provided by the
Romanian VAT legislation.

According to the Romanian Fiscal Code, the place of supply of goods subject to installation or assembly is,
considered to be the place where such goods are installed or assembled, by or on behalf of the supplier.

The so-called ‘extended’ reverse charge mechanism applies if the supplier is a taxable person not established
- nor deemed as established for VAT purposes - in Romania and which is not registered for VAT purposes
either. Thus, the person liable to account for VAT is the taxable person or the non-taxable person established
in Romania, irrespective of its being registered or not for VAT purposes, or the non-resident taxable person
registered for VAT purposes in Romania, which is the customer of the supply taking place in Romania,

33 Distance sales

Starting | July 2021, revised distance sales rules under the provisions of the Romanian Fiscal Code
transposed Article 33 of the EU VAT Directive, as amended by Council Directive (EU) 2017/2455 of
December 5, 2017 (the ‘VAT e-commerce rules’)

‘As of | July 2021, under the Romanian Fiscal Code, distance sales are, generally, deliveries of goods sent or
transported by the supplier or a third party into an EU Member State from a point of origin outside that EU

506 | Romania

--- Page 7 ---
Member State to a recipient who is an unregistered taxable person or a person otherwise not subject to VAT,
inchiding a non-taxable legal person.

The goods must not be new means of transport.

There is a distinction between intra~Community distance sales of goods, which are dispatched or transported
from an EU Member State other than the Member State of destination, and distance sales of goods imported
from third (erritories or third countries.

Under the provisions of the Romanian Fiscal Code, the place of supply or delivery of distance sales of low
value goods imported from outside the EU directly into the Member State of final destination is that Member
State, provided that VAT on the goods is declared under the One Stop Shop, discussed below.

The place of supply of distance sales of goods imported from outside the BU into a Member State other than
that in which dispatch or transport of the goods to the customer ends is the place where the goods are located
at the time when the dispatch or transport of the goods to the customer ends.

The place of supply of intra-Community distance sales of goods is generally the place where the goods are
located at the time when the dispatch or transport of the goods to the customer ends.

However, the place of supply of intra-Community distance sales is the Member Siate where the supply begins

{unless an election to the contrary is made) if:

* The supplier operates his company in one EU Member State and has no fixed establishment outside of
that state;

* Goods are delivered to another Member State; and

+ The total value, exclusive of VAT, of distance sales to that other Member State, plus B2C TBE services,
does not exceed the E-commerce Distance Sales Threshold of EUR 10,000 or RON 46,337.

Suppliers who meet this ‘de minimis’ exception may nonethcless opt to have their distance sales taxed in the
Member State of destination under the default place of supply rule for distance sales. This election, if made,
remains in force for at least two calendar years.

No de minimis exception applies to distance sales of goods imported from outside the EU.

A special One Stop Shop (OSS) scheme applies to simplify reporting on distance sales, enabling suppliers
both within and outside the EU to account for distance sales in a single Member State of registration, if
specified conditions apply. This scheme also applies to qualifying cross-border services.

In addition, as of 1 July 2021, electronic marketplace operators assume liability for low value distance
sales effected through them from outside the EU if certain conditions are met. If the electronic marketplace
assumes liability, the supplier is not required to register for VAT.

3.4 Simplified triangulation

Romania has implemented simplified triangulation rules in line with the EU VAT Directive.

The following conditions must be cumulatively met for applying the simplified triangulation rules:

+ party B is not established in Romania for VAT purposes, but is registered for VAT purposes in another EU
Member State (different than the one of dispatch of the goods);

+ party B purchases the goods with the intention of reselling them;

+ the goods are transported by the initial supplier party A, party B or a third party on account of any of
the two (i.e. not by the Romanian final customer or by a third party on its account), directly to Romania
from a EU Member State, other than the one where party B is registered for VAT purposes, to the final
customer of the goods (party C);

+ party C is a taxable person or a non-taxable legal person, registered for VAT purposes in Romania;

+ party C in Romania is appointed by party B as the person liable to pay VAT in Romania on its supply; and

+ party B may be registered for VAT purposes in Romania but should not be established for VAT purposes
in Romania,

Romania | 507

--- Page 8 ---
Ina triangular (ABC) transaction, where party C is registered for VAT purposes in Romania, the following

obligations would arise for C:

+ toaccount for VAT under the reverse charge mechanism on the domestic supply performed by party B
which is not established for VAT purposes in Romania; and

+ torecord the acquisition in the VAT return and in the EU sales and purchases listing in boxes dedicated to
taxable intra-Community acquisition of goods, as well as in the EU sales and purchases listing.

In a triangular (ABC) transaction, where the intermediary is a Romanian entity, which provided the first

supplier with its Romanian VAT identification number, the following obligations would arise for party B:

+ B should not record in the EU sales and purchases listing the intra-Community acquisition of the goods,
such operation being deemed as non-taxable in Romania. The transaction should be included in the VAT
return in the line referring to non-taxable acquisitions;

» to issue an invoice in line with the formal requirements of the Romanian VAT legislation, that contains its
Romanian VAT identification number, as well as the VAT identification number of the final customer (C).
B should also appoint C as the person liable to pay VAT on the domestic supply of goods taking place in
the third EU Member State under the ‘reverse-charge’ mechanism; and

+ B should report in its VAT return the supply of goods towards the final customer in EU Member State (C)
in the box dedicated to intra~-Community supplies and report the following in its EU sales and purchases
listing,:

+ the VAT identification number of the final customer from EU Member State (C);
+» the T-code in the relevant heading; and
+ the value of the supply.

3.5

Intra-Community acquisitions of goods in the current calendar year or in the previous one not exceeding
the threshold of RON 34,000 (EUR 10,000, computed at the exchange rate published by the National Bank
of Romania valid at the date of Romania accession to the EU) are not taxable in Romania, provided that
such intra-Community acquisitions are performed by non-taxable persons or by taxable persons exclusively
carrying out operations without VAT deduction right.

3.6 Effective Use & Enjoyment

Romania has implemented the following use and enjoyment rules in its national legislation:

+ services ancillary to transport, like loading, unloading, manipulation and other similar services, work
on and valuation of movable goods and transport of goods actually performed in Romania for a non-BU
taxable person, shall be taxed in Romania; and

+ transportation of goods performed outside the European Union (i.e. the starting point and the point of
arrival are located outside the European Union) for the benefit of a taxable person established in Romania,
shall not be taxed in Romania.

4, Chargeable event & Chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

Under the Romanian Fiscal Code, the chargeable event and chargeability generally occur on the date of delivery
of goods / supply of services, subject to certain special rules for specific types of supplies, such as real estate or
goods supplied under a contract of consignment building — assembly services, consultancy, research, expertise
and similar services. Further, VAT may become chargeable on the earlier of the following dates, if they occur
before the date of delivery (or before the dates referred to under the special rules), namely the date:

+ the invoice is issued (including for an advance payment before receiving it); or

+ the supplier receives an advance payment of partial or full consideration for the supply.

Special rules are provided on when VAT becomes chargeable under the cash accounting scheme

508 | Romania

--- Page 9 ---
4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied.

In the case of supplies other than intra-Community supplies, a taxable person must issue an invoice for the
amount of advances received in connection with a supply of goods or provision of services no later than the
15 day of the month following the month in which the advance is received, unless the invoice has already
been issued.

43 Continuous supplies

In the case of continuous supplies other than building -a sembly services, consultancy, research, expertise
and similar services, such as supply of gas, water, electricity, telephone services, rental, leasing, concession,
lease of goods, granting of rights in rem, such as the right of usufruct and the right of superficies, over
immovable property for a certain period of time, and other similar supplies, the chargeable event occurs at
the payment date settled in the agreement or, in case the payment date is not stipulated, when the invoice is
issued, but the settlement period cannot exceed one year.

However, if the agreement does not mention a payment date and the chargeable event occurs when the
invoice is issued, the payment date mentioned on the invoice has no impact on the chargeable event (i.c.
chargeable event still occurs when the invoice is issued). By way of exception, continuous supplies of goods
(other than gas, electricity, heating or cooling) over a period of more than one calendar month which are
dispatched or transported from Romania to another EU Member State and which are supplied or transferred
VAT exempt, shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the
supply comes to an end.

Services ongoing over a period of more than 1 year, that do not give rise to settlements or payments in that
period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT (reverse-charge mechanism), are regarded
as being supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time the provision of the services ceases.

44° Cash accounting

Romania implements an optional VAT cash-accounting system applicable to taxable persons established in
Romania whose annual turnover does not exceed the threshold of RON 4,5 million. The VAT cash-accounting
system is characterized by the fact that charging VAT occurs when an invoice is fully or partially cashed-in
Therefore, the payment of the VAT to the state budget must be done by the 25% day of the month following
the fiscal period when the VAT becomes chargeable under the “cash-accounting system.”

‘The VAT cash-accounting system does not apply to certain transactions, including transactions:

+ with affiliated parties;

+ for which the person liable to pay VAT is the beneficiary (using the reverse charge mechanism);

+ for which the place of supply is not considered to be in Romania:

+ that are VAT-exempt; or

+ that are subject to special schemes for travel agents and supplies of secondhand goods, works of art,
collector’s items, antiques and investment gold.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

VAT on sales made to a customer can be adjusted (i.e., output VAT can be decreased) as of the date of court
decision confirming a reorganization plan, or a ruling/ court decision confirming the date of the initiation of
bankruptcy procedure,

The right of adjusting the taxable base will arise from the date of the court decision or, where appropriate,

Romania | 509

--- Page 10 ---
of the court’s conclusion by which bankruptcy was decided, without waiting for the conclusion of the
bankruptcy procedure.

In order to align with the jurisprudence of the CJEU, new provisions are introduced with respect to the
adjustment of the taxable base for bad debts from individuals (with the exception of affiliated individuals)
within 12 months from the payment deadline set by the parties or, in its absence, from the date of the invoice.
In this respect, the adjustment will be allowed within a S year period starting from the 1“ of January of the
year following the one when the payment deadline was set by the parties or, in its absence, the year following
the one when the invoice was issued. The adjustment would be permitted if proof is provided that commercial
measures have been taken to recover claims up to RON 1,000 or legal proceedings have been taken to recover
claims in a higher amount than RON 1,000.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
19% VAT EUR_190
Total to be paid EUR 1,190

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier has to pay RON 855 (approximately
EUR 190) in its VAT return, The customer can deduct this RON 855 (BUR 190) in its VAT return, Therefore,
the supplier paid too much VAT and the customer deducted too much.

In such case, as the discount occurred after the invoice issuance, the supplier has to adjust its taxable amount,
by issuing a credit invoice for the 2%. The invoice should be issued to the customer, who is also required to
adjust the initially exercised VAT deduction right,

5.3 Open market value

Ifa beneficiary is affiliated to a supplier of goods or provider of services, the taxable amount is deemed to be

the market value in certain situations that may give rise to VAT evasion. The situations are as follows, namely,

where the consideration is:

+ less than the market value and the beneficiary of the supply does not have a full right of deduction;

+ Jess than the market value and the supplier or the provider does not have a full right of deduction while
the supply is VAT exempt without deduction right; and

+ higher than the market value and the supplier or the provider does not have a full right of deduction.

5.4 Returnable packaging

According to the Romanian Fiscal Code, packaging that is exchanged between a supplier and a buyer without
being included in the invoice, shall not be included in the taxable amount even if the buyer does not hold

the quantity of packaging necessary for the exchange and pays a monetary guarantee in exchange for the
packaging received.

On the other hand, when the packaging was included in the invoice and therefore in the taxable amount, the
taxable amount must be adjusted by the supplier by issuing a credit invoice if the buyer returns the packages
in which the commodity was dispatched.

An Emergency Ordinance was also published including clarifications on the VAT treatment applicable to non-

reusable packaging subject to Return Guarantee Scheme. The main points of the provisions state that:

+ the guarantee for non-reusable packaging subject to Return Guarantee Scheme charged by manufacturers,
distributors and traders is not deemed as consideration for a supply of goods subject to VAT;

+ non-reusable packaging subject to Return Guarantee Scheme which is not returned at the end of a
calendar year shall be deemed as a supply of goods subject to VAT effected by the Return Guarantee
Scheme administrator (i.e. Returo R omanian company managing the Return Guarantee Scheme). The

510 | Romania

--- Page 11 ---
chargeable event for the respective supply will occur on the last day of the calendar year and the tax point
date occurs at the submission deadline of the annual financial statements of Returo;

+ the taxable amount for the mentioned supply of goods shall be the difference between the value of
the guarantees received and the value of the guarantees returned by the Return Guarantee Scheme
administrator in a calendar year, exclusive of VAT; and

+ if the computed taxable amount is negative, i.e. the value of the returned guarantees is higher than the
value of the guarantees received in a calendar year, the Return Guarantee Scheme administrator shall
reduce accordingly the output VAT in the VAT return of the last tax period of that year;

6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate is 19% (from January 2017).

6.2 Reduced rate

Romania uses two reduced VAT rates: 9% and 5%.

The 9% reduced rate is used for the following:

+ supplies of medicines for human and veterinarian use;

+ food and non-alcoholic beverages intended for human and animal consumption (excluding non-alcoholic
beverages with 2202 NC, food with added sugar of at least | 0g/100g of product other than powdered
milk, sweet bread, biscuits and bee feed);

+ the supply of live domestic animals and poultry;

+ the supply of seed, plants and ingredients used in preparing food;

* the supply of certain food supplements and substitutes;

* supply of drinking water and water for irrigation;

+ the supply of fertilizers and pesticides used in agriculture, seeds and other agricultural products intended
for sowing and planting, and also the provision of the specific types of services used in agriculture;

+ water supply and sewerage services;

* accommodation in the hotel sector or similar-function sectors, including rental of camping grounds;

* restaurant and catering services, excluding alcoholic beverages other than beer (classified under NC code
22 03 00 10 - namely, beer made of malt, delivered in containers exceeding the capacity of 101 - ‘draft
beer’);

+ the supply of certain affordable and social housing such as land and buildings for old age pensioners and
for children, on and after | January, 2024:

+ the supply and installation of certain high-efficiency, tow-emission heating systems such as photovoltaic
panels and the supply and installation of repairing/extension components, on and after | January, 2024;

+ the supply of buildings with a maximum utilized area of 120 square meters, with value (land included) of
less than RON 600,000, excl. VAT; and

* access to amusement parks, recreational parks and sporting events, on and after | January, 2024.

The 5% reduced rate is used for the following:

+ manuals, books, newspapers and magazines;

* admission to castles, museums, memorial houses, zoos etc.;

«certain supplies of firewood, until December 31, 2029; and

+ thermal energy supplied in the cold season to certain specified categories of consumers.

63 Super-reduced and zero rates

No super-reduced rate was implemented in Romania, The Romanian VAT system does not have the concept
of a zero-rated supply. Instead, it uses the analogous concept of a VAT exemption with credit for related
input VAT (which generally allows VAT recovery by the taxable person for input VAT attributable to its own

Romania | S11

--- Page 12 ---
supplies, if certain conditions are met) or VAT exempt without credit for related input VAT (which does not
allow the VAT recovery by the taxable person for input VAT attributable to such output transactions).

Please see next chapter related to exemptions for more details.

1 Chapter 7 Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right of deduction

The main supplies eligible for a VAT exemption with credit for related input VAT, including exports and
related services, are set out in Articles 294-296 of the Romanian Fiscal Code.

Supplies VAT exempt with deduction right include:

* export of goods, transport of such goods and other closely related services;

+ intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ — international transport of passengers;

+ supplies of goods and services to the benefit of sea vessels and aircraft;

+ — supplies of goods intended to be placed under a VAT warehouse regime and related services;

+ supplies of goods placed under suspending customs arrangements and related services:

* certain operations performed in free zones and free warehouses;

+ supplies of services on goods acquired or imported into Romania for processing purposes that are
subsequently transported outside the Community;

«supplies of goods to diplomatic missions, international organisations and NATO forces; and

+ supplies of gold to the National Bank of Romania.

7.1.1 Intra~Community supplies

Intra-Community supplies of goods benefit from VAT exemption with credit. Specifically for goods,

other than means of transport and excisable products for which specific requirements apply, the following

documentation as applicable, should be held by the supplier to justify the VAT exemption:

+ an invoice containing all the mandatory information required under the Romanian Fiscal Code, including
the VAT registration number assigned to the customer by another Member State; and

+ the documents proving that goods were transported from Romania to another Member State.

The Romanian Fiscal Code provides that in order for the supply to qualify as exempt with credit, the taxable
person or non-taxable legal person to whom the supply is made must provide his or her VAT identification
number to the supplier.

In addition, the supplier must comply with the obligation to submit a duly completed EC Sales and Purchase
List.

For the proof of the intra-Community transport of goods, the following will be analysed:

+ Regulation 2018/1912 amending the Implementing Regulation (EU) no. 282/2011 regarding certain
exemptions for intra-Community operations; or

+ OMEP 103/2016 on the justification of the VAT exemption for intra-Community supplies of goods and
exports in situations not covered by the EC Regulation.

In case a taxable person meets the conditions provided by Art.45a of the Regulation, it is presumed that the
goods were transported intra~-Community, and the intra~-Community transport of the goods is justified based
on the documents mentioned in the Regulation.

In principle, taxpayers may choose between the two regulations governing the documentation required to
support the VAT exemption for intra-Community supplies. The Romanian Ministry of Finance has confirmed
through a Circular letter that taxpayers are allowed to apply either the Romanian rules, which may be more
favourable, or the EU Regulation, without requiring proof of the reasons for choosing to apply the Romanian
rules.

512 | Romania

--- Page 13 ---
In case a taxable person opts to apply the Romanian legislation if it is more favourable, the intra-Community
transport of goods is justified according to the provisions of the Instructions for applying the VAT exemption
approved by Order no. 103/2016, by respectively:
+ adocument directly related to transport: a signed CMR document or a signed consignment note, a bill of
lading, the specific air waybill document; and
* an additional document of the following:
+ an insurance policy corresponding to the dispatch or transport of the goods;
* bank documents proving payment for the dispatch or transport of goods;
* official documents issued by a public authority, such as a notary, certifying the arrival of the good is
in the Member State of destination;
+ adocument certifying receipt of the goods, issued by a depositary in the Member State of destination,
other than the buyer of the goods; and
* awritten declaration from the buyer stating that the goods have been dispatched or transported to
the Member State of destination and including: date of issue, name and address of the buyer, as well
as the quantity and nature of the goods, date and place of arrival of the goods, identification of the
person who accepts the goods on behalf of the buyer.

Taxable persons can justify the intra-Community transport of goods with the documents provided under the
local regulation (as stipulated above) for situations such as, but not limited to:

* the supplier or the buyer carry out the transport of the goods with their own means of transportation;

* _ the supplied goods are means of transportation that move on their own by wheels, by sea, river or air;

+ _ the persons involved in the transport of the goods are not independent of each other, as well as of the seller.

It is presumed that the goods are transported to another Member State if two of the following means of proof
are held: documents relating to the dispatch or transport of the goods, such as a signed CMR document or
note, a bill of lading, an air freight invoice or an invoice from the goods carrier, or one means of proof of those
previously mentioned (issued by two different parties that are independent of each other and of the seller and
the buyer) and one document from the following: (i) an insurance policy with regard to the dispatch or transport
of the goods, or bank documents proving payment for the dispatch or transport of the goods; (ii) official
documents issued by a public authority, such as a notary, confirming the arrival of the goods in the Member
State of destination; (if) a receipt issued by a warehouse keeper in the Member State of destination, confirming
storage of the goods in that Member State. Additionally, for the cases in which the customer is in charge with
the transport of the goods, the supplier should be in possession of a written statement from the client, stating
that the goods have been transported by the customer or by a third party on behalf of the customer.

7.1.2 Export supplies

Under Romanian VAT law, the export of goods (i.e. dispatch of goods outside the EU) is VAT-exempt with the
right to deduction, to enable the exporter to recover input VAT, provided certain conditions are met, Exports
of goods benefit from VAT exemption with the right to deduction of related input VAT, only to the extent that
the taxable person meets the requirements of Romanian VAT legislation. In particular, if'a tax audit takes
place, the exporter must provide the tax inspectors with:

* an invoice containing all the mandatory information provided by the Romanian Fiscal Code or, if the
transport of goods does not imply a transaction, the documents required by the customs authorities for
preparing the customs export statement; and

* one of the following documents:

* certification issued by the export customs office attesting that the export operation was finalized
or, the export notification certified by the export customs office in case of electronic custom export
statements; or

* the third copy of the single administrative document used as a customs export statement, certified by
the confirmation of exit from the customs office; or

* in the case of excise goods moved under suspension of excise duty using EMCS, the export report
submitted to the consignor certifying that the excise goods have left the EU territory.

In addition, the Romanian VAT regulations also provide that in the absence of an export declaration, the
export can also be documented by other means of proof confirming the goods actually left the EU territory (in
accordance with the conclusions set out in case C-275/18 Milan Vins).

Romania | 513

--- Page 14 ---
The VAT exemption is granted for supplies of services, including transport and ancillary transport services
directly related to the export of goods which contribute to the actual export and which ate rendered directly to
the exporter or recipient of the exported goods in accordance with the conclusions set out in case C-288/16 L. C.

‘The Romanian Ministry of Finance has issued a Circular letter clarifying the person entitled to apply the VAT
exemption for exports in case of chain supplics. According to the guidance set by the authorities, the person
entitled to apply the VAT exemption for exports depends as well on the person in charge of transport of goods.

From a VAT perspective, a person transporting goods outside the European Union in lack of a commercial
transaction is also deemed an exporter.

7.1.3. Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Only EU goods can be placed in a VAT warehouse. Goods which have not been imported yet, cannot be

stored in a VAT warehouse. VAT warehouses can only be used for:

+ excisable goods and other goods that are included in a list provided by the law. The goods can be stored in
any location situated in Romania which is defined as a fiscal warchouse by the Romanian law; and

+ other goods provided by the law (such as: potatocs, olives, cereals, raw sugar, copper, aluminium, zine,
tin), The warehouse must be a location situated in Romania which is defined as such by the Romanian
law.

As a general rule, the VAT warehouse regime includes a chain system of VAT exemptions consisting of:

+ the granting of temporary VAT exemptions for all transactions performed within the regime; and

+ the granting of definitive VAT exemptions upon a subsequent supply of the goods within the VAT
warehouse regime.

In principle, all transactions performed in connection with goods placed under the regime are definitively VAT
exempted, except for transactions performed towards the last customer which benefit from a temporary VAT
exemption.

The person liable to pay VAT is the person who causes goods to cease to be covered by the VAT warehouse
regime, except when the goods leave the VAT warehouse regime through an import, in which case the person
liable to pay the VAT is the importer.

In principle, any taxable person - irrespective if registered or not for VAT purposes in Romania - can place
goods in a VAT warehouse, as long as it identifies itself towards the warehouse keeper and issues invoices
compliant with the Romanian VAT legislation for its supplies.

Nevertheless, the warehouse keeper can only be a taxable person established in Romania and registered for
Romanian VAT purposes, which has been authorized for acting as such by the Ministry of Public Finance. The
warehouse keeper must keep special evidences and documents regarding the goods being placed or leaving
the regime.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

Excise warehouse refers to the physical place under the control of the competent tax authority where
excise goods may be manufactured, processed, held, received or dispatched under suspensive regime by
the authorized warehouse keeper (i.e. the natural or legal person authorized by the competent authority).
‘The suspension regime means that, during the production, processing, holding and movement of the goods,
payment of excise duty is suspended.

Only excise goods belonging to one of the following groups of excise goods may be produced, processed
and held under suspension of excise duty in a tax warehouse: alcohol and alcoholic beverages, manufactured
tobacco, energy products and electricity, The excise warehouse cannot be used for retail purposes of the
excise goods with certain exceptions such as:

+ excise warehouse supplying energy products to aircrafts or vessels;

+ those supplies which, irrespective of the consignee - final consumer or not - are carried out by means of

514 | Romania

--- Page 15 ---
tanks, reservoirs or other similar containers which are an integral part of the means of | transport carrying
out the delivery;

+ the supply of energy products for certain purposes, namely: other purposes than as motor fuel or heating,
fuel, dual use, for chemical reduction purposes and in electrolytic and metallurgical processes, electricity
when it represents more than 50% of the cost of a product, mineralogical processes;

* the sale of wine bottles from wine cellars or exhibitions within the excise warehouse authorized
exclusively to produce still and/or sparkling wines; and

* the sale of beer within the excise warehouse authorized exclusively to produce beer.

It is prohibited to hold an excise product outside the excise warehouse if the excise duty for that product has
not been levied. The holding of excise products outside the excise warehouse, for which it cannot be proved
that excise duty has been levied, shall entail the obligation to pay excise duty.

The production or processing of excise products outside the excise warehouse is prohibited, with certain
exceptions,

The Romanian legislation provides that the excise warehouse rules shall not apply to excise products subject
to a customs suspensive procedure or regime.

7.1.3.3 Bonded warehouse

The placing of goods under a customs bonded warehouse and their supply while placed in the bonded
warehouse follow the rules provided by the customs legislation. The VAT related to the goods placed in the
bonded warehouse only becomes chargeable when the goods are removed from them. The person liable to
pay VAT is the person who causes goods to cease to be covered by the customs warehouse regime.

Customs bonded warehouses are approved by, and supervised by, the customs authority, and goods may be
warehoused under specific conditions. Authorization to manage customs bonded warehouses is granted only
to taxable persons established in Romania.

‘The VAT exemption regarding the supply of goods placed under a customs warehouse can be justified with

the following documents:

+ the agreement concluded with the holder of the regime or with the beneficiary of the goods stored in a
customs bonded warehouse;

+ an invoice containing all the mandatory information provided by the Romanian VAT legislation; and

+ documents attesting that the goods were placed under a suspending VAT regime at the moment of the
supply,

7.2. Exemptions without the right to deduction

Supplies VAT exempt without deduction right include:

+ hospital treatment, medical care, and closely related operations carried out by authorised units;

+ services supplied as part of their profession by dentists and dental technicians, as well as the supply of
dental prostheses by dentists and dental technicians;

* provision of medical care and supervision services supplied by medical and paramedical personnel
according to the applicable legal provisions;

* transport of sick or injured persons in vehicles specially designed for this purpose, by authorized entities;

+ supplies of organs, blood and milk of human origin;

* educational activities provided by the Law on education no. 1/2011, with subsequent modifications and
completions, carried out by authorized public institutions and other units, including the vocational training
of adults, as well as supplies of services and the deliveries of goods closely related to such activities:

* tuition given privately by the teachers covering school or university education;

* supplies of services and of goods closely related to social assistance and/or social protection performed by
public institutions or other entities recognized as having a social character, including those provided for
the prosperous elderly;

* the supply of services and of goods closely linked to the protection of children and young persons by
bodies governed by public Jaw or by other organisations recognised as being devoted to social wellbeing;

+ supplies of services closely related to the practice of sports or physical education performed by
organisations without a patrimonial purpose for persons who practice sports or physical education;

Romania | 515

--- Page 16 ---
+ the supply of certain cultural services, and the supply of goods closely linked thereto, by bodies governed
by public law or by other cultural bodies recognised by the Ministry of Culture;

+ the supply of services and goods, by organisations whose activities are exempt in connection with fund-
raising events organised exclusively for their own benefit, provided that the exemption is not likely to
cause distortion of competition:

+ — services related to broadcasting of the public radio and television channels, other than commercial
activities;

+ public postal services, as well as the supply of goods in connection with such services;

+ the supply of services by independent groups of persons, who are carrying on an activity which is exempt
from VAT or in relalion to which they are not taxable persons, for the purpose of rendering their members
the services directly necessary for the exercise of that activity, where those groups merely claim from
their members exact reimbursement of their share of the joint expenses, provided that such exemption is
not likely to cause distortion of competition;

+ the supply of staff by religious or philosophical institutions for the purpose of the activities provided by
the law;

+ supplies of specific financial and banking services (e.g. the granting and negotiation of credit and the
management of credit by the person granting it; transactions, including the negotiation, regarding
the deposit or current accounts, payments, money transfers, receivables, checks and other negotiable
instruments, except for the recovery of receivables; administration of special investments funds; insurance
and/or reinsurance operations, as well as the supply of services in connection with insurance and/or
reinsurance operations that are performed by taxable persons who intermediate such operations, etc);

bets, lotteries and other forms of gambling organised by persons authorized according to law to carry out
such activities;

+ the rental, concession, lease and the leasing of immovable goods, the granting of real rights over
immovable goods such as usufruct and superficies, with the following exceptions: accommodations
performed in the hotel sector or sectors with a similar function, including the rental of land prepared for
camping; rental of spaces or locations for parking vehicles; the rental of equipment and machinery that is
affixed to immovable goods; and the rental of safes;

+ the supply of a building or parts thereof, and of the land on which it stands as well as of any other land.
By way of exception, the exemption does not apply to supply of a new building, parts of a building or
building land; and

+ the supply of goods which have been used for an exempt activity if the VAT relating to those goods has
not been deducted, as well as the supply of goods of which the acquisition was subject to an exclusion of
the deduction right or subject to a total limitation of the deduction right.

7.3 Option for taxation

‘Taxpayers may opt to tax supplies or leases of land (farmlands) and buildings (old buildings meaning that
first occupation or use of the building took place more than 2 years prior to the supply) otherwise exempted,
so that these otherwise exempt supplies become taxable and input VAT may be recovered. This option is
exercised by notifying the tax authorities. However, taxation is not permitted in connection with the sale of
goods previously leased under the VAT exemption regime.

8. Liability

81 Reverse charges

In Romania there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by non-resident suppliers to taxable persons and legal entities with a Romanian VAT

identification number established in Romania. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and
follows from Art. 44 and Art. 196 EU VAT Directive. In addition, there is also the reverse charge for the
supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in Romania.

8.1.1 General reverse charge — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

Ifa taxable person who is not established in Romania supplies goods or services, the reverse charge applies
if the customer is a taxable person or a legal non-taxable person established in Romania. The reverse charge

516 | Romania

--- Page 17 ---
even applies if the Romanian customer is not VAT registered in Romania. If the customer is not established
in Romania but has a VAT registration in Romania, the reverse charge also applies. If the supplier is VAT
registered in Romania, the reverse charge does not apply.

Note that the application of the reverse charge is mandatory and not optional.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 Directive)

According to the Romanian VAT rules, reverse charge applies on the following domestic supplies when

carried out between taxable persons registered for VAT purposes in Romania:

* waste and secondary raw materials resulting from its recovery;

+ wood and wood materials;

* gas certificates;

* electricity to taxable dealers (other cumulative conditions should be met, e.g. own consumption norm
of the taxable trader is negligible, the main activity consists of reselling the energy purchased), until 31
December 2026;

* natural gas to taxable dealers (other cumulative conditions should be met, e.g. own consumption norm:
of the taxable trader is negligible, the main activity consists of reselling the gas purchased), until 31
December 2026;

+ the supply of constructions, parts of constructions and plots of land for which VAT is applicable by law or
by option;

+ supply of investment gold, if taxable by option, and supplies of raw materials or semi-manufactured
products of a purity of 325 thousandths or greater performed to taxable persons;

+ _ supply of mobile phones, devices with integrated circuits, game consoles, PC tablets, laptops - for such
supplies, the VAT reverse charge mechanism is applicable provided that the value of the invoice is greater
than or equal to RON 22,500 (VAT excl.), until 31 December 2026;

+ supply of cereal crops including oilseed and sugar beet, until 31 December 2026;

* transfers of allowances for greenhouse gas emissions and green certificates relating to greenhouse gas
emissions, as defined in Directive 2003/87/CE, until 31 December 2026; and

* transfers of green certificates relating to energy production from renewable energy sources, as defined in
Axt(2\(h) of Law No, 220/2008, until 31 December 2026.

8.2 Joint and several liability

8.2.1 VAT group

All VAT members are jointly and severally liable for the individual VAT liabilities of the other group members
for the period when they formed part of the group.

8.2.2 VAT representative

AAT representative is, generally, a legal entity established and registered for VAT in Romania, It is jointly
and severally liable with the taxpayer for its VAT liabilities, without any cap,

8.2.3 Imports

The person providing representation for the importer, the person that submits the import customs declaration
or the VAT import declaration and the owner of goods may be held jointly and severally liable for the
payment of VAT, together with the importer,

8.2.4 Bonded Warehouses

To apply the VAT bonded warehouse procedure, the bonded warehouse keeper and the cartier of goods from
the bonded warehouse or the person in charge of the transport may be held jointly and severally liable for the
payment of VAT, together with the person liable to pay it.
