--- Page 18 ---
9}. Recovery of input VAT

9 Non-recoverable input VAT

As a general rule, VAT related to goods and services not intended to be used for business purposes is non-
recoverable, Also, the VAT related to the acquisition of alcoholic beverages and tobacco products is not
recoverable, except when such goods are subject to re-sale or are used as part of supplies of services, 50%

of the VAT related to purchases, intra-Community acquisitions, importation, rental and leasing of passenger
cars, as well as for expenditures thereof (c.g. fuel, repairs, operating expenses) related to vehicles which have
a maximum authorised total weight below 3,500 kg or with no more than 9 passenger seats including the
driver’s seat, owned or used by the taxable person and which are not used exclusively for business purposes is
non-recoverable.

The Romanian VAT legislation provides certain exceptions, whereby, the entire VAT is deductible if certain
vehicles (and related purchases of fuel, repairs, etc.) are exclusively used for special purposes, such as:

+ exclusive use by/for sales agents;

* vehicles used for intervention; security and protection;

* courier services;

+ passenger transport services;

+ taxi; and

+ driving schools, ete.

VAT on payments on account of another person (that are later recovered from the other person) cannot be
deducted.

Romania requested a derogation from the VAT Directive to apply a special limitation. Starting with 1* of

the month following the one in which the derogation is approved, a 50% limitation of VAT deduction right
will be implemented for purchase, rental or lease of buildings/tenanted premises, regardless of their purpose,
located in residential areas or in blocks of flats and of VAT on expenditure relating to such buildings/tenanted
premises, where they are not used exclusively for business purposes.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT can be either carried forward to be offset against output VAT in future periods or claimed from
the Romanian state, if the VAT credit amount exceeds RON 5,000 (approximately EUR 1,000).

According to the Romanian VAT law, the deduction right arises at the moment when VAT becomes chargeable,
However, a taxable person can deduct the input VAT only if such purchases are used for certain operations (e.g.
taxable transactions, VAT exempt transactions with deduction right, transfer of going concem).

‘Taxable persons who apply the VAT cash-accounting system or purchase goods/services from companies that
apply this system, are entitled to deduct the input VAT when the value of the related invoice is actually paid to
the supplier.

The Romanian Fiscal Code allows taxable persons registered for VAT purposes in Romania to deduct the
input VAT through the VAT return within the statutory limitation period of 5 consecutive years (counting
starts with the 1* of January of the year following the one in which the tax point occurred).

Moreover, please note that in order to exercise the VAT deduction right after more than 3 consecutive
years, a notification should be submitted in this respect with the fiscal authorities through a registered letter,
accompanied by copies of the relevant invoices or other documents justifying the deduction right.

Although in principle the legal term to refund the VAT is 45 days from the submission of the VAT refund
claim, in practice this can take even up to one year. Furthermore, the specific legislation governing VAT
refunds also provides for siluations whereby the VAT audit period may be extended. Note that the 45 days
term does not include the periods when the VAT audit is suspended (e.g. in case of cross checks initiated by
the tax authorities at the level of the taxable person’s commercial partners).

518 | Romania

--- Page 19 ---
9.3. Adjustment periods

The Romanian Fiscal Code provides that under certain circumstances, a taxpayer may be required to adjust
the initial VAT deduction for the acquisition of capital goods. Adjustments may be required if the proportional
usage of the capital good for economic activities or for tax deductible transactions changes during the
applicable ‘VAT adjustment period’ by more than a negligible amount.

‘The applicable VAT adjustment periods generally begin on | January of the year in which the goods are
acquired, manufactured, received or first used, as applicable, and end after:

* 5 years for capital goods acquired or manufactured, other than the ones listed below; and

* 20 years for construction or acquisition of immovable assets and for the transformation or change of
immovable assets, if the value of each transformation or modernization is at least 20% of the total value
of the transformed or modernized assets.

The adjustment must be made each year under a pro rata apportionment of the total input VAT (i.¢., 1/5 or
1/20 of the VAT on capital goods and immovable property, respectively) to reflect a change in the proportion
of input VAT that is deductible under the allocation rules. Adjustments may result in either a VAT liability or
credit in the year for which it is calculated.

Special rules apply to certain goods that are delivered to another party or which cease to exist.

According to the Romanian Fiscal Code, taxpayers must retain a record of capital goods subject to adjustment
of the input VAT, which allows the control of the deductible tax and of the adjustments made. This record
must be kept for a period starting at the time when the tax related to the acquisition of the capital goods
becomes chargeable and ending five years after the expiry of the adjustment period. Other records, documents
and journals on capital goods should be kept for the same period.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

In order to refund VAT from Romania, a non-resident EU taxable person should submit the refund claim on
the electronic portal made available by the tax authorities of the EU Member State where it is established.
‘The claim can be submitted until 30 September of the calendar year following the period for which the claim
is made.

If the claim refers to a period of at least 3 months but not more than one calendar year, the minimum amount
claimed for refund should be the RON equivalent of EUR 400 (approximately RON 2,000). Separately, if the
claim refers to a calendar year or to the remainder of a calendar year, the minimum amount claimed for refund
should be the RON equivalent of EUR 50 (approximately RON 250). If the individual taxable amount of an
invoice exceeds EUR 1,000 or EUR 250 - in case of fuel - the applicant is required to provide by electronic
means additional information (i.e. copy of related invoices or import documents).

‘The time limit for the Romanian tax authorities to issue a decision with respect to the VAT refund claim, is
generally within 4 months after receipt of the claim. However, this period can be extended to 6 or 8 months
after receipt of the claim where additional information is required. If the refund claim is approved, the VAT
should be refunded within 10 working days from the expiry of the deadlines previously mentioned.

If the VAT refund claim is rejected, the non-resident has the possibility to file an appeal against the decision
issued by the Romanian tax authorities. According to the relevant national law, the appeal must be submitted
within 45 days since the fiscal administrative decision was communicated.

Furthermore, the VAT to be refunded will bear the special deduction limitations provided by the Romanian
VAT legislation (e.g. 50% in case of certain vehicles and related expenses such as fuel, repairs), as well as the
general deduction limitations (e.g. pro-rata in case of mixed taxable persons).

Romania | 519

--- Page 20 ---
95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

‘The annual/interim limits and minimum values for submitting the claims, the general eligibility conditions
and deductibility limitations, as well as the periods mentioned in paragraph 9.4 above are generally applicable
in this case as well.

Additionally, in order for a non-EU taxable person to obtain a refund of VAT from Romania, reciprocity is
required. Until now, Romania has concluded reciprocity treaties with the UK, Serbia, Norway, Turkey and
Switzerland.

Furthermore, non-EU claimanis must appoint a representative in Romania for the refund, The Romanian
representative is jointly and severally liable with the applicant for the rights and obligations arising from the
VAT refund claim.

The fiscal representative must fulfil on behalf of the non-EU taxable person the following requirements:

* to submit with the tax authorities the authorization whereby it is assigned the VAT statements;

+ to provide documents showing that the claimant is engaged in an economic activity which would confer
the quality of “taxable person from a VAT perspective” according to the Romanian VAT legislation;

+ to submit an affidavit stating that the claimant has not performed any supplies of goods or services
taking place in Romania during the VAT refund period, except for services such as transportation or other
supplies for which the customer is liable to pay VAT.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

Under the Romanian Fiscal Code, invoices should contain the following information:

+ asequential number, based on one or more series, which uniquely identifies the invoice;

+ the date of issuance of the invoice;

+ the date on which the goods/services were supplied, or the date when an advance payment was made if
such date is prior to the invoice’s issuance date;

* name, address and the VAT ID number of the supplier of goods/services;

* name and address of the supplier which is not established in Romania and has appointed a fiscal
representative in Romania, as well as the name, address and VAT identification number of the fiscal
representative;

+ name, address and VAT (identification) number of the customer;

+ name and address of the customer who is not established in Romania and has appointed a fiscal
representative in Romania, as well as the name, address and VAT ID number of the fiscal representative in
Romania;

+ description and quantity of the goods/services supplied;

+ the taxable amount for goods/services supplied or, as appropriate, the advance payments already credited,
for each VAT rate, exemption or non-taxable operation, the unit price, exclusive of VAT, as well as any
discounts not included in the unit price;

+ the applicable VAT rate and the amount of output VAT expressed in national currency, depending on each
VAT rate;

+ if the invoice is issued by the customer in the name and on bebalf of the supplier, the mention “self-
billing” (Romanian language: “autofactura”);

+ ifa VAT exemption is applicd, a reference to the relevant provision of the Romanian Fiscal Code or of the
EU VAT Directive or any other reference which shows that the supply of goods/services is subject to a
VAT exemption;

+ the indication for the application of a zero rate or reverse charge, For instance:

+ intra-Community supply:"livrare intracomunitara art.294 paragraph (2), letter a”
+ export supply:”export”
+ B2B service (reverse charge):"taxare inversa””);

+ if special regimes are applied, the invoice should include specific references, such as, margin scheme

travel agents (Romanian language: “regimul marjei ~ agentii de turism”), margin scheme ~ second —

520 | Romania

--- Page 21 ---
hand goods (Romanian language: “regimul marjei ~ bunuri second-hand”), margin scheme — works of

art (Romanian language: “regimul marjei-opera de arta”), margin scheme-collector’s items and antiques

(Romanian language: “regimul marjei-obiecte de colectie si antichitati”), indicating that one of the

respective regimes has been applied;

+ in case of intra-Community supplies of new means of transport, the invoice must include in addition to
the above the particularities based on which a mean of transport is deemed new, namely

+ for motorised land vehicles: the capacity/power, the date of the first entry into service and the number
of kilometres travelled - according to the Romanian VAT law a motorised land vehicle with a capacity
exceeding 48 cm3 or the power of which exceeds 7.2kW is deemed new if the supply takes place
within 6 months of the date of first entry into service or where the vehicle has travelled for no more
than 6,000 kilometres;

* for vessels: the length, the date of the first entry into service and the number of hours of sailing-
according to the Romanian VAT law a vessel exceeding 7.5 meters in length is deemed new if the
supply takes place within 3 months of the date of first entry into service or where the vessel has sailed
for no more than 100 hours;

* for aircraft: the take-off weight, the date of the first entry into service and the number of flight hours-
according to the Romanian VAT law an aircraft which has a take-off weight exceeding 1,550 kg is
deemed new if the supply takes place within 3 months of the date of first entry into service or where
the aircraft has flown for no more than 40 hours;

* where the VAT becomes chargeable at the time when the payment is received fully or partially, a reference
to application of the “cash-accounting system” (Romanian language: “TVA la incasare”), if the case; and

+ areference to other invoices or documents previously issued, when several invoices or documents are
issued for the same transaction.

10.1.1 Time limits

Invoices need to be issued at the latest by the 15% day of the month following the month in which the
chargeable event of the supply of goods/services occurred.

In the case of supplies other than intra-Community supplies, a taxable person must issue an invoice for the
amount of advances received in connection with a supply of goods or provision of services no later than the
15* day of the month following the month in the advance is received, unless the invoice has already been
issued.

10.1.2 Currency
According to the Romanian VAT law, values mentioned on an invoice can be expressed in any foreign
currency as long as the total VAT amount is expressed also in RON

10.1.3 Conversion and exchange rate

If the elements used to determine the taxable amount of a transaction, other than the import of goods, are
expressed in a foreign currency, there are 3 options to obtain the exchange rate:

+ the last exchange rate communicated by the National Bank of Romania;

+ the last exchange rate published by the Central European Bank; or

* the exchange rate used by the settlement bank.

The exchange rate used is valid on the date when the tax becomes chargeable. In case of operations subject to
the VAT cash accounting system, the date when the chargeability of tax for the operation in question would
have occurred had it not been subject to the VAT cash accounting system should be considered.

If the exchange rate published by the European Central Bank is used, the conversion between currencies,
other than EUR, shall be made by reference to the Euro exchange rate applicable for converting each
currency,

In contracts between parties, it should be noted which exchange rate will be used for settlement purposes
10.1.4 Self-billing

As a general rule, self-invoices need to be prepared by the customer of transactions subject to reverse charge
(except domestic reverse charge), in the absence of an invoice issued by their suppliers by the [5" day of the

Romania | 521

--- Page 22 ---
month following the month when the transaction took place. The above-mentioned rule applies for intra-
Community acquisitions as well. Additionally, a self-invoice needs to be issued for each self-supply of goods
services and for each transfer of own goods to another Member State (i.e. for assimilated intra-Community
supply of goods).

The customer may issue invoices in the name and on behalf of the supplier of goods/services. The supplier
and the customer must have concluded a prior written agreement regarding the self-billing procedure in order
to stipulate the terms and conditions of the invoicing procedure and the acceptance procedure for the self-
invoices issued by the customer on behalf of the supplier.

10.1.5 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ identity of the supplier of the goods and/or services;

+ type of goods or services supplied;

+ the VAT amount payable or the information needed to calculate it; and

+ specific, unambiguous reference to the initial invoice and the details that are being amended (on a credit
note, debit note or the other document treated as an invoice); and

+ the VAT identification number or tax identification code of the customer, if the customer is a taxable
person or a non-taxable legal person.

Itis possible to issue a simplified invoice if the ammount of the invoice is not higher than EUR 100 or the
invoice issued is a document or message which changes the initial invoice and a specific and unambiguous
reference to the initial invoice is made with a specific mention of the details that are amended.

10.1.6 Storage of invoices

Generally, invoices must be kept for 5 years starting from the date of closing of the financial year during
which the documents have been issued. However, invoices related to capital goods must be kept for 5 years
after expiry of the VAT adjustment period.

With respect to the medium of storage, invoices can be stored either on paper or electronically, irrespective of
the original form in which they were issued.

10.1.7 Electronic invoi
For B2G supplies electronic invoicing is mandatory with effect from 1 July 2022.

For B2C supplies, electronic invoicing is mandatory starting 1 January 2025, except for simplified invoices
(paragraph 10.1.5) which are not subject to electronic invoicing

For B2B supplies having the place of supply in Romania (except for exports and intra-community supplies)
electronic invoicing is mandatory with effect from 1 January 2024, This covers both taxable persons
established in Romania and non-residents having a VAT registration number in Romania.

Economic operators established in Romania are obliged to issue and communicate electronic invoices
exclusively through the RO e-Factura system within five calendar days since the invoice was issued, but
no later than five calendar days from the legal deadline to issue the traditional invoice based on the VAT
legislation, for B2B transactions performed toward economic operators established in Romania.

Only the invoice communicated via RO e-Factura system qualifies as invoice for VAT purposes

Fines are applicable for failing to comply with the RO e-Factura system and, depending on the obligation that
has been breached, the applicable fines are ranging from RON 1,000 (EUR 200) to RON 10,000 (EUR 2,000)
or 15% of the total invoice amount (e.g. if the supplier issues an electronic invoice and does not transmit it
through RO e-Factura system to the beneficiary).

The electronic invoice must be prepared in XML format according to specific technical requirements and

must be uploaded via the RO e-Factura system managed by the Ministry of Finance and available via the
virtual private space. After upload, the XML is subject to a technical validation. If validation is successful, the

522 | Romania

--- Page 23 ---
Ministry of Finances applies an electronic seal on the e-invoice and makes it available via the virtual private
space to both the supplier and the customer, The electronic signature of the Ministry of Finances cerlifies that
the e-invoice has been received in the RO e-Factura system

10.2 Compliance

VAT return period
Asa general rule, the fiscal period is the calendar month.

For taxable persons with an annual tumover below the threshold of EUR 100,000 (or equivalent) and not
having caried out any intra-Community acquisitions of goods, the fiscal period is the calendar quarter.

The fiscal period changes to the calendar month as soon as the above-mentioned threshold is exceeded or
when an intra~-Community acquisition is made. Moreover, the tax authorities may approve, at the taxpayer’s
justified request, a fiscal period different than the month/quarter such as bi-annual or annual.

There are no differences for foreign taxable persons regarding the VAT return period.

Filing of VAT return and payment
VAT returns need to be submitted and VAT liabilities (if any) need to be paid by the 25“ of the month
following the fiscal period when the VAT becomes chargeable. Failure to comply with this obligation, is
subject to late payment interest and fines.

Separately, note that Romania has also implemented the VAT cash-accounting system. It applies to Romanian
taxable persons whose annual turnover does not exceed the threshold of RON 4,500,000 (approximately EUR
905,600). The application of the VAT cash-accounting system is optional. The VAT cash-accounting system is
characterized by the fact that the VAT chargeability occurs when an invoice is fully/partially paid. Hence, the
payment of the VAT to the tax authorities should be done by the 25" of the month following the fiscal period
when the VAT becomes chargeable under the cash-accounting system.

Please find below the link to the official webpage to the National Authority for Fiscal Administration related

to the filing of the VAT return:

* VAT return and related filing instructions: hitps://tinyurl.com/nrxSzayx

* Pdf format of the VAT return to be filed and submitted with the Romanian tax authorities:
Attps:/Ainyurl.com/bdzadhkb

Starting January 2018, the electronic filing of the VAT return is mandatory for all the categories of taxable
persons.

European sales listing
Both intra-Community sales and intra-Community acquisitions of goods and services ate mentioned in the
European sales listing. Such statement should be submitted by the 25" of each month following the one in
which the intra~Community transactions occur.

There is no threshold provided by the Romanian VAT legislation with respect to European sales/acquisitions
to be reported in the European sales listing.

Please find below the link to the official webpage of the National Authority for Fiscal Administration related
to the filing of the European sales listing: https.//iinyurl.com/2wr8ujav.

National informative statement
The informative statement regarding taxable supplies/acquisitions performed having the place of supply in
Romania (ic. ‘394 Statement’) should be submitted for the same fiscal period as the VAT retum (monthly,
quarterly or bi-annually/annually in certain exceptional circumstances).

‘National informative statement template can be downloaded at the following link: https://tinyurl.com/2vkk42br

Romania | 523

--- Page 24 ---
Tax assessment period

‘The right of the Romanian tax authorities to determine fiscal obligations is subject to a statute of limitation of
5 years, starting from the 1* of July of the year following the year when the fiscal debt arose. As an exception,
the statute of limitation is extended to 10 years in cases of fraud or other criminal offences.

Due to COVID freezing of terms by approx. 9 months, the first open year is 2018 (for which the 5 years term
ends in April 2025).

10.3 Penalties

Default penalties for late registration

The tax authorities will issue a penalty ranging between RON 1,000 (approximately EUR 200) and RON
5,000 (approximately EUR 1,000) or between RON 500 (approximately EUR 100) and RON 1,000
(approximately EUR 200) depending on the taxpayer category.

Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time
Failure to submit the VAT retum or the European sales listing within the legal deadline is subject to a fine
ranging between RON 1,000 (approximately EUR 200) and RON 5,000 (approximately EUR 1,000).

Failure to file a correct and complete European sales listing within the legal deadline is sanctioned with a fine
ranging between RON 500 (approximately EUR 100) and RON 1,500 (approximately EUR 300).

No fine should be charged in case the taxable person submits a corrected European sales listing before the
legal deadline for submitting the next listing has passed and it was not noticed by the tax authorities before
the correction was performed.

Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the VAT authorities
Failure to pay VAT within the legal deadline leads to paying late payment interest, currently at 0.02% per day
of delay and late payment penalties, currently at 0.01% per each day of delay.

Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Generally, errors occurring in VAT returns are corrected in the designated rows of the next VAT return.
Payment of differences in VAT Liabilities may, however, be done sooner in order to limit the period of
assessment of related Jate payment interest and penalties.

In certain limited circumstances, taxable persons can voluntarily correct information in already filed VAT
returns via a special procedure for correction of material errors coordinated with the tax authorities. However,
even if VAT is voluntarily corrected or assessed by the tax authorities, corresponding interest and late
payment penalties are still due.

10.4 Intrastat

Taxable persons registered for VAT purposes in Romania and trading goods with other EU Member

States which exceed any of the annual Intrastat arrival/dispatches thresholds, are liable to submit Intrastat
declarations. Please note that there are separate Intrastat declarations for arrivals and dispatches. The Intrastat
declarations should be submitted by the 15" of the month following the reporting period.

Intrastat threshold

Under the Romanian Jaw, Intrastat declarations must be filed on a monthly basis starting from the first month
when the annual threshold for intra-Community acquisitions (i.e. RON 1,000,000, approximately EUR
200,000 + hreshold valid for 2025) or for the intra-Community supplies (i.e. RON 1,000,000, approximately
EUR 200,000 -va lid for 2025) are exceeded. Please note that distance sales or supply of goods with
installation should also be reported in the [ntrastat declaration. Each year the Intrastat thresholds are
confirmed by means of an official Order.

Entering goods from distance sales in the Member State of destination should be reported in the Intrastat
system if the Intrastat threshold established by national law has been exceeded in the Member State of
destination. The declaration procedure is as follows:

524 | Romania

--- Page 25 ---
+ the dispatches will be reported by the supplier of the goods;

* the arrivals, when the Intrastat threshold is exceeded, will be reported by the person responsible for the
payment of the supplier’s tax liabilities (tax representative) in the Member State of destination of the
goods.

Acquisitions by individual Romanian individuals in stores located in other Member States, as well as

acquisitions made by individuals in other Member States in stores in Romania will not be reported in the

Intrastat declaration.

Intrastat data to be reported
‘The information which needs to be reported in the Intrastat statements for dispatches from/to Romania is the
following:

+ date of departure/arrival;

«VAT ID of the customer

+ CN 8-digit code of the goods;

+ country of destination/origin;

* country of dispatch;

+ quantity(kilos) and the additional units;

* invoice value and statistic value (denominated in RON);

+ delivery terms (as per invoice);

* nature of transaction; and

* type of transport.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

Virtual Private Space
From 1 March 2022, Romanian taxpayers (legal entities, individuals and other entities without legal
personality) will be required to follow a specific procedure as to register within the Romanian Virtual Private
Space, due to the fact that the communication with the Romanian tax authorities will be performed only by
electronic means via VPS as of this date.

The Virtual Private Space is a system designed to facilitate the online communication of the Romanian
‘Tax Authorities with the taxpayers ~ in brief, the Virtual Private Space will grant taxpayers easier access
to their fiscal file and allow them to view/download electronically documents such as the taxpayer sheet,
tax residency certificate, any fiscal-administrative documents issued by the Romanian Tax Authorities (i.¢.,
decisions imposing tax liabilities etc.), as well as to submit any claims/applications/letters or any other
documents.

The Virtual Private Space is also used by taxpayers for the purpose of fulfilling their reporting obligations with
respect to E-Invoicing and E-Transport as the related systems can be accessed by the taxpayer through its VPS.

SAF-T
‘The Standard Audit File for Tax (SAF-T) represents an international standard used for the electronic transfer
data from accounting and fiscal records, from taxpayers/payers to fiscal authorities and auditors,

It is submitted by taxpayers/payers through the Informative Statement (D406) via the digital public service
“Online services — Submission of statements” or through website www.e-guvernare.ro. The model of
Informative Statement requires the preparation of certain accounting ledgers (i.e. balance sheet, trade balance
etc.) and reporting mandatory accounting information (i.e, stock related information, fixed assets ete.). D406
being a fully electronic statement, it must be signed using a qualified certificate issued by a certification
service provider.

According with Order of the President of ANAF no. 1783/2021, the start date of reporting the Informative

Statement (D406) is different, depending on the type of taxpayer (i.e. large, medium or small)

+ | January 2022, for large taxpayers classified in this category on January 2022 and who were also part of
this category in 2021;

+ 1 July 2022, for lange taxpayers classified in this category on January 2022 and who were not part of this
category in 2021;

Romania | 525

--- Page 26 ---
+ 1 January 2023, for medium taxpayers classified in this category as of 31 December 2021;

+ 1 January 2025, for small taxpayers (including non-residents registered for VAT purposes under the direct
procedure, and non-residents which are fiscally represented by large or medium taxpayers) classified in
this category as of 31 December 2021.

‘Taxpayers must submit the Informative Statement (D406) monthly or quarterly, as follows the applicable lax

period for VAT. The penalties applied for incorrect/incomplete submission or failure to submit on time are:

+ Between RON 1,000 (approximately EUR 200) and RON 5,000 (approximately EUR 1,000) for failure to
submit on time;

+ Between RON 500 (approximately EUR 100) and RON 1,500 (approximately EUR 300) for incorrect/
incomplete submission.

£-Transport

Under Government Emergency Ordinance 41/2022, starting with 1 July 2022, taxpayers shall declare certain

transports of high tax risk goods on Romanian territory:

+ vegetables, plants, roots and tubers, food (NC codes 0701 to 0714);

+ edible fruits, peels of citrus fruits or melons (NC codes 0801 to 0814);

+ drinks, alcoholic liquids and vinegar (NC codes 2201 to 2208);

+ salt, sulphur, soils and stones; plaster, lime and cement (NC codes 2505 and 2517);

+ clothes and clothing accessories, knitted or crocheted (NC codes 6101 to 6117);

+ clothing and clothing accessories, other than knitted or crocheted (NC codes 6201 to 6212 and NC codes.
6214 to 6217);

+ footwear, boots and similar articles; parts of these articles (NC codes 6401 to 6405); and

cast iron, iron and steel (NC codes 7213 and 7214).

Subsequently, the following criteria must be taken into account regarding the obligation to declare the
transport in the national system:

+ category of road vehicle -ve_hicles which have a maximum authorized weight of at least 2.5 tons;
+ the weight of goods -w ith a total gross mass greater than 500 Kg; and

+ value of good -¥ alue greater than RON 10,000 (approximately EUR 2,000).

If any of the above criteria is fulfilled within a transport, then the transport shall be declared via the RO
e-Transport system.

Starting from 1 January 2024, the Emergency Ordinance no. 115/2023 introduced a new obligation to report

all international transports of goods fulfilling the conditions related to road vehicle category, goods weight

and value. The obligation to declare in the Ro E-Transport System the data related to the international road
transport of goods is for the following users:

+ the consignee entered in the customs import declaration, respectively the consignor entered in the customs
export declaration, in the case of goods subject to import or export operations, as the case may be;

+ the beneficiary in Romania, in the case of intra-Community acquisitions of goods;

+ the supplier in Romania, in the case of intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ the warehouse keeper, in the case of goods subject to intra-Community transactions in transit, both for
goods unloaded on the territory of Romania for storage or for the formation ofa new consignment of
one or more consignments of goods, and for goods loaded after storage or after the formation of a new
consignment on the national territory of one or more consignments of goods;

+ the service provider in Romania, in the case of commercial operations representing a non-transfer both
for goods unloaded on the territory of Romania for the provision of services and for the resulting goods
re-dispatched to the trading partner’s State;

+ the beneficiary in Romania, in the case of commercial operations constituting a non-transfer both for
goods dispatched from Romania for the provision of services in a Member State of the European Union
and for the resulting goods re-dispatched to Romania;

+ the customer in Romania, in the case of commercial transactions that are subject to the call-olf stock
regime if Romania is the Member State to which the goods have been transported both for goods
unloaded in Romania and for goods delivered at a later stage after arrival to another taxable person in
Romania or where the goods are returned to the Member State from which they were initially transported;

--- Page 27 ---
+ the supplier in Romania, in the case of commercial transactions which are subject to the call-off stock
regime where Romania is the Member State from which the goods are transported both for goods
dispatched from Romania and where those goods are returned to Romania,

The e-Transport system will contain information related to: the consignor and the beneficiary, the name,
characteristics, quantities and value of goods transported, places of loading and unloading, details on the
means of transport used, as well as a specific code generated by the e-Transport system (i.¢., UIT code) which
should be further on included in the transport documents for the high tax risk goods (the UIT code should be
made available to the transport operator ahead of actually performing the transport on Romanian territory).

In order to declare the aforementioned types of goods via e-Transport and to obtain the UIT code, the
taxpayer should register with the system within the virtual private space.

Legal entities not declaring the transport information or declaring other quantities than those covered by
the transport of goods in the e-Transport system may be sanctioned with a fine ranging from RON 20,000
(approximately EUR 4,000) to RON 100,000 (approximately FUR 20,000) as well as seizure of goods not
declared,

E-Sigiliu
The National System Ro e-Sigiliu was established to ensure compliance with the traceability of road transport
of goods with high customs/fiscal risk on the territory of Romania. The e-Sigiliu is an intelligent seal which
registers and transmits status and position information to allow the competent authorities to monitor road.
transports of goods with high customs/fiscal risk.

The use of the National System Ro e-Sigiliu will be based on a risk analysis performed by the competent
authorities, who will decide whether the use of the intelligent seal is required, on a case by case basis.

The driver’s non-compliance with the obligation to ensure the integrity of the intelligent seal, constitutes a
contravention, if it has not been committed in such conditions that it is considered, according to the criminal
law, an infraction and is punishable by a fine from RON 20,000 to RON 50,000.

E-VAT

In June 2024, the national RO e-VAT system and the pre-filled RO e-VAT return were implemented by

Emergency Ordinance no. 70/2024 and Emergency Ordinance no. 87/2024. The recently introduced

compliance obligation comprises the following points:

+ the pre-filled VAT return will include data declared by taxpayers in various systems organized by the
Ministry of Finance and ANAF, such as RO e-Invoice, RO e-Transport, RO e-S' igiliu, RO e-SAF-T, RO
e-Electronic cash registers, the integrated customs system and other systems of the Ministry of Finance.

+ the date of implementation for the pre-filled RO e-VAT retum is | August 2024 (which will include the
operations carried out starting 1 July 2024);

* the pre-filled VAT return will be sent by the tax authorities to taxpayers via the Virtual Private Space by
the 5th of the month following the legal deadline for submitting the VAT retum (e.g. by 5 September with
respect to July reporting period as the submission deadline of the related VAT retum is 25 August);

* _ significant differences between the pre-filled VAT return and the VAT return submitted by taxpayers,
defined as being of at least 20% and RON 5,000 (cumulatively), will be notified by Romanian tax
authorities through Virtual Private Space. The Romanian tax authorities can also notify a taxpayer based
on a risk analysis if the differences identified are not significant:

+ the taxpayers are obliged to reply within 20 days from the date of receipt of the notification of
compliance, to justify the significant differences between the pre-completed VAT return and the VAT
return submitted by them;

+ the compliance notification shall not be considered in determining the tax risk indicators until 1 July
2025. Additionally, up until the previously mentioned date, there are no applicable fines (i.e. ranging
between RON 1,000 and RON 10,000, namely approx. EUR 200 and EUR 2,000) for the non-submission
by the taxpayer of the response to the Notification of compliance RO e-VAT.
