Slovakia

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive has been implemented in Slovakia through the “Zakon &. 222/2004
zo 6, aprila 2004 0 dani z pridanej hodnoty v zneni neskorsich predpisov” (hereafter referred to as the “VAT
Law”). VAT is referred to in Slovakia as “daii z pridanej hodnoty” or “DPH.”

The Slovak Tax Directory publishes explanatory guidelines on the tax administration’s website (in Slovak
only). However, Slovak courts have ruled that these guidelines are not legally binding if they conflict with
EU legislation and the established case law of the Court of Justice of the EU (CJEU). At times, the guidelines
may not reflect the latest developments in case law.

‘The complete list of guidelines can be found using the following links:

News from the legislation: hitps://tinyurl.com/s@ dB

Methodological instructions: htips://tinyurl.com/® pfitx

Methodological guidelines: Atips://tinyurl.com/B —B

Minutes from the meetings of methodological committees of the Ministry of Finance: htfps://tinyurl.com/Syrwdawd
Case law: htips://tinyurl.com/spj b de

Significant penalties may be imposed for the late submission of VAT returns, underreporting VAT liabilities,
etc, The tax authorities take a very strict and formalistic approach, especially as Slovakia does not require
annual VAT returns. This makes it essential to follow the rules regarding tax liability and the correct
periods for credit and debit notes very strictly. Correcting an error generally leads to additional VAT return
submissions, which automatically results in fines if the VAT liability increases.

VAT registration procedures are not uniform across the EU, and Slovak VAT law also addresses delayed VAT
registration cases. From | January 2025, the concept of delayed VAT registration has changed, where both
established and non-established taxable persons become VAT payers retroactively. This has a significant
positive impact on the delayed declaration of the acquisition of goods from another EU member state and a
negative impact on the delayed declaration of the local supply of goods and services, where the VAT liability
is not shifted to the purchaser.

Current VAT audits may not heavily emphasize abusive practices in a comparison with the past, but it remains
crucial to consider the economic substance of transactions.

Even diligent entrepreneurs may inadvertently become part of transactions affected by VAT fraud committed
by another party. It is essential for bonafide businesses to document actions in good faith and take reasonable
steps to avoid participating in transactions flagged for VAT fraud. Implementing mechanisms to identify risk
indicators and verify contractual partners’ transparency is recommended for prudent entrepreneurs. So far, the
tax authorities have not published a list of risk indicators in this respect. During a VAT audit, taxable persons
must substantiate their claims with transparent and consistent evidence, as established by Slovak court
precedents,

Engaging a VAT advisor at the beginning of a VAT audit is highly advisable, as this proactive approach can
help effectively navigating the complexities involved.

VAT returns and other statements must be submitted electronically either by taxpayers or by a tax
representative (domestic or foreign).

Slovakia | 529

--- Page 30 ---
1. Taxable persons

Lt Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

A taxable person who has a registered office, place of business or fixed establishment in Slovakia, or a

residence in Slovakia, or usually stays in Slovakia, becomes a VAT payer:

+ on the first day of the calendar year following the calendar year in which the vaiue of goods or services
supplied by this person, excluding VAT, which are included in the turnover, exceeded EUR 50,000, unless
provided otherwise.

An important change is that the turnover is calculated for the calendar year period and not for the twelve-
month period. The financial administration plans to combat the avoidance of mandatory VAT registration
in situations where, before exceeding the turnover for mandatory VAT registration, business activity is
transferred to a new legal entity. Taxable persons who exclusively supply exempt insurance and financial
services, exempt rental of real estate, and exempt supply of real estate, unless it is occasionally supplied
real estate, may also have to VAT register;

+ _ by supplying goods or services, where the value of goods or services supplied by this person, excluding
VAT, included in the turnover, in the current calendar year exceeds EUR 62,500,

It means that within a single day, a taxable person can carry oul taxable transactions in the position of
anon-VAT payer and at the same time, by exceeding the turnover on that same day, and become a VAT
payer;

* on the day on which this person becomes the legal successor of a VAT payer who ceased to exist without
liquidation;

+ on the day of acquisition of the enterprise or its part, if this person acquires tangible and intangible assets
in Slovakia within the acquired enterprise of the VAT payer or part of the VAT payer’s enterprise that
forms a separate organizational unit,

In practice, there remains an issue with how to interpret the requirement for “a separate organizational
unit” in light of the established case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union;

* on the effective date of transformation in the case of a spin-off or on the effective date of transformation
in the case of a spin-off according to the law valid in another EU member state or a contracting state of
the European Economic Area Agreement or on the effective date of transformation in the case of a cross-
border spin-off, if tangible or intangible assets of the VAT payer that were split by spin-off or cross-border
spin-off, are transferred to this person in Slovakia;

+ on the day on which this person supplies a building, part of a building or construction land, if the tumover
EUR 62,500 is to be exceeded;

+ on the day on which this person receives payment before supplying goods according to letter 1)

This also includes VAT registration in cases where the advance payment itself is lower than the required
turnover but is related to the delivery of real estate, the price of which is higher than the required
turnover;

+ onthe day of delivery of goods with a place of delivery in Slovakia, if it is a taxable person who
facilitates the supply of goods within the territory of the European Union made by a taxable person not
established within the territory of the European Union to a person other than a taxable person through the
use of an electronic interface, such as an electronic marketplace, electronic platform, electronic portal, or
similar electronic means; or

* by supplying goods or services, or acquiring goods in Slovakia from another EU member state, which are
subject to VAT, carried out by a taxable person who has exclusively a fixed establishment Slovakia, unless
it is a small business of a foreign person applying for VAT exemption.

In general, a taxable person is required to submit a VAT registration application to the tax office within five
working days from the day on which the decisive facts occurred

A taxable person who has a registered office or place of business in Slovakia, or if they do not have such a
place but have a residence or usually stay in Slovakia, can also voluntarily apply for a VAT registration.

According to the wording of the VAT Law, voluntary VAT registration is not possible for non-established
taxable persons or for fixed establishments of foreign taxable persons. However, since this could be

530 | Slovakia

--- Page 31 ---
considered unequal treatment compared to other types of taxable persons, it is expected that the next VAT
amendment will allow voluntary VAT registration for such taxable persons as well.

In Slovakia, issues often arise when individuals or municipalities sell construction land as taxable persons.
Despite CJEU case law, the Slovak tax administration takes a conservative approach to this matter.

For VAT purposes, a fixed establishment refers to a permanent place of business equipped with the personnel
and material resources necessary to conduct business. In some specific cases, VAT registering the fixed
establishment of a foreign taxable person for VAT purposes in Slovakia can be challenging. The most
common basis for rejecting such a registration request is the perceived lack of sufficient permanence. Tax
authority employees may physically inspect the fixed establishment’s premises to confirm whether continuous
interaction between human and material resources exists. The criteria for consideration can also include
whether the employees of the fixed establishment are involved in concluding contracts with third parties,

or whether fundamental decisions are made in the fixed establishment. However, it should be noted that in
practice, individual tax authorities may take different positions. The primary reason for VAT registering a
fixed establishment in Slovakia is that it reclassifies a foreign person's status from a non-established to an
established taxable person. While a non-established taxable person can only deduct VAT through a VAT return
under certain conditions, Slovakia’s preference is for VAT refunds. Conversely, an established taxable person
is permitted to deduct VAT only through a VAT return.

Upon becoming a VAT registered person, businesses must communicate the details of a bank account (foreign
or local) used in connection with their economic activity in Slovakia, to which tax authorities may transfer
any VAT refunds. Reporting a bank account is important also with regard to the joint responsibility of the
buyer for the VAT unpaid by the supplier if the buyer paid to the supplier’s bank account, which is not listed
in the list of bank accounts published by the tax administration.

1.2 VAT registration and SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

There is no registration threshold for foreign taxable persons who need to register for VAT purposes in
Slovakia.

A taxable person who does not have a registered office, place of business, fixed establishment, residence, or

usualy does nt stay in Slovakia, becomes a VAT payer:
by supplying goods or services that are subject to VAT, except for exceptions;

* by receiving payment before supplying goods or services as above; or

+ by acquiring goods in Slovakia from another EU Member State that are subject to VAT, unless it is a small
business of a foreign person applying for VAT exemption, and unless it is the acquisition of goods in
Slovakia from another EU Member State, which is considered taxed according to the rules of triangular
trade.

For the purpose of applying the special VAT exemption regime for small foreign businesses:
+ asmall foreign business is a taxable person who has a registered office, place of business, residence or
usually stays in another BU member state if
in the current calendar year, the value of goods and services supplied by this person, excluding VAT,
included in the annual turnover in Slovakia, does not exceed EUR 62,500, and at the same time in
the previous calendar year, the value of goods and services supplied by this person, excluding VAT,
included in the annual turnover in Slovakia, did not exceed EUR 50,000; and
+ in the current calendar year, the value of goods and services supplied by this person, excluding VAT,
included in the annual turnover in the EU, does not exceed EUR 100,000, and at the same time in
the previous calendar year, the value of goods and services supplied by this person, excluding VAT,
included in the annual turnover in the EU, did not exceed EUR 100,000;
+ the Member State of establishment is another EU Member State in which the small foreign business has a
registered office, place of business, residence, or usually stays.

There are a few exceptions to this obligation, such as imports, local reverse charges, exempt transport
services, etc.

Slovakia | 531

--- Page 32 ---
A separate VIES registration is not required.

For VAT registration in Slovakia, foreign applicants must provide:

+ an original or notarized extract from the applicant’s commercial register, with an official Slovak
translation certified by a Slovak-certified translator; and

+ the completed VAT registration application in Slovak, signed by an authorized representative.

A foreign person is required to submit a VAT registration application to the Tax Office Bratislava within five
working days from the day they became a VAT payer. The Tax Office Bratislava will register the VAT payer,
assign him a VAT identification number, and issue a VAT registration decision no later than ten days from the
date of receipt of the VAT registration application. The VAT identification number becomes valid on the day
the foreign person became a VAT payer.

In Slovakia, special rules for VAT deduction through a VAT return apply to non-established taxable persons
registered for VAT purposes. VAT registration does not automatically allow for VAT deduction through a VAT
return, Non-established taxable persons may only deduct VAT through a VAT return if they:

+ apply Slovak VAT on output supplies;

+ deliver goods from Slovakia to another EU Member State (intra~-Community supply); or

* — export goods from Slovakia to outside the EU.

If none of these conditions are met, the non-established taxable person may not deduct VAT through a VAT
return but can only request a VAT refund. Conversely, they can claim VAT deduction through a VAT return
when acquiring goods in Slovakia from another EU Member State or applying domestic reverse charge
mechanism.

In some cases, non-cstablished taxable persons may seck VAT deduction through a VAT return, apply the
reverse charge mechanism, and simultaneously request a VAT refund,

13 VAT grouping

‘Two or more taxable persons residing or having a fixed establishment in Slovakia and closely bound by
financial, economic, and organizational links may choose to be treated as a single taxable person for VAT
purposes.

VAT grouping is optional in Slovakia, applying only if the entities involved request it.
There are various motives for establishing a VAT group, though it does not always result in positive cash flow.

Financially connected taxable persons arc those where one entity controls others. Economic connections exist
when principal activities are mutually dependent, share a common economic objective, or when one entity
operates to benefit other members. Organizational connections occur when there is common management or
control through shared participation.

The VAT group registration application must include details of the member appointed as the “group
representative” for VAT issues, along with the same information required for domestic taxable persons (see
Section 1.1). This includes the general application form for established entities and details on the number of
group members.

VAT Attachment No, 2 applies to group member registration or any change in group registration. The
following information is required:

+ names of local tax authorities for each group member;

+ VAT ID of each group member (if assigned in Slovakia);

* business name and address of each group member’s seat, business location, or fixed establishment; and
+ _ signature of the statutory representative(s) of each group member.

Only taxable persons can form a VAT group; holding companies with no economic activities for VAT

532 | Slovakia

--- Page 33 ---
purposes are excluded. Non-established taxable persons cannot be in the VAT group unless they have a fixed
establishment in Slovakia.

VAT group members share joint and several liability for the group’s VAT obligations, which remains in force
after group dissolution or a member's withdrawal.

Members of a VAT group operate under a single VAT number. A group representative files VAT reports on
behalf of the group, while individual members cannot file separate VAT returns or other reports, such as
European sales listings.

1.4 Fiscal representative

Slovakia has not implemented a typical concept of fiscal representation.

A foreign taxable person, whether from an EU or non-EU country, does not need to appoint a fiscal
representative to register for VAT purposes in Slovakia. Foreign taxable persons can register directly for VAT.
However, it is possible to appoint a tax representative in the following situations:
+ importation of goods followed by an intra~Community supply:
A non-resident taxable person who is not registered for VAT in Slovakia may appoint a tax representative for
importing goods into Slovakia if the goods are dispatched or transported from a non-EU country and their
transport ends in an EU Member State other than Slovakia. In this case, the importation must be followed
by an intra-Community supply. The Slovak tax representative will report the subsequent intra-Community
supply in their VAT return, avoiding the foreign taxable person’s obligation to register for VAT in Slovakia.
The tax representative must be a company or individual with a seat or residence in Slovakia.
Customs authorities may require the importer to provide a guarantee equivalent to the potential import
VAT before the goods are released for free circulation in Slovakia; and
+ intra-Community acquisition followed by:
+ an intra-Community supply of goods;
+ anexport of goods from the EU; or
+ distance sales within the EU.

Anon-resident taxable person who is not registered for VAT in Slovakia may appoint a tax representative for
an intra-Community acquisition if this acquisition is followed by an intra~Community supply of goods, an
export of goods from the EU, or distance sales within the EU (e.g. via an electronic platform).

‘The tax representative is jointly and severally liable for the VAT liabilities of the non-resident taxable person.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Slovakia allows for the VAT exemption on the transfer of a going concern, following Articles 19 and 29 of the
VAT Directive. However, it is not always clear how to interpret the requirement of “a separate organizational
unit” defined by the VAT Law in light of the established case law of the CJEU.

The no-supply rule does not apply if the transferee’s business activities are exempt from VAT unless the
transferor’s activities are also exempt.

The transfer of goods and services is not considered the transfer of tangible or intangible assets to the
acquiring company if the transfer occurs through a division or cross-border separation and the acquiring
company is, or becomes, a taxpayer. In such cases, the acquiring company is regarded as the legal successor
to the taxpayer, extending to the assets transferred.

While a business transfer meeting these conditions is not subject to VAT, there are implications. The entity
acquiring the business assumes all VAT-related rights, obligations, claims, liabilities, and responsibilities,
including any remaining adjustment periods for (im)movable tangible property.

Slovakia | 533

--- Page 34 ---
22 Supplies free of charge

When a taxpayer supplies goods for personal use, provides goods for his employees’ personal consumption,
supplies goods free of charge, or supplies goods for non-business purposes while deducting VAT on the
acquisition or production of those goods, such supplies are treated as supplies for consideration. However,
free supplies of goods for business purposes valued at no more than EUR 17 (excluding VAT) per article and
free business samples are not considered supplies for consideration,

Likewise, services provided free of charge for the personal consumption of the taxpayer, their employees,

or for purposes other than business are considered supplies for consideration, except when the services are
provided to a delegating institution or a recipient of volunteer activity under a separate act (Act No. 406/2011
Coll. on Volunteering as amended).

Slovakia has not established a specific VAT regime for “triggered investments.” For instance, when an
investor donates a children’s playground to a municipality as a condition for obtaining a building permit, it
is highly likely that VAT inspectors will assess such a transaction conservatively. If the developer knew in
advance that they planned to donate the children’s playground free of charge, the tax administrator will not
recognize the VAT deduction related to the construction of the children’s playground, and subsequently, the
developer does not charge VAT on the output when donating the children’s playground. Only if the developer
did not know in advance about the plan to donate the children’s playground free of charge, can they initially
deduct the VAT and then apply VAT on the output. In the past, Slovak courts have ruled that in the case of
applying a symbolic price, it is essentially a free supply.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

Goods imported from a non-EU country with transport ending in another EU Member State are exempt from

VAT in Slovakia if the import is followed by an intra-Community supply exempt from VAT. To qualify for

this exemption, the importer registered for VAT in Stovakia (or their representative) must provide the Slovak

Customs authority with:

* his Slovak VAT identification number or the VAT 1D number of his tax representative for the import
exemption;

* the customer’s VAT ID number from another EU Member State or the VAT ID of the EU Member State
where the goods will be delivered; and

+ proof of dispatch or transport from Slovakia to another EU Member State, such as a contract of carriage
ora transport document.

Customs authorities may require a security deposit (cash or bank guarantee) equal to the import VAT. In
practice, this guarantee is frequently required.

It should be noted that it is very questionable if the entire transportation of goods from a place outside the EU
to the place of delivery in the other EU member state is arranged by a supplier from a third country who is
not the importer of the goods to Slovakia, whether in such a case the importer of the goods meets the defining
condition for intra-Community supply of goods, which is the arrangement of the transportation of goods from
Slovakia to another EU member state on behalf of the importer of the goods.

Non-established entities have two options for applying the import VAT exemption in Slovakia: VAT
registration of the importer or the appointment of a tax representative to manage the exemption.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation
Slovakia is introducing postponed accounting for imports for selected VAT payers as following:
+ VAT payer, established in Slovakia and on whose behalf a customs declaration is filed, who has at the
time of the tax liability arising upon import of goods to Slovakia (effective from 1 July 2025):
+ assigned VAT identification number (taxable person established in Slovakia or a VAT group); and
+ effective authorization granting the status of an approved economic operator:
+ VAT payer, on whose behalf a customs declaration is filed within the framework of centralized customs

534 | Slovakia

--- Page 35 ---
clearance, who has at the time of the tax liability arising upon import of goods to Slovakia (effective from
January 1 2026);

+ assigned VAT identification number (taxable person established or not established in Slovakia or a VAT
group); and

+ effective authorization granting the status of an approved economic operator.

Despite the fact that most importers will not meet the required conditions, the importers who do meet them
ensure the vast majority of imports to Slovakia.

Importers of goods who do not meet the required conditions will, as before, be required to pay import VAT to
the customs authorities.

In Slovakia, the condition for deducting import VAT is interpreted strictly based on ownership of the goods
during importation.

3. Place of Supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

When a VAT-registered taxable person in one EU Member State transfers goods to Slovakia to create a stock
from which their customer can “call-of” (withdraw and pay for) the goods as needed, this arrangement is
known as call-off stock. Under certain conditions, this arrangement allows the supplier to treat the transaction
as an intra~Community supply to their customer, avoiding the need for a local supply registration.

Slovak VAT legislation aligns with the EU VAT Directive articles and the 2020 Quick Fixes Explanatory
Notes. However, the status of a warehouse as a fixed establishment is less commonly assumed than suggested
in the Explanatory Notes.

3.2 Installation

supplies

The place of supply for goods associated with installation or assembly by the supplier, is the location where
the goods are installed or assembled.

In Slovakia, there is no formal definition for “supply with installation or assembly.” Construction work

is generally considered a supply of services related to immovable property, except when it creates a new
building, in which case it is regarded as a supply of goods. Both types of supplies may qualify for reverse
charge VAT treatment when applicable conditions are met.

3.3 Distance sales threshold

AUR 10,000 threshold applies to all EU-wide sales of TBE (telecommunications, broadcasting, and
electronic) services and distance sales of goods. Once this threshold is exceeded, suppliers can either register
directly in Slovakia or in their home country via the One Stop Shop (OSS) to account for Slovak VAT on
distance sales to Slovakia.

A taxable person from another EU Member State may also opt for voluntary VAT registration in Slovakia
before exceeding the threshold. This option remains valid for two calendar years following the election date.
For the special scheme on distance sales of imported goods, a threshold of EUR 150 per consignment applies.

34 Simplified triangulation rules

Under Slovak VAT law, simplified triangulation rules apply when:

+ there are three parties involved (A, B, and C), and goods move directly from the initial supplier (A) to the
final customer (C) across EU Member States;

* each party in the transaction is VAT-registered in a different EU Member State;

Slovakia | 535

--- Page 36 ---
+ the intermediary (B) is not established in the EU Member State of the final customer (C) and uses the
same VAT identification number in transactions with both the supplier (A) and the customer (C);

* goods are dispatched or transported by either the supplier (A), the intermediary (B), or a third party on
their behalf, from an EU Member State other than that of the intermediary's VAT ID;

+ the final customer (C) uses a VAT ID from the EU Member State where the transport of the goods ends; and

+ the final customer (C) is liable to pay VAT on the local supply of goods by (B) to (C).

If B is established or VAT-registered in the dispatch of the goods Member State (Member State 1) and another
EU Member State (Member State 2), B may apply simplification provided all conditions are met and transport
is assigned to the first supply (A-B)..

The term “dispatch or transport of goods on behalf of the person” is generally interpreted to imply a
contractual relationship with the carrier or forwarding agent. During audits, tax auditors closely examine this
aspect.

Even in situations where the final customer (C) operates under a Slovak VAT number provided the goods are
transported to Slovakia and the intermediary (B) is not established in Slovakia, and thus, in accordance with
the invoicing directive, the intermediary (B) issues an invoice based on the VAT rules valid in the member
state of its establishment, considering the Luxury Trust judgment, it is recommended that the invoice issued
by the intermediary (B) includes the wording “reverse-charge”.

The simplification should also apply between (B) and (C) where four parties are involved in the chain
transaction and (C) and (D) are established in Slovakia, if the goods are transported by (A) or (B) to Slovakia.
3.5 Acquisition threshold

For non-taxable legal persons and taxable persons not VAT registered in Slovakia, the threshold for acquiring
goods from suppliers in another EU Member State is EUR 14,000.

This acquisition threshold does not apply to the acquisition of goods;

+ which are subject to excise duty, if according to a special regulation the obligation to pay excise duty in
Slovakia arises for the acquirer;

+ bya foreign person unless it is a small business of a foreign person applying for VAT exemption; o

+ a taxable person who has exclusively a permanent establishment in Slovakia, unless it is a small business
of a foreign person applying for VAT exemption.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Slovakia has not adopted any optional ‘effective use and enjoyment’ rules.

4, Taxable event & chargeability

4.1 Principles

For goods, VAT is generally chargeable when they arc made available to the acquirer. For services, VAT
becomes chargeable at the time the services are provided

For transfers or assignments of immovable property, the supply date is the day the property is handed over
for use, provided this occurs before the title’s entry into the real estate register. In construction contracts, the
supply date is the day the construction is handed over.

Slovakia has implemented a system for “partial” and “repeated” taxable supplies:

* partial supplies: goods or services supplied as part of an overall contract, such as construction milestones,
are taxed as they are supplied;

+ repeated supplies: goods or services supplied on a recurring basis (e.g., energy, water, gas, or rental) are
taxed at the latest by the last day of the payment period.

536 | Slovakia

--- Page 37 ---
For partial or repeated supplies for longer than 12 months:

+ general rule: jf the payment covers a supply period of more than 12 months, the supply is deemed made at
the end of each 12-month period until fully supplied;

+ VAT Directive services (Article 44): if subject to tax, these are deemed to be supplied on the last day of
each calendar year if paid for a supply period that is longer than 12 months;

+ utilities for leased properties: goods like electricity and gas are deemed supplied on the invoice date when
billed separately from rent provided that it is an exactly measured quantity;

+ electronic services: electronic communication networks and services are considered supplied on the
invoice date or, if no invoice is issued, by the last day of the third month following the service provision
VAT is due on this last day if the recipient is liable.

For intra-Community supplies of goods repeatedly performed for a period that is longer than a month, the
goods are considered to be supplied at the end of each month until the supply ends.

Additional chargeability conditions:
* consignment contracts: tax liability for the consignee arises the same day as for the consignor;
+ vending machines: VAT liability arises on the day when cash is collected or turnover is determined;
+ intra-Community supplies: VAT becomes chargeable on:

+ the 15th day of the month following supply; or

+ the invoice issue date, if invoiced before the 1 Sth of the following month.

If goods have not left Slovakia by the VAT return deadline, the invoice date is ignored for chargeability
purposes.

If goods are dispatched or transported from Slovakia to a third country outside the EU, the supply date
is considered the day the goods exit the European Union. This must be confirmed by a customs authority
through a customs declaration or a simplified customs declaration.

When goods are supplied by a taxable person facilitating an intra~Community supply, a distance sale (via
digital platforms), or a supply to another taxable person, the date the payment is received is regarded as the
supply date and the date on which VAT becomes chargeable.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT may become chargeable before goods are made available to the acquirer or services are completed if the
full or partial payment is received in advance. This rule does not apply to intra-Community supplies of goods
or exports from the EU.

43 Cash accounting

A taxable person established and VAT registered in Slovakia can choose to apply the cash accounting scheme

for VAT payments if:

+ his tumover did not exceed EUR 100,000 in the previous calendar year, and he reasonably expects it will
not exceed EUR 100,000 in the current year; and

+ the taxable person is not undergoing bankruptcy or liquidation proceedings.

A taxable person meeting these criteria and opting for the cash accounting scheme must begin applying it
from the start of the relevant tax period. Notification must be submitted in writing to the tax office by the end
of the month in which the scheme begins.

This scheme applies to domestic supplies of goods and services subject to Slovak VAT, with output VAT
becoming chargeable upon receipt of payment and input VAT upon payment made.

Invoices under this scheme must clearly state “tax is applied on the basis of payment receipt.” Failure to
include this statement results in VAT liability based on standard rules, and the taxpayer cannot correct the
invoice by adding this wording afterward.

Slovakia | 537

--- Page 38 ---
5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

The Slovak VAT Law permits a correction of the tax base and corresponding VAT for outstanding receivables.
A taxpayer may correct the VAT charged on a supply if the customer does not fully or partially pay for goods
or services supplied in Slovakia, and the receivable becomes irrecoverable.
The VAT Law specifically outlines conditions under which a receivable is considered irrecoverable, based on
various debt recovery frameworks, including:
+ 150 days past due: if 150 days have passed since the due date without payment, the receivable may be
deemed irrecoverable if:
+ the receivable does not exceed EUR 1,000 (including VAT) and the taxpayer has taken steps to collect
the payment;
* the receivable exceeds EUR 1,000 (including VAT) and the taxpayer can demonstrate he has filed a
claim in a court (not an arbitration court) to recover it; and
+ the receivable exceeds EUR 1,000 (including VAT) and the taxpayer shows it is being pursued
through debt enforcement proceedings;
+ bankruptcy or debt discharge proceedings: the receivable is involved in ongoing bankruptcy or debt
discharge processes;
+ winding-up ofa legal entity: the entity responsible for the deb has been dissolved without a legal
successor;
+ debtor’s death: the customer or debtor has passed away; and
+ termination of restructuring proceedings: a resolution terminating restructuring proceedings is published,
and the receivable is not registered in the proceedings.

In each case, the VAT payer must demonstrate that he took actions to collect the receivable.
‘The debtor must repare deducted VAT on unpaid receivables once 100 days have passed since the due date.
Slovak VAT Law includes mechanisms to prevent tax evasion and misuse of bad debt relief.

5.2 Cash discounts

Consider an example invoice:

Supply of goods EUR 1,000
23% VAT BUR 230
Total to be paid EUR 1,230

If payment is made within 14 days, a 2% cash discount may be applied.

If payment is received within 14 days, the Slovak VAT Law allows for a reduction in the taxable basis to reflect a
price reduction or discount for early payment. According to Slovak interpretation, if early payment occurs on the
day of supply, the original invoice should show a taxable basis already reduced by the discount amount.

In cases where the early payment is made after the supply date, a credit invoice may be required to reflect the
discount. If a credit invoice is not issued, Articel 69 (5) of the VAT Act specifies that any person who declares
VAT on an invoice or other sales document is liable for the VAT stated. Consequently, the supplier may be
required to settle the entire VAT amount declared on the original invoice.

A supplier registered for VAT in Slovakia is not required to issue a credit invoice if a discount is applied after
the tax obligation to another VAT-registered payer in Slovakia has arisen, as long as both parties have agreed
to this arrangement in writing.

5.3 Open Market Value

When goods or services are supplied to individuals closely related to the taxpayer, and the consideration is
lower than the open market value, the taxable amount is adjusted to the open market value if the recipient is
either not a taxpayer or is a taxpayer without full deduction rights for those goods or services.

538 | Slovakia

--- Page 39 ---
The term “open market value” refers to the price the recipient would have to pay to an independent supplier
within the country, at the same marketing stage, under fair competition conditions.

If no comparable supply can be identified, “open market vatue” is determined as follows: (a) For goods, it
is the amount not less than the purchase price of similar goods, or if no purchase price exists, the production
cost at the time of supply. (b) For services, it is the amount not less than the cost of providing the service.

5.4 Returnable packaging

‘The tax liability for returnable packaging supplied along with goods, arises for the taxpayer who first markets
these returnable packages with goods in Slovakia. This liability is calculated on the last day of the calendar
year based on the difference between the total number of returnable packages first marketed by the taxpayer
and the total number of such packages returned from the market within that year. If this difference is negative,
the tax amount should be recorded with a minus (-) sign in the VAT records and VAT return.

If, during the calendar year, the taxpayer is the first to market certain returnable packages but also supplies
other returnable packages not originally marketed by them in Slovakia, they cannot claim a negative
difference in the VAT return for these packages.

The taxable amount is determined by multiplying the difference calculated by the deposit amount set for
a returnable package in a separate regulation. This deposit amount includes the deposit rate applicable for
reusable returnable packages as of the last day of the calendar year, minus any applicable tax.

6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate
‘The standard VAT rate in Slovakia is 23%.

6.2 Reduced rates

‘The reduced VAT rates in Slovakia are 19% and 5%

19% rate applies to:

+ goods and services listed in Annexes 7 point 1 and 7a point 1 of the VAT Law excluding imported goods
under the iOSS special scheme (Import One Stop Shop). Key items include:

+ essentially all goods intended for human consumption, except those explicitly listed in point 2 of Annex
No. 7 (to which a VAT rate of 5% will apply):
+ animal products (meat products, cheeses, eggs, other dairy products, fish, other products of animal

origin —e.g., tripe);

+ plant products (other fruits and vegetables, cereals, milling products ~ e.g., flour, other bakery products)
+ animal and vegetable fats and oils;

+ electricity (including related services, if it is a bundled supply of electricity); and

+ non-alcoholic beverages served within catering services, ready-made meals in supermarkets and so-called
takeaways + deliveries.

5% rate applies to:
+ goods and services listed in Annexes 7 points 2 and 3 and 7a point 2 of the VAT Law, excluding imported
goods under the iOSS special scheme (Import One Stop Shop). Key items include:

‘Annex 7 points 2 and 3:

+ pharmaceutical products;

+ certain diagnostic or laboratory reagents;

+ sanitary wares made of plas

+ health tools;

+ printed books, brochures, and other similar printed matter with advertisements not exceeding 50% of the
content;

Slovakia | 539

--- Page 40 ---
+ children’s picture books, drawing books, or coloring books;

+ music, printed or in manuscript form;

+ selected food items; and

+ audiobooks, maps, atlases (if they are educational publications)

Annex 7a point 2:

+ accommodation services (including private accommodation for up to three months);

* restaurant and catering services (excluding other food and beverage services);

* operation of sports facilities — only services related to entry to sports events (passive participation only); and
+ fitness facility entrance fees.

Other items:

Goods and services provided to eligible authorized customers within social economy activities by registered
social enterprises, using 100% of profits for their objectives without distorting competition within the internal
market. Eligible customers are non-taxable individuals or entities in social economy or public administration
sectors. Supply of buildings or parts thereof, including associated land, meeting conditions for state-supported
rental housing, The beneficiary must be a landlord of a state-supported rental unit with an operational contract
for the specific building, excluding non-residential spaces.

Renovation and reconstruction work on buildings or parts thereof, including construction and assembly
work for state-supported rental housing. The beneficiary is a landlord of a state-supported rental unit with an
operational contract, excluding renovations or reconstructions related to non-residential spaces.

ii Exemptions

7 Exemptions with right of deduction

Certain supplies and services are VAT-exempt in Slovakia, with a right to deduct input VAT. These include:

+ exports and intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ goods supplied to non-EU resident travellers (under a refund scheme);

+ processing operations on movable property imported from a third country or acquired within Slovakia for
further processing, where the goods are subsequently exported from the EU by the supplier, customer, or
a representative on their behalf

+ intermediary services related to transactions outside the EU;

sea vessels, aircraft, and related goods and services;

+ supplies made to customers with diplomatic tax immunity (refund scheme-based);

+ supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

* international passenger transport;

+ — supply of goods in customs warehouses, restricted areas of international airports/ports, and onboard
aircraft for passengers departing the EU or traveling to third countries (under specific conditions);

« goods donated free of charge for humanitarian and charity activities, provided a written donation contract
with the Slovak Ministry of Interior exists;

+ goods supplied under customs control or defined warehousing arrangements;

+ _ services related to exported goods or goods under customs control or defined warehousing arrangements;

. supply of goods and services to:

diplomatic missions and consulates from non-Slovak states within the EU, and their staff who are

+ EU institutions (e.g., European Central Bank, European Investment Bank), under conditions set by
international treaties:

+ international organizations and their non-Slovak staff based in other EU Member States, under
international treaties;

+ NATO Member State armed forces, for use by these forces or accompanying civilian staff and
catering facilities, provided they participate in a common defense effort.

540 | Slovakia

--- Page 41 ---
For these VAT exemptions, taxpayers must present official confirmation from the relevant government
authority of another EU Member State, provided by the customer.

74.1 Intra-Community supplies

To prove the applicability of the VAT exemption for intra-Community supplies, the following documentation
is required:

The supplier must gather a combination of documents, such as a copy of the sales invoice, contract, proof of
payment for goods and transport, a delivery note, and, most critically, transport documents. These documents,
or a suitable combination thereof, must confirm that the goods were transported to another EU Member State
(.e., they left Slovakia). The invoice must also reference the VAT exemption (e.g., “VAT-exempt intra-
Community supply, Article 138 of the EU VAT Directive”) and include the VAT identification numbers of
both the supplier and the customer.

Ifa third party transports goods on behalf of the supplier or customer, transport documents (bills of lading/
CMR) or equivalent documents must be signed by an authorized representative of the customer upon receipt.

Slovak authorities apply a strict interpretation of ‘transport on behalf of.’ The tax authorities require evidence
that the party responsible for the transport (the organizer) directly incurred the transport costs, typically
shown by the forwarding agent or carrier issuing the transport invoice to the organizing party. Re-invoicing of
transport costs alone is insufficient,

The Explanatory Notes on the Quick Fixes should also be considered.

In addition to the above, transport documents should ideally include:
+ the identity of the person authorized to confirm receipt;

+ the date and place of goods receipt; and

* identification details for the driver and vehicle.

Additional details are recommended to enhance the documentation in all cases.

These rules supplement Article 45a Implementing Regulation’s transport presumptions and the Explanatory
Notes on Quick Fixes.

TA2 Export of goods from the EU

To prove VAT exemption for exports, companies must have evidence demonstrating that goods have left the
EU, including:

+ — transport documents (e.g, bills of lading/CMR);

* export declaration with exit confirmation; and

+ other valid proofs under customs legislation.

Accurate identification of the party responsible for arranging transport from Slovakia to outside the EU is
essential.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 Customs warehouse.s pecial warehouse and tax warehouse

Slovakia provides a VAT exemption for certain transactions related to the international trade of stored goods
like crude oil, diesel, and petrol listed on international stock exchanges. This exemption applies to specific
goods (e.g, crude oil and mineral oils) and related services in customs warehouses, special warehouses, or
tax warehouses as defined by the VAT Law. The applicable goods are listed in Annex No. 9 of the VAT Law,
categorized by the type of warehouse. Foreign persons exclusively conducting such transactions are not
required to register for VAT in Slovakia.

Customs Warehouse and Special Warehouse (Goods Listed in Part I of Annex No. 9 ~ included below)
+ VAT exemptions apply to the following transactions involving goods listed in Part I of Annex No. 9,
applicable to both customs warehouses and special warehouses:

+ supply of goods listed in Part 1 of Annex No. 9 within a public customs warehouse of type I in
Slovakia, provided these goods are under a customs warehousing procedure (exemptions do not apply
if the warehousing procedure has ended);

+ supply of goods listed in Part I of Annex No. 9 within a special warchouse as defined in 48d of the
VAT Law, provided the procedure has not been terminated; and

+ supply of services related to goods placed under the customs warehousing or special warehousing
regime, per above mentioned supplies.

Tax Warehouse (Goods Listed in Part II of Annex No. 9 — included below)
+ Under 48e of the VAT Law, the following transactions are exempt from VAT when they involve goods
listed in Part II of Annex No. 9 and are part of a tax warehousing arrangement within Slovakia:
+ importation of goods intended for a tax warehousing arrangement within Slovakia.
+ supply of goods intended for tax warehousing within Slovakia and related services.
+ _ supply of goods within the tax warehouse, along with related services.
+ intra-Community acquisition of goods intended for tax warehousing within Slovakia, along with
related services.

‘The VAT exemption does not apply to goods intended for final use or consumption, or to services related to
such goods.

7.4.3.2 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Goods may be stored in a bonded warehouse in Slovakia. For goods arriving from a third country and directly
placed under a bonded warehousing regime, customs duties and VAT are not immediately due. VAT and duties
become payable when the goods are released from the warehouse for free circulation, with the customer
declaring the goods responsible for VAT payment.

If goods leave the bonded warehouse and enter another customs regime, this release does not constitute an
importation.

Please note that the supply of goods within a bonded (customs) warehouse is not exempt from VAT in
Slovakia. In Case C-165/11 (Profitube), the Court of Justice of EU addressed whether Slovakia’s decision
not to exempt supplies of goods within a customs warehouse from VAT aligns with EU legislation. The Court
ruled that such supplies may indeed be subject to VAT in EU Member States that have not opted to exempt
these types of transactions.

72 Exemptions without the right of deduction

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Certain services in the public interest are exempt from VAT, including:
+ hospital services;

+ medical and paramedical care;

* cultural activities;

+ ambulance services;

+ undertaker services;

+ educational services;

* social and cultural services; and

+ some sports-related services.

Some of these exemptions apply only if the supplier is a non-profit organization and/or if no competitive
distortion arises.

7.2.2 Other exemptions

Additional VAT exemptions apply to:

* — financial and banking services;

* insurance services;

+ collective investment fund management;
+ certain games of chance;

* postal services;

+ non-commercial activities of public radio and TV broadcasters;
+ letting of immovable property;

+ supply of immovable property; and

+ services provided to members.

7.2.3. Option for taxation
‘Taxable persons may opt to tax certain supplies and leases of immovable property, under specific conditions.

Slovakia | 543

--- Page 44 ---
Supply of Immovable Property

‘The supply of a building or part of a building, including the building land on which it stands, is exempt from
VAT, except if the supply occurs within 5 years of the building’s first approval for use or within 5 years of its
initial use.

If the building or part of it undergoes reconstruction requiring a construction permit, and the cost of the
reconstruction is at least 40% of the building’s market value before the work started, the 5-year period for the
VAT exemption restarts from the date of reconstruction.

Land supply is also exempt from VAT, except in the case of building land. If building land is supplied with a
structure or part of a structure, the VAT treatment of the land follows the VAT regime applied to the structure.

A taxpayer supplying a building or part of it that meets VAT exemption conditions may choose to waive the
exemption, except in cases of housing construction, individual flats, or suites within residential buildings. For
housing construction used for non-residential purposes, the taxpayer may opt out of the VAT exemption only
for the portion not intended for housing.

A “suite in a residential building” refers to a room or set of rooms in a residential building that serves the
same purpose as a flat,

Letting of Immovable Property

Leasing of immovable property is generally exempt, except for:
* accommodation services;

* parking spaces;

+ permanently installed equipment and machinery: and

+ safe rentals.

A taxable person who leases or sub-leases immovable property, or a part of it, to another taxable person may
choose not to apply the VAT exemption to the lease. However, this option does not apply to the letting or sub-leas-
ing ofa flat, detached house, or a suite in a residential building, or parts thereof, which remain VAT-exempt.

8 Liability

81 Reverse charges

Slovakia mandates several reverse charge rules in addition to the standard reverse charge mechanism for
general B2B services as performed by non-resident suppliers, applicable to taxable persons with a Slovak
VAT number. ‘This mechanism aligns with Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT Directive. Additionally, a
reverse charge applies to natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in
Slovakia,

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)
For supplies of goods and services with a place of supply in Slovakia, when provided by a non-established
taxable person, the VAT liability is shifted to the customer if the customer is a taxable person established
in Slovakia. It is not required for the customer to be registered for VAT purposes in Slovakia. Therefore,
the domestic reverse charge applies only if the supplier is not established in Slovakia and the customer is

a taxable person established in Slovakia. The reverse charge does not apply if the non-established supplier
provides a domestic supply of goods or services to a non-taxable legal person established in Slovakia or to
another non-established taxable person, even if that customer is VAT-registered in Slovakia,

‘The reverse charge applies even if the non-established supplier is VAT-registered in Slovakia, The supplier’s
invoice should indicate the application of the reverse charge.
Exceptions to the general reverse charge include:

544 | Slovakia

--- Page 45 ---
+ distance sales of goods;

+ goods in Part I of Annex No. 9, which are not exempt from VAT in a public customs warehouse of type I
(48c(1)(a) of the VAT Act):

+ goods in Part I of Annex No. 9, which are not exempt in a special warehouse (48c(1)(b) of the VAT Law);
and

+ goods in Part II of Annex No. 9, which are not exempt in a tax warehouse (48c(2)(c) of the VAT Law).

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 Directive)

Domestic reverse charge rules apply to the following supplies of goods and/or services between two taxable

persons, provided both are VAT-registered in Slovakia, even if not established there:
supply of metal waste and scrap;

+ transfer of greenhouse gas emission quotas;

+ supply of construction (unless VAT-exempt, such as for constructions older than five years where the
supplier opts to apply Stovak VAT, and where the construction plot includes a building);

+ supply of immovable property as part of enforcement or bankruptcy procedures;

* goods provided as security by one taxable person to another in execution of that security;

+ — supply of specified agricultural products and metals (except when a simplified invoice is issued);

+ — supply of cellular phones and integrated circuits if the taxable basis on the invoice exceeds EUR 5.000;

* supply of investment gold; and

+ — supply of construction works, which may include services, building supply or parts, and goods with
assembly or installation if such work constitutes construction.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Businesses that form part of a VAT group in Slovakia are jointly and severally liable for the VAT debts of each
member for the entire period that the entity was part of the VAT group.

A taxpayer receiving goods or services in Slovakia may be held liable for unpaid VAT from the previous
stage, as indicated on an invoice, if the supplier failed to pay or became unable to pay the VAT. This liability
applies if, at the time the VAT became chargeable, the taxpayer knew or, based on reasonable grounds, should
or could haye known that all or part of the VAT on the goods or services would not be paid.

‘The taxpayer should reasonably suspect potential VAT non-payment if:

+ the consideration on the invoice is, without economic justification, unreasonably high or low (artificially
inflated or reduced fees);

+ at the time the VAT became chargeable, a statutory body member or shareholder of the taxpayer receiving
the goods or services was also part of the supplier’s statutory body or a member (indicating a related-
party transaction); and

* the payment for the supply or part of it was made to a bank account other than the supplier’s bank account
published on the tax administration’s website. VAT payers are required to declare the bank accounts used
for business activities in Slovakia, which are publicly listed by the tax administration. Payments made to
accounts not listed may indicate a risk of VAT non-payment.

9. Recovery of input VAT

ol Non-recoverable input VAT

Input VAT cannot be recovered in the following cases:

+ supplies not used for business purposes;

+ supplies acquired or imported for an exempt business activity without the right to deduct input VAT;

+ restaurant and catering expenses;

+ purchases related to entertainment activities;

* transitional items, such as suspension accounts or disbursements, and

+ travel agencies cannot deduct VAT on supplies for travel services directly consumed by travellers.

For mixed-use capital goods (e.g., vehicles, real estate), the deductible VAT cannot exceed the professional
use of the asset.

Slovakia | 545

--- Page 46 ---
Non-resident taxable persons registered for VAT in Slovakia, making supplies where the VAT liability is
shifted to the recipient under a local reverse charge, generally cannot deduct input VAT through their VAT
return, Input VAT may only be recoverable via a VAT refund claim. The Slovak tax administration currently
favours VAT refunds based on reimbursement requests over offsetting VAT through a VAT return, particularly
for non-EU taxable persons not VAT-registered outside the FU.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Offset and carry-forward: deductible VAT should first be offset with any VAT due in the VAT return. If there

is excess deductible VAT that cannot be offset, it is carried forward to the next VAT period. If a refundable

amount remains in the following period, the Slovak tax authorities will refund the excess within 30 days after

the tax return filing.

+ if the taxpayer is not required to file a VAT retumn for the period immediately following the one in which
the excess input VAT arose, the refund period is 30 days after the filing deadline for the return in which
the excess was accounted for.

Conditions for refund within 30 days: tax authorities will refund excess input VAT within 30 days after the tax

return filing deadline if the following conditions are met:

= the taxpayer’s VAT period is a calendar month;

+ the taxpayer has been VAT-registered for at least the 12 months preceding the month of excess input VAT
creation; and

+ the taxpayer had no outstanding tax, customs, or mandatory social insurance contributions exceeding
EUR 1,000 in total for the 6 months before the month of excess input VAT creation.

‘Tax audit: if the tax authorities initiate an audit within the refund period, they will refund the excess input
VAT, as determined by the audit, within 10 days of completing the audit.

9.3 Capital goods and services — adjustment periods

‘The adjustment of deducted VAT applies to investment property (any goods with an acquisition cost
excluding VAT greater than 1,700 euros, which have a separate technical-economic determination and are
long-term assets of the VAT payer, and intangible assets with an acquisition cost excluding VAT greater than
2,400 euros, if they are long-term assets of the VAT payer).

+ immovable property: 19 years following the year of first use.
+ movable property and intangible property: 4 years following the year of first use.

When the capital goods or services are transferred as a ‘transfer of going concern’, the capital goods and
services adjustment period continues with the legal successor. The seller must provide the buyer with relevant
details on input VAT deduction related to the property’s acquisition.

9.4 VAT refund for foreigners

94.1 VAT refund to EU taxable persons
L:U-based taxable persons must submit VAT refund applications electronically through their home country’s
tax portal. The claim periods and minimum ctaim limits are as follows:

+ annual limit (for a full calendar year or the remainder): EUR 50; and

+ interim limit (for a period of at least 3 months); EUR 400,

The minimum claim period is 3 consecutive months within the same year, except for the year-end remainder.
The maximum period is one calendar year. Claims must be submitted by 30 September of the year following
the claim year.

If the taxable amount on an invoice or import document is EUR 1,000 or more, or if the invoice concerns fuel
totalling EUR 250 or more, the applicant must electronically submit a copy of the invoice or import document
along with the refund application. In some cases, the tax authorities may request additional information,

546 | Slovakia

--- Page 47 ---
which may include original or copies of invoices or import documents relevant to the refund claim, even if
the taxable amount is below these thresholds.

The tax authorities are required to issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receiving it. If
additional information is requested, this decision period may be extended to 6 or 8 months.

If the refund is approved, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account provided by
the applicant. The time limit for filing an appeal is 15 days following the decision. If the appeal is unsuccessful,
the claimant may proceed with legal action in a Slovakian court, Input VAT is not recoverable for:

+ non-business-related supplies;

+ exempt business activities without VAT deduction rights;

* — restaurant and catering expenses;

* purchases for entertainment purposes; and

+ transitional items (e.g., suspense accounts, disbursements).

A person requesting a VAT refund must notify the Slovak Tax Authority if they receive a credit note from
their supplier indicating a price reduction after the VAT has already been refunded. In this case, the applicant
is required to repay the relevant amount of VAT to the tax authority.

9.4.2 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

Slovakia requires reciprocity for VAT refunds to non-EU businesses, though the official list of reciprocity
countries is not published, The model refund application form can be found in Annex 2 of the Slovakian VAT
Law.

The minimum claim limit for a calendar year or its remainder is BUR 50, with an annual claim period
maximum. The claim deadline is 30 June of the year following the year in which the VAT was paid. Non-EU
businesses may also submit a semi-annual claim if the refundable VAT amount is EUR 1,000 or more.
Claims should be sent to:

Daitovy tirad Bratislava

Sevéenkova 32

P.O.Box 154

850 00 Bratislava

Slovakia

The claim should include:

+ the original invoice from the VAT-registered supplier in Slovakia, showing VAT in Euros, along with any
related import documents; and

* a certificate from the tax authority in the claimant’s non-EU country verifying VAT registration or a
similar tax on consumption. This certificate must be dated within the last year and aligns with Annex 3 of
the Slovakian VAT Law.

Slovak tax authorities are required to issue a decision on the refund within 6 months of receiving the complete
application. If approved, the refund will be issued in Euros, either to a Slovak or foreign bank account
specified by the claimant, with any international transfer fees borne by the claimant.

The appeal period for any refund decision is 15 days. If the appeal is unsuccessful, the claimant may seek
recourse in Slovakian court.

Input VAT is non-recoverable in cases involving:

+ goods and services not used for business purposes;

+ purchases related to exempt activities without VAT deduction rights;
* restaurant and catering services;

+ purchases for entertainment purposes; and

+ transitional items (e.g., suspense accounts, disbursements).

Claimants must inform the Slovak Tax Authority if they receive a credit note reflecting a price reduction after
VAT has been refunded, in which case they must repay the corresponding VAT amount.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice in Slovakia must include the following details:

+ the supplier’s name, address, and VAT identification number;

+ the customer’s name, address, and VAT identification number;

+ aunique sequential invoice number;

= the supply date of goods or services, or payment receipt date if different from the invoice date:

+ the invoice issue date;

*  quantily and type of goods supplied or services rendered;

+ taxable amount per rate, unit price excluding tax, and any reductions or discounts if not included in the
unit price;

+ the applicable tax rate or VAT exemption, referencing the VAT Law, EU VAT Directive, or indicating
“dodanie je oslobodené od dane.”;

* — total tax amount in Euros (with exceptions for special schemes);

+ self-billing reference (“vyhotovenie faktiiry odberatefom”) if applicable;

* reverse charge reference (“prenesenie daiiovej povinnosti’) if applicable;

* exemption reference for intra~Community or export supplies;

* information about new means of transport, if relevant;

+ special scheme references, such as:
+ margin scheme for second-hand goods: “prava zdafiovania prirazky — pouzity tovar”;
* margin scheme for works of art: “iprava zdaitovania prirazky — umelecké diela”;
+ margin scheme for collector’s items and antiques: “tprava zdaitovania prirézky — zberatel’ské

predmety a slaroZitnosti”;

* tour operator margin scheme: “tiprava zdahovania priraZky — cestovné kanceldrie”; and

+ if'a tax representative is used, their name, trade name, address, and tax LD.

Invoices may be issued in foreign languages; however, VAT auditors may require an official Slovak
translation during audits.

10.1.2 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice does not need to include all details as listed above. It applies to:

* any document for goods or services where the total, including tax, does not exceed EUR 100. It need not
include customer details or unit price;

+ receipts from electronic cash registers not exceeding EUR 400, and documents from aulomatic refuelling
machines for goods paid by electronic means, not exceeding EUR 400. These documents do not need to
include customer details or unit price;

* acredit note, debit note, or similar document altering the original invoice. Corrective invoices must
include the own invoice’s sequential number, the original invoice’s sequential number and the data that
has been adjusted.

10.1.3 Time limits

Invoicing: An invoice must be issued within 15 days from the date the tax liability arises or, for intra
Community supplies, by the 15* day of the month following the month in which the supply took place.

For services provided with a place of supply per 15() of the VAT Law in another EU Member State, the
invoice should be issued by the 15" day of the end of the calendar month in which the services were supplied,
or payment was received.

Credit and Debit Notes: a credit note or debit note must be issued within 15 days from the end of the calendar
month in which the correction event for the taxable amount occurred.

548 | Slovakia

--- Page 49 ---
10.1.4 Currency
Invoices can be issued in a foreign currency; however, the VAT amount must be expressed in Euros.

10.1.5 Conversion and exchange rate
When calculating VAT in Euros, foreign currency amounts must be converted using the reference exchange
rate set by the European Central Bank or the National Bank of Slovakia on the day before the tax liability
arises. Taxpayers may also use the exchange rate applied in customs regulations as of the tax obligation date.

If chosen, this exchange rate must be declared to the tax office in writing and applied consistently throughout
the calendar year.

10.1.6 — Self-billing

Self-billing is permitted under Slovak VAT law if a written agreement between the supplier and customer
outlines the conditions under which the customer can issue invoices on behalf of the supplier.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices and customs declarations must be retained for 10 years. For invoices related to immovable property,
the storage period is 20 years. Invoices can be archived electronically or in paper format, regardless of the
original format.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Taxable persons may issue electronic invoices in any format that ensures origin authenticity, content integrity,
and readability. Client acceptance of electronic invoices is required, but silent acceptance is allowed.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

‘The standard VAT return period is a calendar month. Taxable persons with annual turnover below EUR
100,000 may opt for quarterly VAT returns.

10.2.2. Filing of VAT return and payment

A VAT return must be filed electronically, and the tax due paid within 25 days after the end of the tax period
Generally, foreign taxable persons are exempt from filing if no tax liability or deduction rights apply in the
period.

10.2.3 European sales listing

A European sales listing must be submitted electronically within 25 days after the end of each tax period if
relevant transactions are present.

Taxable persons can opt for quarterly submissions if their intra-Community supplies remain below
EUR 50,000 in the quarter and the previous four quarters. However, if the quarterly threshold is exceeded, the
taxpayer must file monthly listings for the current and future quarters of the respective year.

A European sales listing must include:

+ the VAT identification number of the person submitting the listing;

+ for goods: the customer’s VAT ID and the total value of goods supplied to each customer;

+ for own goods transfer: the VAT ID issued to the taxpayer in the EU Member State where dispatch
concluded and the corresponding taxable value;

+ for triangular transactions: the VAT [D of the final customer in the EU Member State where the dispatch
concluded and the value of goods supplied;

+ for services: the customer’s VAT ID and the total service value supplied to each customer; and

* for call-off stock transfers: the VAT ID of the designated recipient, or if replaced, the VAT ID of the
substitute.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

‘Taxable persons in Slovakia must submit VAT ledgers electronically, in addition to filing the VAT return and
European sales listing. Failure to file these statements may result in cancellation of the VAT registration.

Slovakia | 549

--- Page 50 ---
If the tax authorities find that a statement has not been submitted, or if they question its accuracy or
completeness, they may request a new or corrected statement. The tax authorities may also block excess
VAT refunds until a correct and complete statement is filed. In such cases, taxpayers have a 5-working-day
deadline to submit the correction,

‘The national recapitulative statement covers domestic supplies, domestic purchases, and cross-border reverse-
charge purchases of goods and services, but it does not include intra-Community supplies of goods, imports,
or exports.

Based on the official VAT ledger template provided by the Slovak Ministry of Finance, the following
information must be reported for each output and input transaction according to different invoice categories:

following information is required:

+ customer's VAT number,

+ invoice sequential number;

* taxable point date (supply or payment receipt date);

+ taxable amount in Euros;

+ VAT amount in Euros;

+ applied VAT rate;and

+ correction code (applicable only to additional statements)

If the supplier issues an invoice where the customer must apply Slovak VAT through a local reverse charge
mechanism (e.g., for construction work, specific agricultural goods, metals, mobile phones, or integrated

* customer's VAT number;

* invoice sequential number;

+ taxable point date;

+ taxable basis in Euros,

* customs code (for certain agricultural products and metals; first four digits);

+ specific goods details (mobile phones, integrated circuits);

+ quantity and unit of measurement; and

* correction code (applicable only to additional statements).

If the customer applies Slovakian VAT via cross-border or local reverse charge (excluding goods imports),
regardless of VAT deduction:

+ supplier's VAT number;

+ taxable basis in Euros;

* VAT amount in Euros;

+ proportion of VAT deduction in Euros; and

+ correction code (applicable only to additional statements).

+ supplier's VAT number;

+ VAT deduction proportion in Euros, and

For simplified VAT invoices with VAT deduction:

* total taxable bases for VAT in Euros;

+ total VAT amount in Euros;

+ total VAT deduction amount in Euros; and

+ correction code (applicable only to additional statements).

If the total input VAT from simplified invoices in a period exceeds EUR 3,000, each invoice must be listed
separately.

Corrective Invoices (Debit/Credit Notes)

When corrective invoices are issued:
+ customer's VAT number;

+ corrective invoice sequential number;

+ original invoice sequential number;

+ taxable basis difference in Euros;

+ VAT difference in Euros;

+ VAT rate applied;

+ customs code (if applicable for agricultural products and metals);

+ product details (for mobile phones, integrated circuits, if applicable);
«quantity difference and unit of measurement;

+ supplier's VAT number;

+ corrective invoice sequential number;

+ original invoice sequential number;

+ taxable basis difference in Euros;

+ VAT difference in Euros;

+ VAT rate applied;

+ deducted VAT difference in Euros;

+ VAT deduction correction (for bad debt relief); and

+ correction code (applicable only to additional statements).

--- Page 52 ---
Filings in prescribed electronic forms (e.g. tax retums, EU sales lists, national recapitulative statements)
submitted by individuals or entities under a duty to communicate electronically (such as VAT taxpayers or
advisors) are considered not received if delivered by any non-electronic means

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

In Slovakia, the standard tax assessment period is 5 years. However, under specific conditions outlined in the
Tax Administration Act, the tax authorities have the discretion to extend this assessment period to a maximum
of 10 years.

10.2.6 Cash accounting scheme
Slovakia offers a Cash Accounting Scheme, where VAT liability and deduction are based on the payment date
for supplied goods or services. If a taxpayer assigns a receivable, tax liability arises on the assignment date,
with the tax base being the price (excluding VAT) for the goods or services.

A taxpayer may use the Cash Accounting Scheme if:

+ the previous year’s turnover did not exceed EUR 100,000 and it is reasonably expected that it will not
exceed this amount in the current year; and

+ no bankruptcy or liquidation proceedings have been initiated for the taxpayer.

This scheme is not widely used in practice, as dominant market players gencrally do not accept suppliers
using it,

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for a late registration
The penalty for Jate VAT registration ranges from EUR 60 to EUR 20,000.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time
The minimum penalty for late filing of a VAT return or European sales listing is EUR 30 per instance, and the
maximum is EUR 16,000 per late filing. If the return or listing is filed only after a tax authority appeal, the
maximum penalty doubles to EUR 32,000.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If VAT is not paid on time, interest is charged at four times the European Central Bank’s base interest rate on
the unpaid VAT, with a minimum interest rate of 15%, This interest accrues for a maximum of 4 years.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If VAT is incorrectly declared, the Slovak VAT authorities impose penalties, with escalating rates based on the

timing of correction relative to an audit. The penalty structure is as follows:

+ pre-audit submission: if an additional VAT return is submitted before a VAT audit begins, the penalty is
set at the European Central Bank’s base interest rate, with a minimum of 3% annually;

+ within 15 days of audit atart: if the correction occurs within 15 days after an audit has started, the penalty
doubles to at least 7% annually; and

+ after audit completion: if VAT adjustments are imposed by the authorities following an audit, the penalty
triples to at least 10% annually,

The penalty accrues daily from the original VAT filing/payment deadline until the additional VAT return is
filed or the post-audit deadline for filing a corrected VAT retum. The total penalty may range between 1% and
100% of the difference in VAT.

Taxpayers are encouraged to file additional VAT returns within 15 days after a VAT audit begins to potentially
reduce penalties. By voluntarily adjusting VAT liabilities at this stage, penalties are lower compared to those
applied after a full audit.

10.3.5 Penalties with respect to the VAT ledger statement
The Slovak tax authority may impose substantial penalties for non-compliance with VAT obligations:

552 | Slovakia

--- Page 53 ---
* up to EUR 10,000 for failures such as not filing the required statement, filing it late, submitting
incomplete or incorrect information, or not correcting errors as requested; and
+ up to EUR 100,000 if the taxable person repeatedly fails to comply with these requirements.

The penalty amount is determined based on the duration and complexity of the compliance failure.

10.4 Tntrastat

In Slovakia, businesses must submit a separate Intrastat report to the Statistical Office of the Slovak Republic,
‘Statisticky Grad Slovenskej republiky.”
10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

For general imports and exports of goods within the EU, the threshold is set at EUR 1,000,000, For entities in
the agriculture and food sectors, the thresholds are EUR 200,000 for imports and EUR 400,000 for exports,

Entities must track cumulative monthly values for imports and exports within the EU. Reporting obligations
begin in the month where either import or export values exceed the threshold.

Distance sales transactions must also be included in the Intrastat declaration.

10.4.2. Intrastat data to be reported

‘The following data must be included in the Intrastat report:

+ flow (arrival or dispatch);

+ VAT identification number assigned to the reporting party ;

+ representative’s VAT identification number (if applicable);

+ reporting period;

+ total number of items;

* sequential report number;

+ sequential item number;

+ description of goods - optional information;

+ commodity’s 8-digit Combined Nomenclature (CN) code;

+ value of the goods;

* partner EU Member State (EU Member State of consignment on arrival and destination on dispatch);
+ region of destination (intra-EU import) / Region of origin (intra-EU export) — optional information:
= country of origin ;

+ net weight in kilograms;

* quantity in supplementary units:

* nature of the transaction;

+ delivery terms;

+ mode of transport;

+ VAT number of the partner in the member state of destination (intra-EU export);
+ section A: Signature; and

+ section B: Contact information.

Aggregation of transactions

If the data to be entered in the individual sections of the INTRASTAT-SK report, with the exception of
section 7 Description of goods, section 9 Value of goods, section 13 Net weight, and section 14 Quantity in
supplementary units, are identical, including section 18 VAT number of the partner in the member state of
destination, the data related to several transactions can be summed up (aggregated) into one item in the report.

Usually, the reporting unit uses aggregation if it prepares the data for the reports manually. If the data for

the INTRASTAT-SK reports are (aken from the information system of the reporting unit and the reports are
generated automatically, aggregation of transactions is not necessary.
