Slovenia

The European Union (BU) VAT Directive is implemented in the Slovenian legislation through “Zakon 0
davku na dodano vrednost ( ZDDV-1)” (hereafter referred to as the “VAT Act”). In Slovenia, VAT is referred
to.as “davek na dodano vrednost” or “DDV”.

1. Taxable persons

Ll Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

A taxable person does not have to register for VAT if in the previous calendar year he did not exceed the
amount of 60,000 EUR in annual turnover on the territory of Slovenia and does not exceed this amount of
tumover in the current calendar year (Article 94(1) of the VAT Act).

Ifrelated persons perform a supply of goods of the same type or a supply of services of the same nature, the
amount of taxable turnover which each related person achieves by himself will be (for the purposes of finding
whether the threshold is exceeded or not) considered to be the total value of the supply by the related persons
within a 12-month period. Related persons are the persons defined as related in corporate and personal
income tax legislation.

The threshold includes the following transactions:

+ the value of taxed supplies of goods and services;

+ the value of transactions which are VAT exempt, but the taxable person has the right to deduct input VAT
in relation to these transactions (e.g. transactions related to the export of goods, transactions exempt under
diplomatic and consular arrangements); and

+ the value of real estate transactions, certain financial transactions and insurance services, unless those
transactions are considered as ancillary transactions.

Disposals of tangible and intangible assets are not included in the definition of the taxable transactions.

Even though VAT registration for the mentioned small businesses is not obligatory, they can register for VAT
voluntarily. However. in case of voluntary registration they must remain registered in the current and next
calendar year.

‘The VAT registration application must be filed via the electronic portal of the Slovenian tax authorities called
“eDavki” (eTaxes). The application cannot be filed on paper or personally at the competent tax office. On “eDavki””
the taxable person will fill in the form DDV-P2 “claim for issuing VAT identification number” and file also a
document proving that it performs or plans to perform taxable activities (e.g. contract, pre-contract, business plan,
documents on purchases for the purposes of performing business activities, or any similar document).

Form DDV-P2 can be downloaded at: hitps://tinyurl.com/ewrmypr7

12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

The registration threshold (60,000 EUR, mentioned above) applies also for foreign taxable persons with its
seat in the EU (SME Scheme since | January 1 2025). If the conditions to apply SME Scheme are fulfilled
and a taxable person obtains the special EX number, he can benefit from the VAT exemption.

‘Taxable persons established in non-EU countries cannot apply the SME Scheme and are obliged to register.
The VAT registration application must be submitted at least 15 days before the first taxable transaction.
Foreign taxable persons are also obliged to apply for the VAT number via electronic portal “eDavki” or if they

personally appear at special business registration points (SPOT), but first they need to acquire a Slovenian tax
number, which means that they must be registered in the Slovenian tax register.

Slovenia | 555

--- Page 56 ---
Foreign taxable persons obtain a Slovenian tax number by providing the following documents by post or

personally to the tax office which is authorized for the territory in Slovenia where they plan to perform activities:

*  aregistration form DR-04 (application for entry of the legal entity into the tax register);

* an extract from the court or trade register;

* confirmation about their bank account; and

+ acontract, pre-contract or other document proving what activities the foreign legal entity will perform in
Slovenia.

However, before the taxable person is able to obtain a Slovenian tax number, his representative has to obtain
it first. The following documentation / information is needed:

* — aregistration form DR-02 (application for entry of the natural person into the tax register);

+ readable copy of passport/ID card; and

* tax number of the director(s) in residence;

When applying for a VAT number in Slovenia, foreign taxable persons need to file the following:

* aregistration form DDV-P3;

+ acertificate of status of being a VAT taxable person in its home country; and

* acontract, pre-contract or other document proving what activities the foreign legal entity will perform in
Slovenia.

In case the foreign taxable person authorizes another person to obtain the Slovenian tax and VAT number in his
name, an adequate power of attomey prepared according to the Slovenian General Administrative Procedure Act
(Zakon o splosnem upravnem postopku, ZUP) must also be filed. Furthermore, in this case the foreign taxable
person must also sign the “Form for granting of external authorizations in ¢Taxes system”, upon which the author-
ized person can file the VAT registration application in the name of the foreign legal entity via portal “eDavki”.

Further information can be found at: htips://tinyurl.com/27wn27 pt

Taxable persons established outside the EU are obliged to appoint a fiscal representative in Slovenia, while
taxable persons established in other EU Member States may choose to appoint one, As a general rule, it takes
a couple of weeks to register for VAT purposes.

13 VAT grouping

Currently, the VAT grouping is not possible in Slovenia.

However, from | January 2026, two or more taxable persons established in Slovenia, who are financially, eco-
nomically, and organizationally connected, could be considered as a single taxable person and act as a VAT group.
14 Fiscal representative

A foreign taxable person from another EU Member State can obtain a direct VAT registration. It does not have

to appoint a fiscal representative.

However, these taxable persons are obliged to appoint a fiscal representative in case of importing goods into
Slovenia according to the Customs procedure 42 without obtaining a Slovenian VAT number (i.e. exempt
import followed by an intra-Community supply to another EU Member State).

As explained above, in case of non-EU countries the nomination of a tax representative is always mandatory
whereas for taxable persons established in other EU Member States it is always optional.

‘The following requirements for VAT representatives are laid down in Art. 4 of the Rules on the
Implementation of the VAT Act. The representative:

+ must conduct an economic activity in Slovenia;

+ must have its company seat or habitual residence in Slovenia; and

+ must be registered for VAT in Slovenia.

‘The representative is jointly and severally liable for VAT and penalties.

--- Page 57 ---
2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

In the event of a transfer of a business or a part thereof to another taxable person, whether for consideration
or not or as a contribution to a company, no supply of goods is deemed to have taken place. The essential
requirement is that a business or a part thereof is taken over by another taxable person who is acting as the
legal successor of the transferor.

‘The transferee must continue with the taxable economic activity (i.e. not VAT exempt activity without the
right of deduction) in order for the transfer of goods to be deemed as ‘no supply’. A transfer of a business
can take place either through the purchase by another existing taxable person or by a company that is yet
to be established (ie. in case of a merger). In the former case, the transferee must already be registered for
VAT while in the latter case it is sufficient that the newly created company is registered for VAT purposes
immediately after its registration in the Company Register,

A contribution is defined as a transfer of goods in exchange for the participation in the capital of the
transferee. It is also required that the contributions (e.g. goods) not be used for purposes for which there is no
right of VAT deduction (i.e. for exempt activities)

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Slovenia implemented the provisions laid down in Articles 16 and 26 of the EU VAT Directive, With respect
to Article 16, VAT will not be applied to the free provision of samples in reasonable amounts to buyer or
future buyers and awarding gifts of small value carried out by the taxable person as part of his economic
activity, under the condition that those gifts are given occasionally and not to the same persons. Gifts of small
value are gifts with a value under 20 EUR (excluding VAT).

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

In Slovenia taxable persons can import goods exempt of VAT under the “Customs procedure 42”. Importation

performed according to the customs procedure 42, represents the importation of goods from a non-EU

country to Slovenia, if imported goods are not destined for Slovenia but for another EU Member State.

Customs procedure 42 can only be used if the further supply of the imported goods from Slovenia to another

EU Member State can be considered as an intra-Community supply of goods exempt from VAT.

‘Taxable persons do not need a special license to declare such import. However, they must - at the time of

declaring the import - share the following information with the customs office:

+ their VAT identification number issued by the Slovenian tax authorities or the VAT identification number
of their fiscal representative, also issued by the Slovenian tax authorities;

+ a VAT identification number of the customer of the goods from the other EU Member State (or the taxable
person’s own VAT identification number issued by the other EU Member State when the imported goods
are transported from Slovenia to another EU Member State for the purposes of the taxable person); and

* _ aproof that the imported goods are destined to be transported or sent from Slovenia to another EU
Member State (e.g. transport document CMR).

Taxable persons established outside the EU are obliged to appoint a fiscal representative in Slovenia in

order to import the goods under Customs procedure 42 into Slovenia. Taxable persons established in the EU

(however outside Slovenia) have in relation to such an import two possibilities:

* to register for VAT in Slovenia and use their own Slovenian VAT number when declaring the import; or to
appoint a fiscal representative in Slovenia (VAT registration in Slovenia is not needed in this case); and

* to use the VAT number of the fiscal representative when declaring the import. The fiscal representative is
obliged to file the VAT returns and European sales listings (recap statements) in the name of the foreign
taxable person.

Slovenia | 557

--- Page 58 ---
2.3.2. Postponed accounting on import

Purchasing goods from a country outside the EU to Slovenia is subject to import VAT. In general, import VAT
is paid in the same way as a custom duty at the time of the importation (Customs procedure). However, if

the person liable for import VAT is a VAT-registered person established in Slovenia, the import VAT may be
declared (and deducted at the same time) through VAT return.

Non-established but VAT registered taxable persons are also able to do so but only if they appoint a special
fiscal representative for this purpose.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Simplification rules for consignment and call-off stock in Slovenia are provided for in the VAT Act. The
simplification regime for call-off stock arrangements is in line with the EU VAT Directive.

3.2. _ Installation supply

In case the supplied goods are installed or assembled, with or without a trial run by the supplier or on his
behalf, the place of such installation supply is the place where the goods are installed or assembled
(Art 20(5) of the VAT Act).

There is no specific legal definition of installation supplies in the VAT Act. However, if the foreign supplier
does not register for VAT in Slovenia and does not charge Slovenian VAT on the invoice, the reverse charge
applies.

3.3 Distance sales

As of | July 2021, the distance selling threshold covers all European sales of telecommunication, broadcast-
ing and electronically supplied services as well as distance sales of goods and has been set at FUR 10,000.

Once the threshold is exceeded, the EU supplier may either register directly in Slovenia or in their country of
establishment and account for Slovenian VAT due in the OSS return.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Simplified triangulation rules (ABC supplies) are applied in Slovenia in accordance with the EU Directive.
‘Taxable persons A, B and C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU Member
States (i.e. in Member States 1, 2 and 3). The goods must be transported directly from A in Member State | to
C in Member State 3.

If the final customer / second acquirer (C) is registered for VAT in Slovenia, the intra-Community acquisition

of goods that was carried out by the middleman (B) in Slovenia, shall be exempt from VAT and B’s supply to

C falls under the reverse charge mechanism if the following conditions were met (Art 48(2) of VAT Act:

+ Slovenia is the country of arrival of the goods:

+ the acquisition of the goods is performed by a taxable person (B) established outside Slovenia and
registered for VAT in another EU Member State;

+ the goods are acquired for the purposes of the subsequent supply performed by B;

* the goods acquired in Slovenia are transported to Slovenia directly from another EU Member State and
are intended for the customer of the subsequent supply (C):

+ the final customer C is a taxable person registered for VAT in Slovenia or a non-taxable legal person
identified for VAT in Slovenia; and

+ the VAT on the subsequent supply performed by B to C is paid by C.

According to the explanation of the FA and referring to the ECJ case C-247/17 (Luxury Trust Automobil)
the reference “reverse charge” is an obligatory part of the invoice, issued by middleman in the triangular

558 | Slovenia

--- Page 59 ---
transaction, The purpose of this reference is to inform the final recipient that he is liable to pay VAT. Without
such a reference on the invoice, it is considered that the middleman has not opted for triangular simplification
and the recipient of the supply is not determined as the person liable to pay VAT. This reference on the invoice
therefore represents a substantive, not merely formal condition. The FA also wrote that the mentioned rules
(obligatory statement “reverse charge”) apply for the transactions, performed after their explanation was
published (March 20, 2024).

‘The triangulation simplification should be accepted even if the middleman is already VAT registered in
Slovenia as country of destination.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

‘The threshold for application of the special scheme for acquisitions of goods by taxable persons not entitled
to deduct input tax and by non-taxable legal persons is EUR 10,000 for Slovenia.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Slovenia has not implemented the use and enjoyment rules as provided in Article 59a of the BU VAT
Directive.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied. For intracommunity supplies the VAT is due on the day the invoice is issued. If no
invoice is issued or the invoice is issued too late, the VAT is due on the 15* of the month following the month
in which the goods are supplied. This also applies for intra-Community acquisitions.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed, This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put

at the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied. The VAT is due when the advance payment is
received, The VAT does not become due at the time of receipt of the advance payment, when the payment is
received for an intra-Community supply of goods or an intra-Community acquisition.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Supplies of services for which VAT is payable by the customer pursuant (o Article 196 of the EU VAT
Directive and which are supplied continuously over a period of more than one year and which do not give rise
to statements of account or payments during that period, shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of
each calendar year, until the supply of those services comes to an end.

4.4 Cash accounting

In certain cases, it is allowed to pay VAT to the Slovenian state on the basis of cash received during the
respective VAT period as opposed to making payment on the basis of supplied goods and services during the
period. The cash accounting regime is available to taxable persons whose taxable turnover did not exceed
EUR 400,000 in the previous 12 months nor is expected to exceed this amount in the upcoming 12 months.

Slovenia | 559

--- Page 60 ---
5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

According to Art 90 of EU VAT Directive or Art 39 of the VAT Act, if the order is cancelled, refunded or
reduced in price after the supply has been made, the taxable amount is reduced accordingly. The taxable
person may correct (reduce) the amount of VAT charged if he informs the customer in writing of the amount
of VAT to which the customer is not entitled to deduct.

‘The taxable person may also adjust (reduce) the amount of VAT charged if, on the basis of a final court order,
the amount of VAT has not been repaid or has not been repaid in full on the basis of a final court order that
the bankruptcy proceedings have been closed or that the compulsory administration proceedings have been
successfully closed. ‘The same shall apply to a taxable person who obtains a final court order suspending
enforcement proceedings or other document showing that he has not been repaid or has not been repaid in
full in the completed enforcement proceedings, and to a taxable person who has not been repaid or has not
been repaid in full because the debtor has been removed from the court register or other relevant registers or
statutory records.

Where a taxable person subsequently receives payment or partial payment for a supply of goods or services
in respect of which he has claimed an adjustment of the taxable amount, he shall be liable to pay VAT on the
amount received.

Notwithstanding the above, the taxable person may adjust (reduce) the amount of VAT charged and unpaid on
any recognised claims which he has declared in compulsory administration or bankruptcy proceedings.

If the taxable person subsequently receives payment or partial payment for a supply of goods or services in
respect of which he has claimed an adjustment of the taxable amount, he shall account for VAT on the amount
received.

5.2 Cash discounts

‘Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
22% VAT EUR 220
Total to be paid BUR 1,220

If payment is received by us within the next [4 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”.

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. There are no special rules or guidelines regarding cash discounts.
Neither is there any established trade practice (usage) in that regard.

However, if the contract or a subsequent alteration of it, provides for a price reduction the general rule in
Art. 39/2 of the VAT Act shall apply. According to this rule the supplier can only reduce the output VAT upon
informing the customer that he should reduce the VAT deducted.

5.3 Open Market Value

In Slovenia, the option for Member States to use the ‘open market value’ as the taxable amount for specific
transactions is implemented in case of related parties as stipulated in Art 94(5) of VAT Act. Related parties are
persons who are defined as such in corporate and personal income tax legislation.

In line with Article 80 of the EU VAT Directive, Slovenia has implemented the following transactions for

which ‘the open market value’ as the taxable amount must apply (Art 36.a of VAT Act):

+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the recipient of the supply does not have
a full right of deduction;

560 | Slovenia

--- Page 61 ---
+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction and the supply is subject to an exemption; and

+ where the consideration is higher than the open market value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction,

Open market value shall mean the total amount which the buyer or supplier should pay to the goods or service
supplier on the home territory at the time of the supply of goods or services under the principle of non-
disruption of market competition. If a comparable supply of goods or services cannot be determined, the open
market vatue shail refer to:

+ for goods, the amount which is not lower than the purchase price of those or similar goods or if that price
is unknown, the total amount of determined costs at the moment of supply; and

+ for services, the amount which is not lower than the total determined costs of supply of services borne by
the taxable person.

54 Returnable packaging

According to the Rules in the VAT Act, Art 7, the exchange of returnable packaging is not considered a supply
of goods if the supplier of the goods in returnable packaging records the exchange of full packaging for
empty packaging only for evidential purposes, in accordance with the customs of returning and taking back
returnable packaging.

6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate in Slovenia is 22%.

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced VAT rate in Slovenia is 9.5% and applies, for example, to:

* the supply of food and beverages (except alcoholic beverages and beverages with added sugar, sweeteners
or flavourings);

«hotel accommodation;

* passenger transport;

+ medicines;

+ admission fees for exhibitions, theatres, museums, visits to natural sites of interest, cinematographic
and musical performances, circuses, fairs, amusement parks, zoos and similar cultural performances and
sporting events;

+ use of sporting facilities; and

* certain labour intensive services: hairdressers, renovation and repair of private apartments and houses,
window cleaning and cleaning in private households, minor repairing of bicycles, shoes and leather
goods, clothing and household linen.

63 Super-reduced rate

‘The 5% special reduced VAT rate applies to the supply (including on loan by libraries) of books, newspapers
and periodicals either on physical means of support or supplied electronically or both (including brochures,
leaflets and similar printed matter, children’s picture, drawing or colouring books, music printed or in
manuscript form, maps and hydrographic or similar charts), other than publications wholly or predominantly
devoted to advertising and other than publications wholly or predominantly consisting of video content or
audible music.

Slovenia | 561

--- Page 62 ---
7. Exemptions

1 Exemptions with the right to deduction (zero rate)

The following transactions are zero-rated in Slovenia:

+ intra~Community supplies of goods;

* transport services to and from the Azores and Madeira;

+ international transport of passengers (except international road transport);

+ export of goods and providing services directly connected with the export;

+ services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of principals resident outside the BU;

+ supply of goods and services under diplomatic and consular arrangements;

+ _ services of intermediaries if part of export transactions or transactions carried out outside the EU;

+ supply of goods to passengers residing outside the FU (as VAT refund);

+ supply, chartering and repair of vessels and aircraft, provisioning and performance of services to meet the
direct needs of vessels and aircraft;

+ supply of gold to central banks;

+ supply of goods under customs conirol and services performed on such goods; and

+ supply of goods in tax warehouses and services performed on such goods.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

Intra-Community supplies are VAT exempt, if the conditions as laid down in EU VAT Directive are met. To
prove the transport, a taxable person may, besides the proofs mentioned in the Implementing Regulation
EU no. 282/201, provide also the following: invoice and other available evidence (e.g. proof of payment,
purchase order, concluded supply contract, delivery note, packing list, acceptance certificate, etc.).

For the transfers of own goods also an invoice should be issued and reported accordingly,

Furthermore, the intra-Community supply must be declared in the monthly recapitulative statements,

7.1.2 Export supplies
‘The following export supplies are VAT exempt:

+ the supply of goods dispatched or transported from Stovenia to a destination outside the EU by or on
behalf of the supplier; and

+ the supply of goods dispatched or transported from Slovenia to a destination outside the EU by or on
behalf of a customer not established in Slovenia.

The taxable person proves export has occurred with a customs declaration: a received electronic message
“export notification”, which can also be replaced by a certificate of exit issued by the competent export office
in accordance with customs regulations.

In the case where the goods are dispatched from Slovenia by the customer, in accordance with the FA
explanation, any means of proof, such as documents showing that the goods are intended for export, are
sufficient to prove export delivery.

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction

Examples of exemptions without the right to deduct are financial and banking services, the supply of
investment gold, insurance services, postal and telecommunication services, leasing or letting of immovable
property for business purposes, the supply of immovable property and services of small businesses.
Regarding the possibility to opt for taxation please refer to next paragraph.

7.2.1 Option for taxation

‘There is an option for taxation in respect of property-related transactions:
+ the leasing or letting of immovable property;
+ the supply of buildings or parts thereof, and of the land on which they stand unless the supply is

562 | Slovenia

--- Page 63 ---
performed before the buildings or parts thereof are first occupied or used or prior to the expiration of the
2-year period following the commencement of the first use or occupation; and
+ the supply of land other than building land.

In order to be able to opt for taxation, both parties have to conclude a written agreement about that before the
supply is performed. It is not possible to opt to tax financial services.

7.3 Warehousing

7.3.1 VAT warehouse

In Slovenia, only goods which are specially determined in the VAT Act can be stored in tax warehouses, The

term “tax warehouse” means (Art 58 of VAT Act):

+ in case of mineral oils: a place defined as an excise warehouse according to the Excise Duty Act; and

+ in case of other goods: a place defined as a tax warehouse according to Article 86 of the Rules on the
Implementation of the VAT Act: one or more related premises or spaces forming a technological unit,
visibly marked and physically separated from other premises or spaces.

Generally, VAT is charged upon the importation, intra-Community acquisition or the supply of goods within
Slovenia. However, this can be avoided or deferred by placing goods under a tax warehouse arrangement in
Slovenia.

Such VAT exemption will also apply to the supply of goods within the tax warehouse as well as the services
in connection with (the supply of) those goods. Upon the release of the goods from a tax warehouse VAT must
be charged unless the transaction is exempt. The taxable amount corresponds to the value of the goods upon
the last transaction plus the value of the services in relation to these goods.

According to Article 76(1)7 of the VAT Act, VAT will be payable by the person releasing the goods from a tax
warehouse,

7.3.2 Bonded (Customs) warehouse
According to Article 57(1) of the VAT Act, non-EU goods upon importation and certain EU goods about to
be exported can be put in a Customs warehouse or a free zone. Therefore, VAT can be avoided or at least
deferred for the duration of the storage of the goods under these arrangements. Services in connection with
these goods are also exempt if they are performed within the warehouse.

According to Article 59 of the VAT Act, tax warehouses are approved by the customs authority which is
also in charge of control over them. There is, therefore, in fact no particular difference regarding the regime
of tax warehouses and customs warehouses in Slovenia, since both types of warehouses are approved and
supervised by the Customs authority and must meet the same conditions with regard to security.

VAT will be payable by the person releasing the goods from a warehouse into free circutation, while the rules
extend this liability also to the holder of a Customs warehouse in case of breach of his obligations.

8. Liability

8&1 Reverse Charges

In Slovenia there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by non-resident suppliers to taxable persons and legal entities with a Slovenian VAT ID
number established in Slovenia.

Once the reverse charge is applicable, a reference on the invoice must be stated (“reverse charge” or
“obrnjena davéna obveznost”)

Slovenia | 563

--- Page 64 ---
8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

The reverse charge applies if:

+ the non-resident supplier is not registered for VAT in Slovenia but the customer is a taxable person
registered for VAT purposes in Slovenia, Customer does not need to be established in Slovenia but must
be VAT registered; and

+ _ if the non-resident supplier is registered for VAT purposes in Slovenia, the reverse charge does not apply.

This applies for both the supply of goods and services (such as the admission to an event held in Slovenia for
taxable persons).

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

‘The following supplies performed by a taxable person registered for VAT in Slovenia to a taxable person also

registered for VAT in Slovenia are subject to the reverse charge:

+ construction services (including repair, cleaning, maintenance, reconstruction, demolition in connection
with immovable property) as well as supply of personnel involved in construction services;

+ supply of immovable property if opted for taxation;

+ — supply of metal waste, scrap, used material and connected services; and

+ transfer of greenhouse gas emission rights (valid until 31 December 2026)

‘The tax authorities have issued several official notices regarding the above-mentioned domestic rules.

For example, the notices regarding the definition of construction services, specifying that there is a single
non-separable transaction when other services are a necessary part of a particular transaction involving
construction services and regarding the timing of the taxable event in the case of construction services which
are confirmed after they have been performed.

As mentioned, the domestic reverse charge applies only in cases where both recipient and the supplier are
taxable persons registered for VAT purposes in Slovenia.

‘The supplier must also show on the invoice the (informative) VAT amount and the statement that this VAT is
the liability of the customer according to Article 76.a of Slovenian VAT Act. In case of construction services,
Article 138 of the Rules provides that the invoice must also state and identify the immovable property which
these services refer to.

8.2 Joint and several liability

According to the VAT Act, Art 76.b, every taxable person identified for VAT purposes in Slovenia is jointly
and severally liable for the payment of VAT if it follows from objective circumstances that he knew or should
have known that he was participating in transactions the purpose of which is to avoid the payment of VAT.

If the tax authority establishes that the supply of goods or services is an integral part of transactions the
purpose of which is to avoid the payment of VAT, it shall inform the taxable person participating in these
transactions of the existence of these circumstances and of his joint and several liability for the payment
of VAT if he continues to participate in such transactions, From the date of receipt of the notification from
the tax authority, the taxable person shall be deemed to have known that by making the purchase he was
participating in transactions the purpose of which is to avoid the payment of VAT.

There is a joint and several liability for VAT grouping, as according to the provisions, members of a VAT
group exercise their rights and obligations as a single taxable person.

Slovenia has not implemented joint and several liability for e.g, managing directors or board members

9. Recovery of input VAT

o1 Non-recoverable input VAT

Taxable persons are not allowed to deduct input VAT:

* on yachts and boats intended for sport and recreation, on aircraft and on passenger cars and motorcycles,
also on fuels, lubricants, spare parts and services which are closely linked thereto (except when intended
for passenger transport, leasing, renting and resale, taxi, drivers’ training programs, etc., and except
passenger cars with 0 g/km emission and a value not exceeding EUR 80,000 including VAT and other
duties);

+ onentertainment expenses where entertainment expenses include only the costs of entertainment and
amusement during business or social contacts;

+ on meal expenses (including drinks); and

= accommodation expenses.

92 Excess input VAT

In case a taxable person has excess input VAT in a certain tax period, he has two possibilities:

* to request a refund where the excess input VAT is refunded within 21 days after the VAT return in which
the request was made and was filed to the tax authorities; and

+ to transfer it to the next tax period and to use it for covering the VAT liability in future tax period(s).

93 Adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 20 years following the tax period of first use
+ movable property - 5 years following the tax period of first use

‘The tax period of first use means the tax period following the tax period in which the input VAT was or was
not deducted.

There is no adjustment period for services.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

A taxable person established in another EU Member State can claim a VAT refund in Slovenia by submitting a
VAT refund request through the electronic portal in the EU Member State which issued the VAT identification
number or the tax reference number under which the taxable person requests a refund.

The annual minimum limits for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year
is EUR 50. The interim minimum limit for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3
months is EUR 400.

‘The minimum period for making a claim is 3 months. Maximum period for making such a claim is one year.

‘The deadline for making the claim is 30 September following the expiration of the calendar year in which
VAT is charged. It is not possible to receive a refund if this deadline is breached.

No supporting documents are required upon lodging a claim. Nevertheless, if the tax authority considers
that there is insufficient information available to decide on the request for refund, it may, within 4 months
of receipt of the request, require the applicant or the competent authority of the EU Member State of
establishment to provide additional information in electronic form. If the tax authority still has reasonable
doubts about the validity and regularity of a particular request for refund, it may request the applicant to
submit the original or a copy of invoice or import document. The applicant must provide the tax authority
with additional information within one month of the receipt of the tax authority’s request.

‘The tax authorities are obliged to notify the applicant immediately of the date of the receipt of the request and
decide on the request within 4 months of that date. In case additional information or documents are required,

--- Page 66 ---
the tax authority is required to decide on the request within 2 months of the date of the receipt of the required
additional information or within 2 months from the date of reccipt of the request for additional information if
the latter remains unanswered,

As for appeals, the general 15-day time limit applies.

If the tax authority grants the request for a refund, the latter will be made within 10 business days after the
expiration of the time limit for decision.

When a VAT refund was obtained by fraud or in any other incorrect manner, the tax authorities will proceed
with the procedure for recovery of unpaid amounts, including any financial penalties, fines or default interest
charges.

A taxable person established in a non-EU counity files the claim in electronic form through the portal eDavki
using an application form DDV-VTD with prescribed codes for particular goods and services.

The annual minimum limits for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year
is BUR 50. The interim minimum limit for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 6
months is EUR 400.

The minimum period for making a claim is 6 months. Maximum period for making a claim is one year.

The deadline for making the claim is 30 June following the expiration of the calendar year in which the VAT
is charged. The applicant has no recourse once the said time limit is breached

‘The following documents must be submitted with the application:

the general 15-day time limit applies.

decision.

10. | Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice needs to include the following compulsory content (Article 82 of the VAT Act):

+ date when the invoice was issued;

+ invoice number (a sequential number);

+ VAT identification number under which the supply was performed;

+ customer's VAT identification number under which the customer received a supply of goods or services
in respect of which he is liable for payment of VAT, or to which an intra-Community supply of goods was
performed;

+ supplier’s and customer’s name and address;

+ quantity and nature of the goods supplied or extent and nature of the services performed;

+ date on which the supply of goods or services was performed or the date on which the advance payment
was received;

+ taxable amount per VAT rate or amount to which the exemption applies, the unit price (excluding VAT)
and discounts, if not included in the unit price;

* VAT rate;

+ VAT amount;

+ incase of an exemption or where the customer is liable to pay VAT, reference to the provision of the EU
VAT Directive or to the corresponding Article of the VAT Act;

+ incase the reverse charge mechanism is applied the clause “reverse charge” must be included;

+ incase of self-billing a corresponding reference “samofakturiranje” is required;

+ incase of the supply of a new vehicle, the vehicle’s characteristics according to Article 3 of the VAT Act:

+ where the margin scheme for travel agents is applied, the clause “Special Rules — Travel Agencies”,
“posebna ureditev za potovalne agencije” will be included;

* where one of the special arrangements applicable to second-hand goods, works of art, collectors’ items
and antiques is applied, the corresponding clauses “Special Rules — Second-Hand Goods” “posebna
ureditev za rabljeno blago, umetniske predmete, zbirke in starine”, “Special Rules — Works of Art” or
“Special Rules ~ Collector’s Items and Antiques” will be included; and

* name, address and VAT ID number of the fiscal representative, if appointed.

Slovenia | 567

--- Page 68 ---
Additional requirements have been added by the Act on Fiscal Verification of Invoices for transactions

involving cash payments. These further invoice-elements include:

+ exact time of issuing the invoice (hour and minutes);

+ mark of the individual who issues the invoice with the use of the electronic device for issuing invoices;

+ unique identification invoice mark (a code sent by the Tax Authority’s server via the online connection -
the so-called EOR code); and

+ protective mark of the invoice issuer (produced by the seller’s cash register ~ the so-called ZOI code).

As already mentioned, these requirements will apply only to traders located in the Republic of Slovenia who
enter cash transactions. Distance sales from abroad even though there might be a cash payment involved, are
excluded from the scope of this act.

10.1.2 Time limits

In case of intra-Community supplies of goods an invoice must be issued no later than on the 15 day of the
month following the month in which the goods were delivered. Otherwise, there are no time limits for issuing
invoices, However, il is important that the VAT is paid (and invoice reported) in the correct tax period. If the
chargeable event (supply of goods or services or execution of payment) precedes the issuance of the invoice,
it must be included in the tax return for the period in which the chargeable event took place.

10.1.3 Currency

There are no prohibitions of using a foreign currency on an invoice. However, the amount of VAT must be
stated in BUR. Please see below regarding the exchange rate which must be used for the conversion of the
VAT amount from other currencies to EUR.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
‘The exchange rate applicable is the foreign exchange reference rate of the European Central Bank valid on
the day when the tax liability arises and is published by the Bank of Slovenia. According to the VAT Act, the
Minister of Finance may lay down simplifications regarding this conversion which has been done by Art. 44
of Rules on the Implementation of VAT Act.

In case of the option in Art, 91 of the EU VAT Directive, it is the monthly exchange rate published on the
penultimate Wednesday before the month when the supply of goods or services is made, which will be
applied, unless this rate is subsequently altered by more than 1%. However, if this is the case, the taxpayer
uses the reference exchange rate of the European Central Bank, which is published by the Bank of Slovenia
on the Wednesday before the week in which the taxpayer determined that the conditions for changing the
exchange rate have been met,

No notification is required. However, a taxable person is obliged to present all necessary facts about exchange
rates applied if requested by the tax authorities.

10.1.5 Self billing
In Slovenia, self-billing is allowed under the condition that the supplier and the customer sign a written

agreement. They also have to determine the procedure for accepting each invoice. At the request of the tax
authority, they must prove the existence of such agreement.

10.1.6 Simplified invoices

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if the net amount payable does not exceed EUR 100 (Art. 83 the
VAT Act).

Such invoice shall then contain the following items:

+ the date of the invoice;

+ its serial number;

+ the name, address and VAT number of the issuer of the invoice;

+ quantity and nature of the goods supplied or extent and nature of the services performed;
+ the amount of VAT payable or the necessary information for its calculation; and

+ incase of cancellation, the reference to the corresponding document (invoice).

568 | Slovenia

--- Page 69 ---
10.1.7 Storage of invoices

‘Taxable persons registered for VAT in Slovenia are obliged to store invoices for 10 years. Invoices relating
to immovable property must be stored for at least 20 years. In case invoices are stored electronically, the tax
authorities must be provided with guaranteed online access for VAT compliance purposes

Invoices may be issued electronically if a customer agrees with that and if the authenticity of the origin and
the integrity of the content are guaranteed. Electronic invoices are usually issued in accordance with the
regulations governing electronic transactions (Electronic Commerce and Electronic Signature Act). However,
other forms of e-invoicing are also possible according to the Council Directive 2010/45/ EU and Explanatory
notes as long as the above-mentioned basic requirements regarding authenticity and integrity are respected
(via e-mail, PDF file, structured messages, scanned paper invoices, etc.).

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

‘The standard VAT return period (tax period) for VAT purposes in Slovenia is one calendar month, If the
taxable turnover in the last calendar year did not exceed EUR 210,000, the tax period is a quarter, except in
case the taxable person performs intra-Community supplies of goods and services for which he is obliged to
file European sales listings. If performing intra-Community supplies of goods and services, the taxable person
must file monthly VAT returns for the months in which such supplies take place or for which adjustments for
previous supplies must be made.

Foreign taxable persons registered for VAT in Slovenia need to file a monthly VAT return regardless of the
value of transactions performed under the Slovenian VAT number, For a taxable person for whom compulsory
settlement, ending of business or bankruptcy proceedings are initiated, the tax period will end on the day prior
to the commencement of compulsory settlement, ending of business or bankruptcy proceedings.

However, with respect to international passenger transport services by foreign-based carriers which are not
performed on a regular basis, there is an exception which allows foreign-based carriers to submit VAT returns
on an annual basis (further information in this regard: hitps://tinyurl.com/3cz882am ).

If there is nothing to report, still the nil VAT return has to be submitted.

VAT returns can be filed only electronically via eTaxes portal. In Slovenia, no annual VAT returns need to be
filed.

10.2.2. Filing of a VAT return and payment
Slovenian tax authorities must receive the VAT due on a certain bank account on the last working day of the
month following the tax period at the latest. The same deadline is determined for filing VAT returns, except
when a taxable person performs intra-Community supplies of goods and services.

In case of intra-Community supplies of goods and services, the VAT return must be filed by the 20% day of
the month following the tax period. The deadline to pay the VAT in this case remains the same -by t he last
working day of the month following a tax period.

10.2.3. European sales listing

The European sales listing must be filed on a monthly basis, if a taxable person performs intra-Community
supplies of goods or services which must be reported in the European sales listing. The European sales listing
must be filed by the 20" day following the tax period. The European sales listing must be filed regardless of
the value of these supplies. Intra~-Community supplies of goods and services are reported on the same form at
the same time.

European sales listing can only be filed electronically.

Slovenia | 569

--- Page 70 ---
10.2.4 National recapitulative statement
There are no national recapitulative statements in Slovenia.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

‘The tax assessment period in Slovenia is 5 years. This means that in the year 2025, the last open year for
assessments is the year 2020.

10.3 Penalties

‘The system of penalties under the VAT Act has been substantially modified by the amendments from
4 October 2019. These included the amended array of penalties in Art. 140, 141 and 142, some new
incriminations and introduced liability for individuals not being taxable persons or representatives thereof.

In case of infringements, taxpayers can be held responsible for various (administrative) offenses as well as,
in case of late payment, be liable to pay default interest according to a rate prescribed by The Act on the
Prescribed Interest Rate for Default Interest (leading rate plus 8 percentage points). Default interest rate
payment is not levied upon a taxpayer by an administrative decision + he taxpayer is obliged to compute this
rate when performing late payments.

As for the tax offenses, these are classified into simple offenses with relatively minor penalties (intent or
gross negligence is required) on one hand and scrious tax offenses for serious breaches with heavier penalties
where even minor negligence suffices.

A penalty can be levied for a simple offense in an array from EUR 1,200 to EUR 15,000 for taxable persons
that are defined as small businesses according to the Companies Act. In case of medium and large enterprises
the array starts at EUR 3,200 up to EUR 30,000. For sole traders penalties between EUR 800 and EUR
10,000 and for responsible individual acting for legal persons and sole traders an array of penalties from EUR
600 to EUR 4,000 has been prescribed. These amendments even introduced the responsibility of individuals
not being taxable persons, sole traders or their representatives with penallies in the array between EUR 200 to
EUR 1,200.

For heavy offenses defined by Art. 141 of the VAT Act these arrays prescribed are from EUR 4,000 to EUR
75,000 (medium and large companies as taxable persons), from EUR 3,000 to EUR 50,000 (sole traders as
taxable persons), from EUR 1,000 to EUR 10,000 (responsible individuals of medium and large companies),
from EUR 800 to EUR 10,000 (responsible person for sole traders) and finally from EUR 400 to EUR 5,000
for (ordinary) individuals.

The last modification even introduced another category of offences — particularly heavy offences. The
definitions are the same as in Art. 141 (heavy offences). What makes them particularly heavy is either the
heavy consequence of the offence or the particularly heavy culpability of the offender (dolus directus or even
dolus coloratus). The penalty arrays are therefore correspondingly more extensive ranging from EUR 10,500
to EUR 150,000 (medium and large companies as taxable persons), from EUR 4,500 to EUR 100,000 (small
companies), from EUR 3,500 to EUR 75,000 (sole traders as taxable persons) and from EUR 2,500 to EUR
15,000 (individuals). Responsible individuals (representatives) shall be levied in the array from EUR 1,000
to EUR 20,000 (representatives of sole traders) and from EUR 1,200 to EUR 20,000 (responsible person of a
legal entity).

However, in practice the tax administration tends to be rather lenient towards taxpayers so that in case

ofa late tax return it is more likely that a taxpayer would receive a kind reminder to voluntarily fulfil his
obligations instead of opening an administrative penalty procedure. So far, the courts have not asked the ECJ
for a preliminary ruling regarding proportionality of these penalties, although there is at least one pending
case where the taxpayer has demanded such a ruling,

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

Art. 141 of the Act prescribes for such a serious tax offense a penalty from EUR 4,000 to EUR 75,000 or
even EUR 10,000 to EUR 125,000 (medium and large companies) if'a taxable person fails to comply with the

570 | Slovenia

--- Page 71 ---
obligation to notify the tax authority of the commencement, change of termination of its activity as a taxable
person or fails to submit to the tax authority a request for issuance of its identification number for VAT
purposes or fails to submit it within the specific time limit or in the prescribed manner.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

Not filing a VAT return on time is considered a serious tax offense for which the following penalties are

determined (Article 141 of the VAT Act):

* from EUR 4,000 to EUR 75,000 or even EUR 10,000 to EUR 125,000 (large and medium companies) for
the taxable person;

+ — from EUR 3,000 to EUR 50,000 (sole traders); and

* from EUR 1,000 to EUR 10,000 for the responsible person of the taxable person.

In relation to the European sales listing, it is considered a serious tax offense if the taxable person [ails to
submit the European sales listing within the specified time limit or if it fails to provide the required data in the
European sales listing. The penalties determined for such offenses are the same as previously mentioned.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If the VAT is not paid on time, the (administrative) penalties are the same as for not filing the VAT return

on time (for the taxable person and for the responsible person of the taxable person). Also, there is a default
interest to be paid which the taxpayer is obliged to compute by himself (in practice the sum can easily be
verified via the eDavki/eTaxes platform).

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If the VAT is not charged or it is charged incorrectly, tax authorities consider this as tax offense for which the

following penalties are prescribed:

+ from EUR 1,200 to EUR 15,000 for the taxable person or EUR 3,200 to EUR 30,000 (large and medium
companies); and

+ from EUR 400 to EUR 4,000 for the responsible person of the taxable person,

‘Taxable persons have the option to correct the VAT voluntarily. The mistake can be corrected in the current
VAT return. Also in this case the interest determined for self-assessment which is related to the 12 month
EURIBOR and to the period of delay with the VAT payment, must be paid before the expiry of the deadline
for VAT payment based on the VAT return.

In case of voluntary correction, the tax authorities cannot punish the taxable person with the penalty. In cases

where the offenses are of a particularly serious nature due to:

+ the amount of damage caused;

+ the amount of acquired illegal proceeds; or

+ because of the offender’s intent or intention of unlawful gain, a penalty of up to EUR 75,000 (sole traders),
EUR 100,000 (companies) or even up to EUR 150,000 (large and medium companies) will be assessed to
the taxable person and a penalty of up to EUR 20,000 for the responsible person of the taxable person.

According to the Act on Fiscal Verification of Invoices additional penalties up to EUR 125,000 are prescribed
for various infringements of that Act.

10.4 Intrastat

A taxable person performing intra-Community supplies and acquisitions is obliged to file Intrastat returns
to the Intrastat Department within the Customs Office of Nova Gorica (which is authorized for Intrastat
issues by the Statistical office of Republic of Slovenia). The Intrastat returns are required only when certain
thresholds are exceeded.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

In Slovenia there are two kinds of Intrastat thresholds:

* exemption thresholds: EUR 270,000 for dispatches and EUR 240,000 for arrivals; and

* statistical value thresholds (for individual flow of the goods): EUR 9,000,000 for dispatches and EUR
4,000,000 for arrivals.

Slovenia | 571

--- Page 72 ---
10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

In relation to the exemption threshold the following data must be reported in the Intrastat returns:

» description of the goods;

+ EU Member State of consignment/destination;

+ VAT ID number of the partner (only for dispatches),

+ nature of the transaction;

+ &-digit commodity code of the goods;

+ country of origin;

* net weight of the goods;

* quantity for supplementary units (for some commodity codes, supplementary units are prescribed); and
+ invoiced amount.

If statistical value thresholds are exceeded in addition to mandatory data, also the data on delivery terms,
mode of transport and statistical value must be reported.

For more information we refer to the Intrastat Guide for 2025 published by the Statistical Office of the
Republic of Slovenia (Statistitni urad Republike Slovenije, SURS), available at hitps://tinyurl.com/mt8xw3n9

The publication is updated every yeat.

Spain

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Spain through the “Ley 37/1992, de 28 de
diciembre, del Impuesto sobre el Valor Afiadido” (hereafter referred to as the “VAT Act” or “VAT Law”). In
Spain, VAT is known as ““Impuesto sobre el Valor Afiadido” or “IVA.” An updated, consolidated version of
this law can be downloaded from the Official Gazette website (hitps://tinyurl.com/ye2aej52).

Taxes and customs duties in Spain are managed by a specialized governmental body, the “Agencia Estatal
de Administracién Tributaria” (hereinafter, “AEAT” or “Spanish tax authorities”). Their website provides
comprehensive information on the Spanish tax system and offers a “virtual office” where most tax-telated
procedures can be completed electronically (https://tinyurl.com/2bawp4sp).

The website also features the following VAT Virtual Assistance Tools, powered by AI:
+ VAT Virtual Assistant

+ SII Virtual Assistant

+ Goods Provision Locator

+ Service Provision Locator

+ Property Grader to determine VAT or Transfer Tax (ITP) application in immovable property transactions
+ Deadline Calculator for Amending Invoices

+ Apportionment Calculator (pro rata rule - partial deduction scheme)

+ Differentiated Sectors Calculator

* Assistance for Completing Quarterly/Monthly VAT Form 303 (for lessors)

+ Assistance for Completing Annual VAT Form 390
