1, Taxable persons

1 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

General registration

Domestic taxable persons are required to register with the tax authorities and obtain a Spanish tax

identification number (known as “NIF”) under the following conditions:

«they carry out any transaction subject to VAT within the Spanish VAT territory;

+ Spanish VAT is due (even if the transaction qualifies as an exempt supply); and

+ they are designated as the taxable entity responsible for liquidating (settlement of VAT under a specific
Spanish regime), according to Spanish VAT regulations. This excludes cases where the reverse charge
mechanism applies, in which case the supplier of goods or services would not be responsible for VAT
liquidation.

There are no specific simplification measures for VAT registration in Spain, meaning there is no minimum
threshold for domestic companies or entrepreneurs. If the above conditions are met, they are obligated to
register.

The tax registration must be done through the submission of a census form (Form 036) and supporting
documents to the Spanish tax authorities.

Electronic notifications

Taxable persons are required to receive most communications and notifications from the Spanish tax
authorities electronically. To facilitate this, the Spanish tax authorities automatically assign a special

Spain | 573

--- Page 74 ---
electronic address, known as the “direccién electronica habilitada tinica” (DEHU) to each taxable person
under the relevant regulations.

Accessing this special electronic address requires the taxable person to hold an electronic certificate
recognized by AEAT (see paragraph 10.2, filing). Taxable persons without such a certificate may authorize

a third party holding the certificate to access the electronic address on their behalf. Except when a power of
attorney is granted directly before the Spanish tax authorities, this authorization must be formalized through a
special power of attorney deed executed before a public notary.

In case of being formalized in another country, the documentation must be legalized by the foreign public
notary and, when appropriate, stamped with an Apostille issued under the Hague Convention and must be
registered in a special register maintained by AEAT.

Special registrations

Spanish VAT number

‘When a taxable person plans to carry out intra~Community transactions, he must obtain a Spanish VAT
number (referred to as “NIF-IVA”) to be able to carry this type of transactions.

The Spanish VAT number consists of the prefix “ES” followed by the Spanish ID number (NIF).

This number is automatically issued through registration in the “Spanish Register of Intra~Community
Operators” (in Spanish, “ROI”). The application for ROI registration must be done through the submission.
ofa census form (Form 036) to the Spanish tax authorities, the same census form as for the NIF application.
After the submission of the ROI application, the Spanish tax authorities usually start a limited audit to check
the reason of the application and whether the taxable person has already carried out or has the intention to
perform intra-Community transactions.

For such purposes, the taxable person must be in a position to provide the Spanish tax authorities with
documentary evidence of the completion or the intention to carry out intra~Communily transactions (c.g...
purchase invoice, transport document, invoice for transport services, communications with EU providers-
customers, etc.).

Once the registration is approved, the taxable person is fisted in the VAT Information Exchange System
(VIES) database as holding a Spanish VAT number.

Until this point, they will not be considered to have a Spanish VAT number. The ROI registration is
retroactively effective from the date of the first intra‘Community transaction, meaning that the VAT numbers
in Spain can be assigned after such type of transactions have begun.

Monthly refund regime (“REDEME”)

This optional regime allows taxable persons to request a refund of VAT receivable on a monthly basis, rather
than waiting until the final VAT return of the year, as required under the general VAT regime. To benefit from
this, registration in the “Register of Monthly Refunds” (hereinafter, “REDEME”) through the submission of a
census form (Form 036) to the Spanish tax authorities is necessary,

Although this regime is generally mandatory for large companies with an annual turnover exceeding EUR
6,010,121.04, businesses of any size may voluntarily opt to register in the REDEME.

As a general rule, the application for inclusion in the REDEME must be submitted within November of the
previous calendar year to that in which it is to take effect.

Alternatively, it may also be requested during the period for submitting the quarterly VAT returns. In this case,
the inclusion in the REDEME will take effect from the day following the end of the period of payment of the
corresponding quarterly VAT retum, triggering the obligation to submit VAT returns on a monthly basis to the
Spanish tax authorities.

574 | Spain

--- Page 75 ---
After the REDEME application is submitted, the Spanish tax authorities usually start a limited audit to verify
the reason of the application.

‘The REDEME registration implies:

+ The obligation to file monthly VAT returns and the possibility to request the refund on a monthly basis;

+ The obligation to comply with the Immediate Supply of Information System (“SII”), which entails
reporting to the Spanish tax authorities the received and issued invoices almost in real-time (see
paragraph 10.5); and

+ The possibility of applying for a special deferral regime for import VAT payments.

Therefore, taxable persons who are often in a VAT-credit position, as well as companies that import goods
into Spain but are not required to file monthly VAT returns due to their size, may benefit from registering in
the REDEME and opting for the voluntary monthly refund regime.

Special cash accounting regime

Taxable persons who meet the eligibility criteria for this regime and wish to opt-in must notify the Spanish
tax authorities by submitting the appropriate registration census form (Form 036) in December of the year
preceding the year in which they want to apply the regime.

Special VAT grouping regime (REGE)

A specific application must be submitted to the census to opt into the special VAT grouping regime, which has
two possible scopes (see also paragraph 1.3).

Special E-commerce registration (OSS)

If Spain is the Member State of registration, a special application must be made to the census to benefit from
any of the special e-commerce OSS regimes.

Economic Operators Registration and Identification (EORD

Taxable persons intending to perform imports or exports in the Spanish VAT territory must provide the
special identification number issued by the customs authorities of any EU Member State, known as the EORI
number.

For EU-resident taxable persons, the EORI number is granted by the customs authorities of their country of
residence. Non-EU taxable persons can apply for an EORI number in the EU Member State where they intend
to import or export goods.

A taxable person who already holds an EORI number and intends to perform imports or exports in Spain
must first fink their EORI number to a Spanish NIF. If they do not already have a Spanish NIF, they will be
required to register and obtain one.

SME scheme

By | January 2025 Spain has not regulated the new special scheme for small enterprises (SME) special
regime contemplated in the Council Directive (EU) 2020/285 of 18 February 2020.

1.2 Registration threshold & SME scheme for foreign taxable persons

As with domestic companies and entrepreneurs, there are no specific simplification measures for non-

residents regarding their obligation to register with the census bureau and obtain an internal tax identification

number (Spanish NIF) in the following cases:

+ they carry out any transaction within the Spanish VAT territory for VAT purposes:

* Spanish VAT is due (even if the transaction is an exempt supply);

* according to Spanish VAT regulations, they are the designated taxable entity responsible for VAT liquidation,
as would be the case for the supplier of goods or services if the reverse charge mechanism applies.

Spain | 575

--- Page 76 ---
‘The requirements for special registrations, whether optional or mandatory, outlined in paragraph 1.1, also
apply. For example, non-resident taxable persons must register for a Spanish VAT number if engaging in
intra-Community operations, or apply for the special e-commerce OSS regime if Spain is the chosen Member
State for registration.

Registration must be completed through a duly empowered representative authorized to act on behalf of

the company before the Spanish tax authorities, Non-EU taxable persons are required to appoint a local tax
representative. Additionally, non-EU companies seeking to register in Spain to benefit from the special Import
OSS regime must appoint a local tax representative. Foreign companies established in the Canary Islands,
Ceuta or Melilla, and foreign companies established in a country that has a mutual assistance agreement

with Spain are exempt from the above general rule. However, in practice, the tax authorities require the
appointment of a VAT representative even for companies established in the EU because it is mandatory to
have a Spanish address where communications issued by the Spanish tax authorities can be easily received.
This means that the foreign entities need to request a specific digital certificate to access their notifications or
appoint a local representative to receive the notifications on a company’s behalf.

To obtain the Spanish NIF, the following documents must be provided:

+ a registration form (Form 036);

+ the incorporation deed and articles of association, along with a Spanish translation (a sworn translation
may be requested) duly apostilled with the Hague Convention Stamp;

+ declaration of final titularity:

+ if not included in the above documents, a certificate from the company registry showing the business
address and the individual empowered to grant the power of attorney, along with a Spanish translation
(a sworn translation may be requested). When a foreigner, said person must also obtain a Spanish
identification number (Spanish NIE); and

* a power of attorney granted to a local company, or an individual, authorized to [ile the registration form
on behalf of the foreign taxable person. This power of attorney must be granted before a public notary in
the country of residence, stamped with an apostille in accordance with the Hague Convention Stamp, or
through the Spanish Consulate.

Forms and VAT registration information are available at the Spanish Tax Agency's “virtual office”:
ttps://tinyurl.com/Sdfaij3bh.

https.//tinyurl.com/49uwmwab

13 VAT grouping

VAT groups in Spain are not treated as a single taxable entity for VAT purposes. However, under certain
conditions, a so-called dominant resident entity or fixed establishment (referred to as the “dominant”)

may apply for a special VAT grouping regime together with companies that are closely related financially,
economically, and organizationally. The dominant entity must hold, directly or indirectly, more than 50% of
the shares or voting rights (effective control) of the related companies throughout the full calendar year.

This regime is optional, with the following main features:

+ the group is not treated as a separate taxable person. Each company within the group retains its own VAT
obligations;

+ there are two optional scopes for this regime: simplified and extended;

+ under the simplified scope, while each group company maintains its general VAT obligations (Form 322),
the dominant entity must file a consolidated VAT return (Form 353) on behalf of the group. VAT due or
receivable is determined at the group level via this consolidated VAT return. VAT credits and debits can be
offset among group entities;

+ under the extended scope, inter-company transactions are subject to special valuation rules, additional
cost-control obligations must be met and domestic exemptions can be waived in inter-company
transactions;

+ the dominant entity assumes responsibility for the VAT group’s obligations; and

+ joint responsibility applies to liabilities arising from the VAT group scheme.

576 | Spain

--- Page 77 ---
14 Fiscal representative

Taxable persons from non-EU countries must appoint a local fiscal representative i
+ they are applying for reimbursement of input VAT under the 13 EU VAT Directive; or
+ they are required to register for VAT purposes; and

+ they are registering to apply for the special Import OSS regime.

For residents of another EU Member State, the local representative is classified as a mandatory representative
(see paragraph 1.2).

15 Electronic notifications

Electronic communications and notices from the State Tax Administration Agency (AEAT) apply to almost all
taxable persons, including both resident and non-resident commercial entities. This includes the requirement
to receive most communications and notifications from the Spanish tax authorities by electronic means.

‘To facilitate this, the Spanish tax authorities automatically assign a special electronic address (the “direccién
electronica habilitada”) to all taxable persons covered by the regulation. Before electronic notifications
commence, the Spanish Tax Administration will inform the taxable person of this arrangement.

Access to this special electronic address requires the taxable person to have an electronic certificate
recognized by the AEAT (see paragraph 10.2, filing). Taxable persons without such a certificate may authorize
a third party holding one to access the address on their behalf. Unless the power of attomey is granted
directly before the tax authorities, the authorization must be formalized through a special power of attorney
deed executed before a public notary. The deed must also be stamped with an apostille under the Hague
Convention and registered in a special register maintained by the AEAT.

2. Taxable transactions

241 Transfer of business

For a transfer of assets to be considered non-taxable, the tangible and intangible assets transferred must form,
or be capable of forming, an autonomous economic unit for the transferor that enables the continuation of a
business activity. Whether the transferee continues the same business activity is irrelevant.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Certain free supplies are excluded from the scope of self-consumption, These include those regulated under
numbers 2°, 3°, and 4° of Article 7 of the Spanish VAT Law, which define:

+ samples;

+ demonstration services; and

+ leaflets and objects for publicity purposes.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply of goods
Under specific conditions, imports of goods followed by an intra-Community supply of goods can be exempt.

The importer or their representative must provide the following information to the authorities:
+ the VAT number assigned by the Spanish tax administration to the importer; and
+ the VAT number assigned in another EU Member State to the purchaser of the goods.

Additionally, the following requirements must be met:

* the importer or their tax representative must appear as the consignee on the transport documents;

+ the goods must be dispatched or transported to the destination EU Member State immediately after
importation; and

+ the supply following the importation must qualify for the exemption for intra-Community supplies

Spain | 577

--- Page 78 ---
If all the requirements are met, both the import and the intra-Community supply of goods would benefit from
the exemption.

2.3.2. Postponed accounting on importation

An optional scheme is available for taxpayers who are required to file monthly VAT returns, such as:
+ large companies (with a tumover exceeding EUR 6,010,121.04);

+ taxpayers under the special monthly refund regime (REDEME); and

+ taxpayers under the special VAT grouping regime (REGE).

Companies that opt for this regime can settle import VAT through their VAT return for the period in which
they receive the customs liquidation document. This means that the import VAT is recorded both as output
VAT (i.e., import VAT pending to be paid to the Spanish tax authorities) and input VAT, similar to how the
reverse charge rule operates. Unless there is a restriction on the right to deduction, this mechanism triggers a
neutral effect on the VAT return and the company does not need to finance the import VAT.

New companies can elect this option when registering with Form 036 (see paragraph 1.1.1). Existing
companies can apply for the special regime by November of the calendar year before the regime takes effect.
After the application is submitted, the Spanish tax authorities usually start a limited audit to verify the reason
and correctness of the application

It is important for companies to track their imports closely, as failure to declare import VAT in the
corresponding period (month/quarter) after opting for this regime is subject to a specific penalty (see
paragraph 10.3). To assist with this, the tax administration has introduced a procedure in the “Electronic
Office” section of its website, allowing companies to check any outstanding import VAT payments that have
been deferred and are pending declaration,

In addition to this regime, a company may request the postponement of import VAT payments if it faces
liquidity issues. Such deferrals are granted on a case-by-case basis and may require a bank guarantee (in
certain cases, a different type of guarantee or no guarantee at all may be possible),

2.3.3 Importation special regimes

Due to their relevance in optimizing the financial cost of import VAT quotas and in the broader context of
international trade, speciat mention should be made of Articles 23 and 24 of the Spanish VAT Law. These
articles outline specific exemptions for operations subject to special customs regimes under the Union
Customs Code, such as:

+ bonded Customs Warehouses and VAT Warehouses;

+ temporary imports;

+ inward processing; and

* free zones.

Failure to comply with the conditions of these special regimes or the termination of the regime will result in
an import or assimilated import, where the corresponding VAT will be assessed. When applicable, specific
provisions for determining the taxable base will be applied.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Spanish VAT legislation aligns with the provisions introduced in the EU VAT Directive by Directive
2018/1910, which covers VAT “quick fixes” for intra-Community transactions, and Council Implementing
Regulation 2018/1912, effective from | January 2020. One such provision relates to “call-off stock
agreements.” In these cases, goods are transported to Spain with the intention of supplying them later to
customers registered for VAT in Spain. These customers are entitled to take ownership of the goods based on
an existing agreement. This transaction is treated as an intra-Community supply from the EU Member State
of origin, rather than as a transfer of own goods followed by a domestic supply of goods.

578 | Spain

--- Page 79 ---
However, this simplification has certain requirements. The company must record the physical movement of
stock in its Recapitulative Statements, and both the company and its customers must maintain a register of the
goods. Additionally, the stock must be used by the customer within a 12-month period. If not, the transaction
would need to be reported by the supplier as a transfer of own goods, followed by a domestic supply of
goods.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

Work on movable or immovable property

Work on movable or immovable property is treated as a supply of services. However, the Spanish VAT Law
specifies that construction or renovation of buildings, where the supplier provides materials and the cost of
those materials exceeds 40% of the total project, is considered a supply of goods. The General Tax Directorate
also ruled in several tax rulings that, depending on the circumstances, the classification as a supply of goods
could prevail in works not explicitly mentioned in the law.

Supplies of goods with installation

A special location rule applies to supplies of goods with installation that results in the goods being fixed to
the ground. In these cases, the supply is considered to occur where the installation takes place. There is no
requirement that installation costs represent a minimum percentage of the project.

Under this scenario, if goods are transported from an EU Member State to the Spanish VAT territory to be
installed or assembled in such territory before being put at the customer’s disposal, the transaction will not
qualify as a transfer of own goods followed by a domestic supply of goods but only as a domestic supply of
goods within the Spanish VAT territory, as the installation and the transfer of the power of disposal occurs in
the Spanish VAT territory.

33 Distance sales

Spain has implemented the e-commerce legislation which was introduced on July 1, 2021 in line with the BU
VAT Directive.

3.4 Simplified triangulation

Spain has implemented the simplified triangulation rules without modifications. The rules cannot be applied if
the intermediary (B) is established in the Spanish VAT territory, According to Spanish VAT law, B must not be
registered in the country of dispatch or the country of arrival. However, following the ruling in Hans Biihler
(ECJ C-580/16), the condition that B cannot have a VAT registration number in the country of dispatch should
be interpreted to allow simplification, as long as the VAT number used is not from the EU Member State of
dispatch, In practice, Spain does not allow application of the simplified triangulation in chains consisting or
more than 3 parties where the goods are transported directly from the first to the last party.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The intra-Community acquisitions of goods carried out by (i) non-taxable legal persons, (ii) taxable persons
exclusively performing transactions which do not fully or partially give the right to deduct the input VAT,
(iii) and taxpayers under the Special system for agriculture, livestock farming and fishing from suppliers
established in another EU Member State are not subject to Spanish VAT unless the threshold of EUR 10,000
during the precedent or current calendar year is exceeded.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Spain has implemented effective use and enjoyment rules based on Article 59b of the VAT Directive 2006/112/EU.

Asa result, certain services provided by a Spanish supplier to non-EU final consumers that, according to the
general B2C rules, would normally be considered for VAT purposes outside the EU, the Canary Islands and
the autonomous cities of Ceuta and Melilla (which are not part of the Spanish VAT territory) are relocated and
subject to Spanish VAT if they are effectively used and enjoyed within the Spanish VAT territory.

Spain | 579

--- Page 80 ---
This rule applies to the following services:
+ the following services rendered to final consumers:

+ transfers/assignments of copyrights and similar services;

= goodwill;

+ advertising services;

+ advisory, consultancy, audit, engineering, legal, and similar services;

* data processing and information supply services;

+ translation and interpretation services;

+ insurance, banking, and financial services (except for the hire of safes);

+ supply of staff;

+ dubbing of films;

= leasing of tangible movable property, with the exception of those that involve any means of

transportation and containers;

+ provision of access to electricity, heating, cooling, or gas networks; and

+ obligations to refrain from business activities or rights;
+ leasing of means of transport.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

For the supply of goods or services, VAT typically becomes chargeable at the time the goods or services
are supplied. However, if an invoice is issued before the taxable transaction takes place, VAT is charged in
proportion to the amount that has been paid in the advance payment (see also 4.2).

Intra-Community supplies and consignment stock arrangements

For intra-Community supplies of goods exempt from VAT, the VAT becomes chargeable on the 15" day of the

month following:

+ the date on which the dispatch or transport of the goods to the purchaser begins; and

+ in the case of deliveries of goods on consignment, from March 1, 2020, when the goods are made
available to the purchaser.

If an invoice is issued before the specified dates mentioned above, VAT becomes chargeable when the invoice
is issued.

Additionally, from 1 March 2020:
VAT becomes chargeable if the conditions set out in the relevant provision of the Spanish VAT Law (call-
off stock agreements, see 3.1) are breached;

+ VAT also becomes chargeable the day after the 12-month period referred to in the relevant provision in
the Spanish VAT Law expires.

Commission agent

In cases involving purchase and sale commissions, where the commission agent acts on their own behalf, the
chargeability of VAT for goods supplied by the principal to the commission agent (and vice versa) coincides
with the chargeability of VAT for the goods supplied between the commission agent and the client or supplier.

Interfaces

From 1 July 2021, when goods are sold through a digital interface under the terms of Article 8 bis of the
Spanish VAT Law (“deemed supplier”), VAT becomes chargeable when the customer’s payment is accepted
(see also paragraph 3.3).

4.2 Advance payments

VAT may become chargeable before goods are delivered or services are completed if the total or partial
payment is received in advance. In this case, VAT becomes chargeable on the amount received before the
goods are made available or the services are provided.

580 | Spain

--- Page 81 ---
This VAT chargeability advance payment rule does not apply to intra-Community supplies of goods and intra-
Community acquisitions of goods.

4.3 Continuous supplies

In leases, utilities, and, in general, in successive or continuous supplies, the VAT becomes chargeable at the
moment when the portion of the price that includes each payment becomes due.

When no price bas been agreed upon or, if agreed upon, the moment of its due date has not been determined,
or if it has been established with a periodicity exceeding one calendar year, the VAT becomes chargeable on
31 December of each calendar year for the proportional part corresponding to the period elapsed from the
start of the transaction or from the previous accrual until such date, the service is being regarded as supplied
at the end of each calendar year until the service provision ceases,

44 Cash accounting

Under the special cash accounting regime, the VAT becomes chargeable:

* at the time of total or partial payment, for the amount received;

+ on 31 December of the calendar year following the one in which the transaction took place, if no payment
has been received by then.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

The taxable base can be adjusted for bad debt under the following circumstances:

+ the customer is officially declared bankrupt; or

+ due to late payment, where the payment has not been made within a specific period following the accrual
of the tax (2 months is the standard, though small businesses can voluntarily reduce this to 6 months). A
special rule applies when the special cash accounting regime is in place.

However, no adjustment of the taxable base is allowed when the payment is secured by a mortgage, or if the
customer is a related party, a non-established entity that is not resident in Spain (i.e., Spanish VAT territory,
Canary Islands, Ceuta and Melilla), or a non-taxable person when the transaction does not exceed EUR 50
(VAT excluded).

It is important to note that, following the ruling in the Ramada Storax case (CJEU Case C-756/19), the
Spanish General Tax Directorate has updated its earlier doctrine and the Spanish VAT Law has been updated.
Now, the correction of the taxable base is also allowed when the bankruptcy of'a non-resident client has been
declared by a court in another EU Member State, in accordance with the Regulation (EU) No 2015/848 on
insolvency proceedings.

The correction is subject to the following formalities:

+ a formal payment request to the client (i.e., judicial claim, notarial request or by any other means that
reliably certifies the claim for payment);

+ _ issuing a corrective invoice and registering it in the VAT register before the following deadlines:

+ — for bad debts due to bankruptcy: 3 months following the publication of the bankruptcy declaration;

+ for late payment debts: 6 months after the bad-debt period specified in the law; and

+ informing the VAT authorities of the correction and providing supporting documentation within | month
of issuing the corrective invoice. A specific electronic Form 592 is available on the tax authorities’
website (Aitps://tinyurl.com/3ppnrxhj).

5.2 Cash discounts

VAT regulations outline two scenarios for cash discounts:

Spain | 581

--- Page 82 ---
Scenario I: Early discount included in the invoice

Discounts for early payment, granted before or at the time of the transaction, must be explicitly mentioned in
the invoice and will not be included in the taxable base, If the customer fails to make the payment on time, a
corrective invoice must be issued to adjust the taxable base.

Example:

Invoice amount: BUR 1,000

Cash discount: 2% for payment within 14 days
Taxable base if paid within 14 days: EUR 980

This invoice is reported for EUR 980 plus VAT on EUR 980 in the VAT register and the corresponding VAT
return when the original invoice is issued.

Hypothesis 1: Customer Fails to Pay on Time
In this case, a corrective invoice for the 2% discount (EUR 20 plus VAT) must be issued, referencing the
original invoice.

The corrective invoice is reported for EUR 20 plus VAT in the VAT register and the VAT return when it is issued.

Hypothesis 2: Customer Pays on Time
No correction is necessary.

Scenario 1: Early discount granted after the transaction
Ifa discount for early payment is granted after the transaction, this constitutes a reason to adjust the taxable
base, requiring the issuance of a corrective invoice.

Example:
Invoice amount: EUR 1,000
Taxable base: EUR 1,000

The invoice is reported for EUR 1,000 plus VAT in the VAT register and the corresponding VAT return when
the original invoice is issued.

Subsequently, a 2% cash discount for early payment is agreed. In this case, a corrective invoice for the 2%
discount (EUR 20 plus VAT) must be issued, with a reference to the original invoice.

The corrective invoice is reported for EUR 20 plus VAT in the VAT register and the corresponding VAT return
when it is issued.

5.3 Open market value

Article 79.5 of the Spanish VAT Law introduces a special valuation rule for related-party transactions where

the consideration differs from the “open market value,” as defined in Article 72 of the VAT Directive. This

rule applies if the difference in value leads to a more favourable VAT position for either the recipient or the

supplier. This occurs in situations such as:

+ the recipient is not entitled to fully deduct the VAT incurred, and the agreed consideration is lower than
the open market value; and

+ the supplier is in a proportional deduction situation (Articles 173-175 of the VAT Directive), and the
agreed consideration is either lower (for an exempt transaction that does not grant the right to deduction)
or higher than the open market value.

5.4  Returnable packing

When reusable packaging is returned, it is treated as an adjustment to the taxable base. If the same VAT rate
applies, the refund can be handled as a reduction of the taxable base in a subsequent invoice to the client
without needing to issue a corrective invoice.

$82 | Spain

--- Page 83 ---
6. VAT Rates

6.1 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate in Spain is 21%.

6.2 Reduced rate

A reduced VAT rate of 10% applies to the following items:

+ food and beverages (excluding alcoholic beverages and soft drinks, juices and sodas with added sugars or
sweeteners);

+ animal and human food products, as well as products used for their production;

* water supplies;

+ pharmaceutical products (for both humans and animals);

+ certain medical equipment;

+ residential apartments;

+ plants and seeds;

+ certain energy products, such as electricity for vulnerable people, gas, and pellets. A super-reduced rate
of 5% was temporarily applied to electricity and other energy products. Since I January 2025, 5% is no
longer applicable and 21% VAT applies;

+ certain agricultural services:

+ repair and rehabilitation work on residential buildings or parts of them;

» certain construction work;

+ passenger transport services;

+ hotel accommodations;

* certain sanitary products (as listed in Annex Eight of the Spanish VAT Law);

«commercial fairs;

* entrance to libraries, museums, art galleries, cinemas, theatres, and live cultural events; and

* certain importations and intra~Community acquisitions of artworks.

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rates

A ssuper-reduced VAT rate of 4% applies to:

+ certain basic foodstuffs (bread, flour, milk, cheese, eggs, fruits, vegetables and other natural product
according to the Food Code). Also, since 1 January 2025 olive oil;

+ books, magazines, and newspapers;

+ medication for human use;

+ orthopaedic products;

+ government-subsidized housing; and

+ services provided to dependent persons;

The 0% VAT rate applies to:

* donations:

Since 10 April 2022, a 0% VAT rate has been applied to in-kind donations to non-profit entities as regulated
by Law 48/2002, provided these donations serve to further their goals.

+ Temporary:

During the period 23 April 2020 — 13 December 2022, a 0% VAT rate was temporarily applied to, certain
supplies of goods and services deemed essential, due to extraordinary reasons (¢.b. COVID-19 Relief
Measures, Inflation Control Measures).

This 0% VAT rate is no longer applicable.

Spain | 583

--- Page 84 ---
a Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right of deduction (intra-Community supplies of goods and export of goods)

The following supplies of goods and services are exempt from VAT but retain the right to deduct any input

VAT incurred in carrying them out:

* exports and intra~Community supplies of goods;

+ goods supplied under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops to travellers residing outside the
EU;

+ the supply, construction, repair, hire, or charter of seagoing vessels used for commercial transport of
goods or people, warfare vessels, safeguard vessels, or vessels used in coastal fishing;

+ the supply, construction, repair, hire, or charter of airplanes exclusively used by companies for intemational
navigation, commercial transport of goods or people, or by public bodies for public functions;

+ services related to and goods incorporated into the above-mentioned vessels or aircraft;

* supplies made to customers with diplomatic tax immunity;

+ supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

+ services related to inward processing on behalf of principals residing outside the EU;

* goods under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ international passenger transport by seagoing vessels and airplanes;

+ provisioning of seagoing vessels with foodstuffs (excluding inshore fishing vessels); and

+ _ services provided to goods under customs control.

If the exemption for intra-Community supplies of goods is applied, the supplier must retain a copy of the
invoice showing the customer’s valid VAT identification number (issued by another EU Member State) along
with commercial and intra-Community transport documentation (e.g., bills of lading, transport documents,
and proof of payment) that demonstrate that the goods have left Spain (in line with Article 45a of the Council
Implementing Regulation (EU) No 282/201). Also the transaction must be declared in the intra-Community
transactions recapitulative return (Form 349).

For exports, where the supplier or their representative transports the goods, they must keep the invoice,
delivery notes, transport documents, and proof that the goods have left the EU VAT territory. In cases where
the customer or their representative transports the goods (indirect export), a copy of the customs declaration
(export single administrative document (SAD)) is also required as proof. Goods must leave the EU VAT
territory within 1 month of the sale.

For supplies and provisioning of vessels and airplanes, a copy of the bill of lading must be provided by the
charterer within the month following the supply to prove the application of the exemption.

7.1.1 Warehousing

7.1.1.1 VAT warehouse

Asticle 24, One 1°) letter £) and 2°) of the Spanish VAT Law exempts the supply of goods and related services

destined for a VAT warehouse, as defined in Annex V of the Spanish VAT Law. The VAT warehouse regime

applies to the following operations:

* goods subject to excise duties during production, transformation, and storage within factories and tax
deposits, as well as their movement between such places and importation into them;

+ natural gas within a distribution network in the EU or any network connected to it;

+ goods excluded from the customs warehouse regime due to their origin, provided they are not intended
for final consumers (unless introduced in tax-free shops); and

* goods traded in futures markets until they are made available to the purchaser.

The sale of goods while still in bond, and related services, are exempt from Spanish VAT. The supplier must
obtain a statement from the purchaser confirming eligibility for the exemption.

When goods are released from the VAT warehouse, the event is treated as an import, requiring the owner of
the goods to liquidate the VAT via a specific VAT form. However, the import will not be considered to have
occurred if the release is due to an export, assimilated export, or intra-Community supply.

584 | Spain

--- Page 85 ---
The VAT warehouse operator is jointly responsible with the taxable person for VAT liabilities on goods
exiting the regime, except for goods subject to excise duties.

When goods under CN codes 19 to 25, as listed in Annex V, are released from the VAT warehouse, this is
treated as a reverse charge situation under Article 84.1.2° of the Spanish VAT Law.

7.1.1.2 Excise warehouse

The VAT warehouse regime also applies to operations related to goods subject to excise duties, including:
+ production, transformation, and storage within factories and tax deposits;

+ movement of such goods between these locations; and

* — importation of such goods.

7.1.1.3 Bonded (customs)

Goods entering Spain from non-EU countries can be placed in a bonded warehouse, where no customs duties
or import VAT are payable.

When goods are released from the bonded warehouse for free circulation, this is considered an importation,
and customs duties and VAT become due. The taxable person declaring the goods into free circulation is
responsible for paying the taxes. However, this is not considered an importation if the release is for export,
assimilated export, or intra~-Community supply of goods,

The sale of goods while in bond, and related services, are exempt from VAT. The supplier must obtain a
statement from the purchaser confirming eligibility for the exemption. Supplies of goods prior to importation
into Spain are outside the scope of Spanish VAT.

7.2 Exemptions without the right of deduction

As general rule, exempt transactions do not grant the right to deduct input VAT, unless specified otherwise.
As a result, companies benefiting from these exemptions generally have limited the right to deduct input
VAT. There is no option to opt-out, except for supplies of rural land and second or subsequent transfers of
immovable property eligible for exemption (see paragraph 7.3).

Exemptions in the public interest
Examples include:

+ health and welfare services; and

+ education services (excluding matters not included in an official Spanish education plan).

+ Other Exemptions

Examples include:

+ renting immovable property for residential purposes (except when additional services similar to those in
the hotel industry are provided);

+ the transfer of second-hand immovable property (exemption can be waived if specific requirements are
met);

+ insurance services;

+ credit, banking, and similar activities;

+ cultural and religious services;

+ sports services;

* _ intermediation services related to exempt financial services;

* _ services provided to members by economic interest associations;

+ services provided by non-profit organizations;

« lotteries, bets, and games organized by the State Lottery and Betting Company and the National
Organization for the Blind, and by the corresponding bodies of the Autonomous Communities, as well
as activities that constitute taxable events for gambling taxes and random combinations. However,
the exemption does not extend to management services and other operations of an accessory or
complementary nature to those included in the previous paragraph that do not constitute taxable events for
gambling taxes, with the exception of bingo management services.

* postal services; and

* supplies of goods where the supplier is not entitled to input tax credit.

Spain | 585

--- Page 86 ---
7.3. Option for taxation

There is an option to waive the exemption in supplies of rural land and second or subsequent transfers of

immovable property. The conditions for exercising this option are:

«the purchaser must have full or partial rights to deduct the input VAT associated with the purchase of the
immovable property; and

+ the supplier must be formally notified of the option by the customer, along with a signed declaration
stating that the purchaser is acting in his capacity of entrepreneur/professional and has the partial or full
right to deduct input VAT resulting from the transaction, based on the intended use of the immovable
property.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In addition to the reverse charge rules stipulated in the EU VAT Directive, such as Articles 194 to 196, the
Spanish VAT Law has implemented several specific reverse chatge mechanisms.

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)
For supplies of goods or services made by a non-resident supplier to taxable person located within the
Spanish VAT territory, the VAT liability is shifted to the recipient. This rule applies regardless of whether the
non-resident supplier is registered for VAT in Spain (i.e., has a Spanish Tax Identification Number - NIF - or a
Spanish VAT number) as long as the supplier is not established in Spain.

However, the application of the reverse charge rule varies for supplies of goods and services:

Goods

‘The reverse charge mechanism always applics to the domestic supply of goods by a non-resident taxable
person to another taxable person, even if the customer does not reside in Spain. As a result, a non-established
taxable person purchasing goods from another non-established taxable person is liable for Spanish VAT (self-
assessment). If they are not registered for VAT in Spain, they must register to self-assess the VAT through a
quarterly VAT return.

Additionally, special reverse charge rules mandatorily apply to specific goods (see paragraph 8.1.2).

Services

The reverse charge mechanism applies to domestic services provided by a non-resident taxable person
to another taxable person residing in the Spanish VAT territory or with a permanent establishment there,
provided the services are rendered to that establishment (there are some exceptions in which VAT will always
need to be charged - e.g., non-VAT exempt lease of immovable property located in the Spanish VAT territory
and intermediary services with respect to the lease of immovable property located on the Spanish VAT
territory).

If the customer is not a resident or is not established for VAT purposes in Spain, and the service must be
located in the Spanish VAT territory due to a special rule, the reverse charge does not apply. In such cases, the
non-resident supplier must register for VAT in Spain (if not already registered) and charge Spanish VAT to the
customer,

Legal entity with a Spanish VAT number not acting in a business capacity

‘The reverse charge mechanism also applies to legal entities with a Spanish VAT number that are not acting in
their business or professional capacity, if they receive any of the following from a non-resident supplier:

* subsequent supply of goods within a triangulation scheme; or

+ services subject to Spanish VAT.

586 | Spain

--- Page 87 ---
8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)
Supply of certain goods and services

The VAT liability is shifted to the customer (whether resident or non-resident) in the following cases:

+ supplies of non-manufactured gold or semi-finished gold products with a purity of 325 thousandths
(325/1000) or greater;

* supplies of certain types of waste (e.g., Waste or scrap of paper, cardboard, or glass);

+ sales of emission rights, certified emission reductions, and emission reduction units for greenhouse gases:

+ sales of mobile phones, video game consoles, computers, and tablets when the invoice exceeds EUR
10,000 (VAT included) being the acquirer a business or professional, or when the acquirer is considered a
“reseller”, regardless of the invoice amount; and

+ _ silver, platinum, and palladium, in raw, powdered, or semi-finished form: deliveries of these metals
resulting from transformation activities carried out by the acquiring entrepreneur or professional shall be
assimilated to the same. In any case, these must be products that are not included within the scope of the
special regime applicable to used goods, works of art, antiques, and collectibles.

Reverse charge mechanism — certain supplies of immovable property

‘The reverse charge mechanism also applies to the supply of immovable property in the following situations:

+ the supply of immovable property resulting from a bankruptcy procedure;

+ the taxable person waives the exemption on the second or subsequent supply of immovable property (as
per Article 20.One sections 20° and 22° of the Spanish VAT Law); and

+ the supply of immovable property following the execution of a real guarantee on the property.

Reverse charge mechanism — construction work on immovable property

‘The reverse charge mechanism applies to construction work related to immovable property under certain

conditions:

+ the customer must be acting as a taxable person;

+ the construction work must involve the urbanization of land or the construction or rebabilitation of
buildings (the concept of “rehabilitation” is defined in Article 20.1 section 22° Letter B of the VAT Law);

+ the construction work may or may not include the provision of materials (if the materials contribute more
than 40% of the project cost, it is considered a supply of goods; otherwise, it is a service). This includes
the provision of personnel for the work; and

* the work must be carried out under a contract between the real estate developer and the main contractor,
or between the main contractor and subcontractors.

8.2 Joint and several liability

In addition to specific provisions in the Spanish VAT Law (in particular, Article 87 of the Spanish VAT Law)
and Customs regulations (responsibility arising from special Customs and VAT regimes), as well as for
representatives under the IOSS regime, Articles 42 and 43 of the General Tax Law outline situations where
third parties may be held liable for the payment of outstanding tax debts.

Joint liability

‘The following entities can be held jointly liable for tax debts:

+ anyone involved in causing or actively collaborating in a tax offense. In this case, liability extends to
penalties as well;

+ successors of a business activity. As defined in Article 40 of the General Tax Law, successors are liable
for outstanding tax obligations arising from the business activity. If no certification clearing tax debts was
requested from the tax authorities, liability extends to the corresponding penalties;

+ up to the amount of the assets involved, the following parties may also be held jointly responsible for
tax debts and penalties; anyone causing or collaborating in hiding or transferring assets belonging to the
taxable subject with the intent of preventing the tax administration from collecting taxes;

+ anyone guilty of or negligent in jeopardizing tax embargoes;

Spain | 587

--- Page 88 ---
+ depositories of embargoed assets who permit their unauthorized transfer after receiving notice of the
embargo.

Several liability

The following persons and entities can be held severally liable for tax debts:

+ — directors or de facto managers of companies: those who, through a lack of diligence or active measures,
prevent the company from meeting its tax obligations are liable for both outstanding tax debts and
penalties;

+ managers of companies that have ceased operations: those responsible for managing the bankruptcy
process are also liable;

+ purchasers of goods that serve as collateral for tax debts: as defined in Article 79 of the General Tax Law;

+ customs agents acting on behalf of importers: these agents will not be severally liable for the importer’s
tax debts;

+ principals of subcontractors: principals are liable for the outstanding tax debts of subcontractors related to
the subcontracted work, unless they have obtained a certificate from the tax authorities (issued within the
last 12 months) confirming that the subcontractor has no outstanding tax debts; and

+ individuals or companies involved in legal structures created with the intent of abusive tax avoidance. In
this case, liability extends to penalties as well.

9. Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

VAT costs related to the following goods and services are not deductible:

+ jewellery;

+ food, drinks, and tobacco;

* entertainment expenses;

+ gifisto clients, employees, or third parties, except for samples and promotional items with minimal value; and

+ travel fares, hotel accommodations, and restaurant expenses, if they are not deductible for corporate tax
purposes.

If the goods or services are partially used for business purposes, VAT on those items is generally not

deductible. However, an exception applies to the following:

+ investment goods: VAT can be deducted on a pro-rata basis; and

+ purchase and lease of cars, including their spare parts, maintenance, and related expenses (e.g,, petrol,
lubricants, parking, tolls): a partial deduction of 50% is allowed unless a higher proportion is proven (the
burden of proof lies with the taxable person, who can use any means to substantiate the claim).

9.2 Excess input VAT

Deductible VAT, as defined by the Spanish VAT Law, can be offset against VAT charged on the sale of goods
or services when filing quarterly or monthly VAT returns (Form 303).

If the return results in a receivable balance, this balance can be carried forward to the next quarterly or

monthly VAT return. If the balance at the end of the last quarterly/monthly VAT return of the year is still a

receivable, the following options are available:

+ offget the receivable in subsequent VAT returns (within a 4-year period); and

+ request a refund in the VAT return for the last period of the year. Taxable persons can also opt for the
monthly refund regime (REDEME, “Régimen de devolucién mensual”) to be eligible for refunds based
on monthly VAT returns.

Refunds must be issued within 6 months of the filing deadline for the corresponding VAT return. If the VAT
authorities fail to comply with this deadline, late payment interest will be paid to the taxpayer,

588 | Spain

--- Page 89 ---
9.3 Adjustment periods

VAT incurred before commencing business activity

VAT incurred before a business activity officially starts is deductible based on a provisional percentage
proposed by the company. If applicable, adjustments should be made according to the definitive percentage
over the first 4 years of activity.

This is also applicable to those who, already having the status of an entrepreneur or professional due to their
ongoing activities of such nature, commence a new business or professional activity that constitutes a distinct
sector from the activities they had previously been carrying out,

VAT incurred on investment goods

A follow-up period applies to VAT incurred on the purchase of investment goods. The VAT deduction must
be adjusted if the original right to deduction changes due to a variation in the pro-rata, the foreseeable
destination of the goods or if such investment goods are delivered. In the latter case, if the delivery is exempt
or not subject to tax, it will be considered that the investment good was used exclusively for transactions that
do not give the right to deduct throughout the year in which the delivery was made and in the remaining years
until the expiration of the adjustment period, Conversely, if the delivery is subject to tax and not exempt, it
will be considered that the investment good was used exclusively for transactions that gave the right to deduct
throughout the year in which the delivery was made and in the remaining years until the expiration of the
adjustment period, The adjustment periods are:

+ immovable property: 9 years following the year of first use; and

+ movable property (including animals): 4 years following the year of first use.

In cases of loss or definitive unusability of investment goods, due to a cause not attributable to the taxpayer
and duly justified, no adjustment shall be made in the years following the occurrence of such circumstance.

Start-up period: input VAT incurred during the start-up phase of a business or professional activity must be
adjusted on a pro-rata basis for the first 4 calendar years.

94 VAT Refund to EU taxable persons

A special refund procedure applies to taxable persons residing in another EU Member State, the Canary
Islands, or the autonomous cities of Ceuta or Melilla if:
* there is no fixed VAT establishment in Spain, or if such an establishment exists, during the period referred
to in the application, they have not made any supplies of goods or services from the fixed establishment:
+ the applicant has not carried out any supply of goods or services subject to Spanish VAT during the refund
period, except for:
* supplies of goods or services where the reverse charge applies; or
+ exempt intra-Community transport and related services;
* the applicant has not purchased goods or services in Spain during the refund period that would make
them liable for VAT under the reverse charge mechanism; and
+ the applicant complies with all requirements and limitations in the Spanish VAT Law for the right to
deduction.

‘The applicant allocates the acquired or imported goods or the services received in the Spanish VAT territory to
transactions that give the right to deduction in accordance with the applicable regulations in the EU Member
State where they are established and based on the applicable deduction percentage in that EU Member State,
If the applicable deduction percentage is adjusted after the submission of the refund request, the applicant
must, in any case, proceed to correct the amount by adjusting the requested amount or reimbursing the excess
amount refunded. The refund application must be submitted electronically via the tax authorities’ portal in the
claimant’s country of residence.

The annual minimum claim limit is EUR 50 (for claims covering a calendar year or the remainder of a year).
For quarterly claims, the minimum is EUR 400.

Acclaim can be filed for periods that are no less than three months and not exceeding the calendar year. An
annual claim may also be submitted to include VAT amounts not reflected in the quarterly returns.

Spain | 589

--- Page 90 ---
Claims covering periods of less than 3 months can be submitted if the period refers to the remainder of the
calendar year.

‘The period for submitting the refund request begins the day after the end of each calendar quarter or each
calendar year. The deadline for submitting claims is September 30 of the year following the ycar in which the
VAT was incurred.

Original supporting documents are not required, but electronic copies of invoices exceeding certain amounts
must be attached (e.g., taxable base exceeding EUR 1,000 or EUR 250 for petrol). The Spanish tax authorities
may request additional information or original documents during the refund process.

The Spanish tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receipt. This period
may be extended to 6 or 8 months if additional information is requested. It shall be deemed rejected if, after
the deadlines referred to, no explicit notification of its resolution has been received. If the deadlines are
exceeded, late payment interest will apply.

If the refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account provided.
The time limit for appealing a decision before the non-resident VAT office is 1 month following the decision.
If the appeal is unsuccessful, the claimant may proceed to the Central Administrative Court (within 1 month
of receiving the appeal decision) and, if still unsuccessfal, to the courts (within 2 months of receiving the
decision on appeal).

The internal regulations governing the right to deduct input VAT also apply to non-resident claimants.
Limitations from the claimant's country of residence may also apply.

9.5 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

In addition to the conditions outlined in paragraph 9.4, the following special requirements apply:

+ areciprocity agreement must exist between Spain and the applicant’s country of residence. Reciprocity
agreements exist with Canada, Israel, Japan, Monaco, Norway, and Switzerland, each with its own
specific terms, Since Brexit, there is a reciprocity agreement also with the UK; and

+ a tax resident must be appointed to file the refund application on behalf of the non-resident. This
representative is jointly responsible for any undue refunds. A special power of attorney, granted before
a public notary and stamped with an apostille under the Hague Convention, is required. The document
must be attached to the first VAT refund application as non-established that is filed before the Spanish Tax
Agency (AEAT).

Reciprocity is not required for refunds of VAT incurred on:

+ purchasing or importing templates and moulds used for the fabrication of goods in Spain, provided the
goods are subsequently exported; and

* accommodation, restaurant, and transportation expenses related to attending fairs, congresses, or
exhibitions of a commercial or professional nature in Spain,

Refund applications for VAT incurred in Spain must be submitted electronically on a quarterly or yearly basis,
using Form 361, available on the Spanish Tax Agency website: hitps://tinyurl.com/mry4r6xz

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

Invoices can be issued either on paper or electronically, provided they meet the following criteria:
«the authenticity of origin is guarantee
«+ the integrity of the contents is maintained;
+ the invoice is legible.

Any legally accepted proof, especially standard business controls, ean be used to meet these criteria.

10.1.1 Invoice content
Standard invoices

(i) Minimum Information Required:
* the date the invoice is issued;
+ the date the transaction took place or when the advance payment was received (if different from the
invoice date);
+ the invoice number (sequential) and series. A different series may be required in specific cases, such as
corrective invoices, invoices issued by the recipient, or invoices issued by a third party;
+ the VAT/tax identification number under which the supply of goods or services is made;
* the VAT/tax identification number of the customer is required for:
+ VAT exempt intra~Community supplies of goods;
+ transactions with reverse charge mechanism;
+ transactions within the Spanish VAT territory when the supplier is established in Spain.
the business name and address of both the supplier and customer;

establishment involved in the transaction must be indicated;
* adescription of the delivered goods or rendered services, with sufficient detail to determine the taxable
base, including quantity, unit price, and any discount not included in the unit price:
+ special references as applicable:
+ for exempt transactions: reference to the Spanish VAT Law, the EU VAT Directive, or the term “VAT
exempt”;
+ for self-billing: the phrase “Facturacién por el destinatario” (invoice issued by the customer);
+ for reverse charge: the phrase “Inversién del sujeto pasivo” (customer is liable for VAT):
+ the taxable amount, broken down if necessary when the invoice covers:
* both exempt and non-exempt transactions;
* transactions subject to reverse charge and those not subject to it;
* transactions subject to different VAT rates;
+ the applied VAT rate(s);
+ the VAT amount, independently stated:
* the word “invoice” is not required, but if the document is a duplicate, this must be stated.
Duplicates are allowed when:
* the transaction involves multiple taxable customers; or
* the original invoice was lost.

When the “Verifactu Ordinance” comes into force additional requirements will include:

+ a QR code;

+ the phrase “factura verificable en la sede electronica de la AEAT” or “VERIFACTU” if the invoicing
software sends a copy of all its records to the Spanish Tax Agency system.

(ii) Additional Information for Specific Transactions:

Invoices documenting intra~Community supplies of new means of transport must include:
+ vehicle specifications;

+ — date of first use;

+ — distance covered or hours of navigation or flight before delivery;

For the travel agency scheme, the invoice must reference the “régimen especial de las agencias de viaje”
(special travel agency regime);

For special regimes covering second-hand goods, art, antiques, and collectibles, the invoice must reference
the applicable special regime: “régimen especial de los bienes usados” (second-hand goods special regime),
“régimen especial de los objetos de arte” (art special regime), or “régimen especial de las antigtiedades y
objetos de coleccién” (antique and collectibles special regime).

Spain | 591

--- Page 92 ---
Recapitulative invoices

It is possible to document multiple transactions made on different dates with the same client within the same
calendar month on a single invoice.

Amendment of invoices

An issued invoice must be corrected if a formal mistake has been made, the VAT amount was incorrect, or
circumstances as described in Article 80 of the VAT Law - regarding modifications of the taxable base - apply.

Correcting an issued invoice requires issuing a new corrective invoice. This invoice can be either a full
invoice or a simplified one, provided it includes the following references:

+ areference to the invoice(s) being corrected (unless the correction is due to the volume of transactions); and
+ — details of the corrections made to the original invoice(s).

The return of products or packaging can be reflected as a reduction of the taxable base on subsequent invoices
issued to the same client.

Language

The language of the invoice may be freely chosen by the business parties, However, if requested by the
Spanish tax authorities, the taxable person must translate the invoice into any of Spain’s official languages in
the following cases:

+ Invoices documenting transactions located within the Spanish VAT territory.

+ Invoices received by taxable persons established within the Spanish VAT territory.

10.1.2 Time limits

Invoices must be issued and delivered to the client when the transaction takes place. If the client is another
taxable subject, the invoice must be issued and delivered before the 16" of the month following the month
in which VAT is accrued (or when the transaction occurred, if the special cash regime applies, or when the
recapitulative invoice was issued).

10.1.3 Currency
Invoices may be issued in foreign currencies. However, the VAT amount must be stated in Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

The applicable exchange rate is the official rate issued by the Central Bank of Spain (identical to the
European Central Bank’s rate) on the date the taxable event occurred. Monthly averages are not permitted,
and no notification to the tax authorities is required.

10.15 Self-billing

Invoices can be issued by:

+ the supplier, obliged to issue the invoice;
+ the recipient of the transactions; and

+ a third party hired for this purpose.

In order for the invoicing obligation to be materially fulfilled by the recipient of the transactions, the

following requirements must be met:

+ there must be an agreement between (he entrepreneur or professional who carries out the transactions and
the recipient of these, by which the former authorizes the latter to issue the corresponding invoices for
said transactions;

+ this agreement must be signed prior to the execution of the transactions and must specify those to which it
refers;

+ each invoice issued must be subject to an acceptance procedure by the entrepreneur or professional who
has carried out the transaction. This procedure will be adjusted to what the parties determine;

+ the recipient of the transactions who issues the invoices must send a copy to the entrepreneur or
professional who carried them out; and

592.1 Spain

--- Page 93 ---
* these invoices will be issued in the name and on behalf of the entrepreneur or professional who has
carried out the operations documented therein.

‘The obligation to issue an invoice may be fulfilled by a third party hired for this purpose.

The Spanish tax authorities must be informed in advance if the customer or third party is not an EU resident
(except if resident in the Canary Islands is, Ceuta, Melilla, or from a country that has a mutual assistance
agreement with Spain).

10.1.6 Simplified invoices

Simplified invoices that have the same legal status as full invoices, but do not give the right to deduct the

input VAT, can be issued in the following cases:

* when a corrective invoice is required;

+ for transactions not exceeding EUR 400 (VAT included);

+ for transactions not exceeding EUR 3,000 (VAT included), if specified in the VAT ordinance (e.g., retail
services, restaurants, bars, hotels, toll roads, parking, passenger transport, etc.);

+ — in other cases, if authorized by the tax authorities.

However, simplified invoices are not permitted in the case of:

+ intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ distance sales, unless under OSS and not exceeding the above mentioned thresholds;

+ transactions carried out by a non-resident company when the reverse charge rule applies and there is self
billing by the recipient or billing by a third party;

* transactions located in another EU Member State or outside the EU and subject to the reverse charge rule.

Minimum Information Required for a Simplified Invoice:

* the date the invoice is issued;

+ the date of the transaction or receipt of advance payment (if different from the invoice date);
* the invoice number (sequential) and series;

+ the tax identification number under which the goods or services are supplied;

+ the business name and address of the supplier;

+ the identification of the type of goods delivered or services rendered,

+ the applied VAT rate and, optionally, the phrase “VAT included”;

* the total consideration;

* fora corrective invoice: reference to the original invoice and details of the corrections made.

10.1.7. Storage of invoices
Invoices issued and received, along with import documents supporting VAT on importation, must be stored.

This obligation applies for the VAT assessment period: 4 years following the VAT return deadiine (longer if
the assessment period is interrupted).

‘The storage period may be extended in specific cases, such as:

+ invoices related to VAT incurred for purchasing fixed assets subject to an adjustment peri

+ storage obligations under mercantile regulations (6 years);

+ Corporate Income Tax regulations (if the taxable person wishes to offset losses or for the useful life of
fixed assets if depreciation is applied).

Third parties hired for storage may fulfil this obligation. Mandatory prior communication to the Spanish tax
authorities is required if the storage is conducted by a third party that is not an EU resident (except if resident
in the Canary Islands is, Ceuta, Melilla, or in a country that has a mutual assistance agreement with Spain).

Invoices can be stored in any format, provided the authenticity of origin, integrity of contents, and legibility
are guaranteed. The Spanish tax authorities must be able to access them upon request without unjustified
delay, E-storage is required if invoices are stored outside Spain.

Spain | 593

--- Page 94 ---
For electronically stored invoices:

+ the system must guarantee the legibility of the original invoice and, when applicable, any associated data,
as well as the verification system or other authorized process that ensures authenticity and integrity:

+ the system must allow the tax authorities complete online access to the data and provide the ability to
download it without unjustified delay.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing
An electronic invoice is any invoice that is issued and received by electronic means. While no special IT
standards are required, the system used must ensure the following for the entire storage period:

+ authenticity of origin;

+ integrity of contents;

+ legibility.

Formerly accepted standards are stil] valid for guaranteeing the authenticity of origin and integrity of
contents, but other methods may also be used, such as:

+ an advanced electronic signature;

+ anelectronic data interchange (EDI) system;

+ any other method proposed by the issuer, provided it is previously accepted by the Spanish tax authorities.

Issuing electronic invoices requires prior consent from the recipient.

Mandatory E-invoicing
The current mandatory B2G e-invoicing has been extended to B2B domestic transactions by the, so called,
“Ley Crea y Crece” (Law 18/2022, of 28 September). The coming into force of this obligation will take effect
12 months after the existing draft Ordinance of January 29, 2024, is approved and published in the Official
Gazette (24 months for companies with turnover under 8 million Euros).

Due to delays in the approval and publishment of the draft Ordinance of January 29, 2024, it is foreseen that
the new mandatory B2B e-invoicing becomes in force by 2026/2027.

Companies operating in Spain from establishments located in autonomous regions with special VAT
competences (the Basque Country and Navarra) must comply with the special e-invoicing requirements for
both B2B and B2C transactions under the “ticketbai” system, which is already in force in those territories.

10.1.9 Statutory requirements for invoicing systems (Verif actn Ordinance)

Spanish companies and non-resident companies operating in Spain through a permanent establishment for
non-resident income tax purposes will be required to use invoicing software compliant with the standards set
out by Royal Decree 1007/2023, of December 5 (the “Veri*factu Ordinance”).

This obligation is expected to take effect by 28 July 2025 for software developers and sellers on | January
2026 for companies eligible for the Spanish Corporate tax and on | July 2026 in other cases.

Compliant invoicing software will need to maintain real-time, time-stamped records of issued and canceled
invoices, including details required by the “Veri*factu Ordinance.” Each entry must be electronically signed
and accompanied by a digital fingerprint (hash).

Companies complying with SII obligations will be exempt from this requirement.

(i) Quarterly/monthly VAT return (Form 303)

+ a “large business” (taxable persons with an annual turnover exceeding EUR 6,010,121.04);

As a general rule, quarterly VAT returns must be filed within the first 20 days of the month following the

During the calendar year, taxpayers must offset the VAT receivable result of the VAT returns with VAT

VAT return.

There is no obligation to file Form 303 for taxable persons who only incur exempt transactions under Articles

Starting from the monthly VAT retumn for September 2024 (3rd quarterly VAT return for 2024), Form 303
has been amended to align with the provisions of the Final Provision Third of Royal Decree 117/2024, With
certain exceptions, this decree introduces the rectifying VAT return as the standard procedure for correcting,
modifying, or completing previously filed VAT returns. Previously, this could only be done by filing an
“additional” VAT return or submitting a rectification application.

If the VAT return results in an amount payable, the payment must be made through a bank account in a

the return.

Payment by direct debit is also possible, either through a Spanish bank or a bank in another member state of

If the last VAT return of the calendar year results in a receivable, refunds will be made by bank transfer.

a nominative or crossed check, will be submitted together with a document requesting said form of refund at

10.3 Penalties

‘Tax offenses in Spain are classified as light, serious, or very serious. Penalties for these offenses can range
from a fixed amount to a percentage of the tax payable (50%~150%), depending on the circumstances
involved, such as:

+ fraud;

+ repeated commission of tax offenses;

+ economic harm caused to the Treasury; and

+ failure to comply with formal requirements.

Considerations regarding penalties include:
+ presumption of innocence;

+ aspecial procedure is required;

* automatic suspension in case of a claim:

* acceptance of the penalty results in a 40% reduction; and
+ voluntary payment results in a 25% reduction.

The tax assessment period in Spain is 4 years from the deadline to file the corresponding VAT return. This
period can be interrupted by actions taken either by the authorities or the taxable person.

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

When a taxable person becomes liable for VAT in Spain, he must register for VAT immediately and inform the
Spanish tax authorities through the submission of a registration census form (Form 036). If this requirement is
not met, and the Spanish tax authorities cannot collect VAT as a result, a penalty for late registration (together
with other eventual penalties for the lack of payment of VAT to the Spanish treasury) may be imposed.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT returns (without payable position) or intra-Community
transactions recapitulative return on time

Late filing of VAT returns (without payable position) incurs a penalty of EUR 100 if submitted voluntarily

(EUR 200 if submitted after a request from the VAT authorities).

Incomplete informative returns result in a fine of EUR 20 per missing piece of information, with a minimum
fine of EUR 300 and a maximum of EUR 20,000 (reduced by half in cases of late voluntary compliance).

10.3.3 Default recharges for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

Without a request from the Spanish tax authorities, late payment surcharges for the late submission of VAT
returns are imposed as follows:

* 1% per each month passed before the payment is made;

+ a flat 15% once 12 months are elapsed plus late payment interests calculated from the 12" month onwards.

The surcharge is reduced to 25% of the penalties mentioned above if the VAT is paid voluntarily when the
VAT return is submitted late, If payment is made after a notice from the VAT authorities, the full penalty plus
late payment interest must be paid.

10.3.4 Penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Penalties for incorrect VAT reporting include:
+ undue reimbursement application: 15%;
+ undue collection of refunds: 50%—150%;

Spain | 597

--- Page 98 ---
+ VAT arising from reverse charge or intra-Community transactions not declared: 10%;

+ failure to communicate the reverse charge applicable to construction or renovation work: 10% of the
involved VAT quotas;

+ failure to declare VAT incurred on imports in the corresponding VAT return (if the special liquidation
regime applies): 10% of the VAT quotas involved.

10.3.5 SI penalties regime

Specific penalties apply to companies not compliant with the SII: 0.5% of the gross amounts involved, with a
minimum penalty of EUR 300 and a maximum of EUR 6,000 per quarter.

Additionally, inconsistencies between SII reports and VAT returns may lead to audits and delays in VAT refunds.

10.4 Intrastat

Monthly Intrastat returns must be filed within the first 12 calendar days of the following month,

Returns can be filed through the corresponding customs office website.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

‘The Intrastat threshold is set annually by a Ministerial Order. For both intra-Community arrivals and
dispatches, the threshold is EUR 400,000.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The following information must be reported for both arrival and dispatch statistics:

* BU Member State of origin or destination;

* nature of the transaction;

* mode of transport;

+ 8-digit commodity code for the goods;

* — statistical procedure (details on the movement of goods);

+ net weight of the goods in kilograms;

+ invoice value of the goods in Euros;

+ — identification number assigned to the customer in the EU Member State of destination;
* country of origin of the merchandise.

10.4.3 Intrastat penalties regime

The Intrastat penalties are regulated by Royal Decree 1305/2024, of 23 December, that approves the
Ordinance for the penalty procedure for the non-compliance with Intrastat obligations and in force since
1 February 2025.

‘Asa result of these new rules, the penalties can range between EUR 150 and EUR 6,000 per Intrastat return, with
the possibility of a 50% reduction in case of acceptance and prompt payment (according to the previous regime
the penalties ranged between EUR 60,10 and FUR 30,050.62, while the reductions were for a maximum of 40%).

It is important to be aware that the values declared in the Intrastat returns should be the consistent with those
declared in the VAT returns (Form 303) and intra-Community transactions recapitulative returns ( Form 349)
for intra-Community acquisitions and supplies of goods.
