Sweden

In Sweden, the VAT Directive is mainly implemented through the Swedish VAT Act “Mervardessk ttelagen
(2023:200)”. On July 2023 a revised VAT Act was enacted in which the structure of the Swedish VAT Act
is in fine with the structure of the VAT Directive. The Swedish term for VAT is “mervirdessk tt” or more
informally “moms”.

iy Taxable persons

1.1 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Domestic taxable persons with annual domestic supplies of SEK 120,000 (approximately EUR 12,000) or
lower are not obligated to register for VAT. The threshold was increased from SEK 80,000 (approximately
EUR 80,000) as from | January 2025. There is, however, no threshold for taxable persons that e.g. mak.
intra-Community supplies or acqi re construction services where the customer is liable for the VAT.

Ifa business has a Swedish electronic identification, it is possible to register for VAT electronically through
the website www.verk amt.se. It is also possible to register by sending the form “Féretagsregistrering”
(Tax and Payroll Tax Application, SKV 4620) to the Swedish Tax Authority (Sk tteverk 1). The brochure
“Pretagsregistrering” (Registering a business, SKV 418) contains instructions for filling in the form.

The form and the brochure can be downloaded at the following link www.sk tteverk t.se

It can also be ordered from the Swedish Tax Authority Customer Service Line. Please see the last page of the
brochure mentioned above for further details.

12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

From | January 2025, new provisions apply in Sweden regarding exemption from tax liability under the
special scheme for taxable persons with small annual turnover (small businesses). The biggest change is that
companies based in another BU country can apply the exemption from tax liability according to the special
scheme for small companies in Sweden and that a company based in Sweden can apply the exemption from
tax liability according to the special scheme for small companies in other EU countries.

According to the Swedish Tax Authority, the term “established” in the VAT Directive means the same as the
term “base” of the economic activity. A company established in another EU country may apply the exemption
from tax liability, even if it has a fixed place of establishment in Sweden.

A condition for the exemption from tax liability to apply is that the taxable person’s annual turnover within
Sweden is no more than SEK 120,000 (approximately EUR 12,000) excluding value added tax during the
calendar year (the annual turnover threshold). The annual turnover must also not have exceeded SEK 120,000
in either of the two immediately preceding calendar years.

Another condition for the exemption from tax liability to apply is that the taxable person’s annual turnover
within the EU is a maximum of EUR 100,000 during the calendar year. The annual turnover within the EU
must also not have exceeded EUR 100,000 in the immediately preceding calendar year.

In order for the exemption from tax liability to apply, it is also required that the taxable person is identified
by means of an individual registration number (EX-number) in the country where it has its permanent
establishment.

A taxable person who applies the exemption from tax liability has no right of deduction link d to transactions
covered by the exemption. This applies regardless of whether the company has its permanent establishment
in another EU country or in Sweden. A taxable person with a permanent establishment in another EU country
that applies the exemption from tax liability also has no right to a refund for input VAT link d to transactions

covered by the exemption.

--- Page 102 ---
To oblain a VAT registration in Sweden, the taxable person must submit an application form “Sk tteanmélan
fOrutkindsk féretagare” (Tax application for foreign entrepreneurs, SKV 4632) to the Swedish Tax Authority
(Sk tteverk t).

This form can be downloaded at the following link www.sk tteverk tse.
The form is to be filled in, printed, signed and submitted in original.

The application form should be submitted with the following documents:

+ acertificate of incorporation which shows who is authorised to sign for the company (refers to legal entities);

+ apower of attomey for the VAT representative (if applicable).

The Swedish Tax Authority usually assigns the VAT identification number within 4 ~ 6 weeks after receiving
all the reqi red documents.

1.3 VAT grouping

According to the Swedish VAT rules, a VAT group may, under certain circumstances, include the following

entities:

+ taxable persons under supervision of the Financial Supervisory Board (in Swedish: Finansinspek ionen)
supplying exempt financial and insurance services, and taxable persons that mainly supply goods or
services to those taxable persons; and

+ taxable persons acting as commissionaires or principals under the commissionaire structure referred to in
Chapter 36 of the Swedish Income Tax Act

‘The taxable persons need to be closely bound to one another by financial, economic and organisational links
in order to form a VAT group. VAT grouping is only allowed for taxable persons resident in Sweden and
foreign taxable persons having a fixed establishment in Sweden.

The VAT grouping is voluntary. Pure holding companies cannot be included.

‘The members of'a VAT group are treated as one single taxable person for VAT purposes, The VAT group is
treated in the same way as a single company registered for VAT on its own. A Swedish VAT group is currently
operating under one single VAT registration number.

The representative member of the VAT group accounts for any VAT due on supplies made by the group to
third parties outside the group. There is only one single VAT return for the whole VAT group. Since the VAT
group is treated as a single taxable person, VAT on goods or services supplied between group members is not
accounted for.

‘The members of the VAT group are jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT debts of the group.

1.4 Fiscal representative

‘Non-LU taxable persons, except for taxable persons from Norway and Iceland, need to appoint a fiscal
representative in Sweden for VAT purposes. The fiscal representative must be a resident in Sweden and needs
to be approved by the Swedish Tax Authority. A fiscal representative is not jointly and severally liable for
payment of VAT and penalties.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

The transfer of business is considered to be an out of scope, a “no supply” transaction, provided that criteria
are met. In order to be able to apply the ‘no supply” provision, the business transferred must be a transfer of

602 | Sweden

--- Page 103 ---
the business as a whole or an independent part (on a standalone basis) of the business. The buyer must be a
taxable person and continue the business in the same manner as the supplier.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

In Sweden free supplies of goods or services are covered by Articles 16, 18 and 26 in the VAT Directive. In
relation to Article 16, the free supply of ‘gifts of small value’ is not taxed in Sweden if the purchase price
per item is less than EUR 30 (excluding VAT). It should be stressed that the value should be considered for
each occasion there is a giveaway. It is less lik ly however, that the Swedish Tax Authority would accept

a giveaway situation where the amount is less than 30 EUR but you give away 50 pieces of the good in

ye stion to one individual.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply
No VAT is to be paid on the importation of goods for a subseye nt exempt intra-Community supply. If VAT
has been paid, the importer is entitled to a refund. The VAT exemption should be claimed in the Customs
declaration. The VAT-number of the purchaser of the intra-Community supply needs to be stated,

2.3.2. Postponed accounting on importation
Taxable persons registered for VAT are to report import VAT payable to the Swedish Tax Authotity. This

regulation means that an importer who is liable for VAT shall report and deduct import VAT in the same VAT
return.

In principle, these import regulations will work’ n the same way as imports made for a subseye nt intra-
Community supply, i.e. no negative cash flow implications at the time of importation regardless of whether
the goods are aimed for the Swedish mark t or for the rest of the EU mark t.

3. Place of supply

31 Consignment and call-off stock

It is possible to apply simplification rules when transferring goods to a consignment or call-off stock instead
of applying the general rules. The rules on call-off stockha ve been implemented in Swedish VAT legislation
in accordance with Articles 17a, 243(3) and 262(2) of the EU VAT Directive.

‘The simplification rules mean that the transfer of goods from Sweden to a call-off stock in another EU Member
State should not be regarded as a delivery of goods in terms of VAT. Corresponding transfers from another EU
Member State to a call-off stock in Sweden should not be regarded as an intra~Community acquisition.

‘The following conditions must be met in order to apply the simplification rules:

+ both the supplier and the intended purchaser are taxable persons;

+ the supplier does not have established the seat of his economic activities or have any permanent
establishment in the EU Member State where the goods are transported;

+ the supplier must how at the time of commencement of the shipment of the goods the identity of the
intended purchaser and his VAT registration number in the country to which the goods are transported.
‘The supplier can exchange the intended purchaser without the exchange resulting in the transfer of the
goods followed by a taxable supply:

+ the supplier must keep records of incoming goods to the call-off stock; and

* the supplier must include the intended purchaser’s VAT registration numbers in the EC sales list.

Transfers of own goods and taxable supplies can occur in the following situations:
«the 12-month time limit is exceeded. The goods can remain in the call-off stock’ or a maximum of twelve
months from the date the goods arrive at the call-off stock;

Sweden | 603

--- Page 104 ---
+ the change of purchaser is not done according to given conditions;

+ the product is destroyed, lost or stolen. However, minor losses due to the goods’ own nature, unforeseen
circumstances or government decisions are such circumstances that should not be regarded as a fictitious
tumover; and

+ delivery of the goods tak s place from the call-off stock o another EU Member State than the EU
Member State from which it was originally transported

If the supplier applies the general rules instead of the simplification rules, the supplier needs to register for
VAT in Sweden for the intra-Community acqi sition of its own goods when goods are transported from an EU
Member State to a consignment stock call-off stock n Sweden. As a main rule, reverse charge applies on the
onward supply by a foreign supplier to a customer as long as the customer is registered for VAT in Sweden. It
is, however, possible for the foreign taxable person to opt for VAT liability (i.e. to apply VAT on the onward
supply cven if the customer is registered for VAT in Sweden and domestic reverse charge would apply).

3.2 Installation supplies

Assembly and installation of goods can either be treated as a supply of services or a supply of goods. The
supply of assembly and installation, although it consists of a service component and a goods component,
should normally not be divided into a service and goods part.

Ifthe assembly and installation worl tself is extensive, the entire supply may be treated as a supply of
services and not as a supply of goods. In cases where the supply only consists of assembling different parts
of a machine which have all been provided to an assembler by his customer, it is considered to be a supply
of services. When determining whether the supply constitutes a supply of services or a supply of goods, the
agreement, the price, the work ime and the labour costs would be considered.

Domestic reverse charge is applicable on the assembly and installation of goods, if the acquirer of the goods or
services is registered for VAT in Sweden, The acquirer of the goods or services does not have to be established
for VAT purposes in Sweden, VAT registration is sufficient for the domestic reverse charge to be applicable.

33 Distance sales

From 2021 onwards, the rules and thresholds for distance sales (both intra-Community distance sales and
distance sales of imported goods) have been harmonized across the EU with no leeway for Member States.
‘The distance selling threshold for sales to customers in other EU Member States without a VAT registration
number (e.g. private individuals) is now EUR 10,000 for intra-Community distance sales,

Tumover generated from the supply of ‘electronically supplied services’, telecommunication services and
broadcasting services are included in the EUR 10,000 threshold. A threshold of EUR 150 per consignment
applies for the application of the special scheme for distance sales of imported goods.

34 Simplified triangulation

Under the Swedish VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the

EU VAT Directive. The simplified triangulation rules can be applied iff

+ Sweden is the country of arrival of the goods; and

+ the person to whom the subsege nt supply is rendered (party C) is registered for VAT purposes in
Sweden.

The simplified triangulation rules cannot be applied if Sweden is the country of arrival and (party B) is
established in Sweden. Where Sweden is the country of arrival, the wording of the Swedish legislation does
not preclude the application of the simplified triangulation rules, if the middleman (party B) is VAT registered
in Sweden (party/country C) or in the country of dispatch (party/country A).

35 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acqi sition of goods by non-taxable legal persons engaged in activities outside the scope
of VAT is SEK 90,000 (approximately EUR 9,000)

--- Page 105 ---
3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

The following services are subject to the effective use and enjoyment rules, if provided to non-taxable persons:

+ transfers and assignments of copyrights, patents, licences, trademarks and similar rights:

+ advertising services;

+ services of consultants, engineers, consultancy firms, lawyers, accountants and other similar services, as
well as data processing and the provision of information;

+ banking, financial and insurance transactions including reinsurance, with the exception of the hire of
safes;

+ supply of staff;

+ hiring out of movable tangible property, with the exception of all means of transport; and

+ provision of access to a natural gas system situated within the territory of the European Community or
to any network onnected to such a system, to the electricity system or to heating or cooling network .
or the transmission or distribution through these systems or network , and the provision of other services
directly linked thereto.

The above-listed services are subject to Swedish VAT if the effective use and enjoyment tak s place in
Sweden. In addition, those services are not subject to the Swedish VAT if the effective use and enjoyment
tak s place outside the European Union.

Certain services provided to a taxable person are provided abroad if they are exclusively used or otherwise
capitalized outside the EU. This applies to the following services:

* goods transport services;

+ _ services related to transport such as loading, unloading, goods handling and similar services; and

+ services in the form of valuation of, or worlon, goods that are movable property.

The provision in the Swedish VAT Act does not have a direct counterpart in the VAT Directive, but is
supported by the VAT Directive's provisions on actual use and enjoyment found in Article 59a(a) of the VAT
Directive.

4, Chargeable event and Chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

‘Tax liability normally arises upon delivery of goods, provision of a service or when a withdrawal is made.
If payment is made before the delivery, the taxable event is considered to be the payment. The time for tax
liability according to Swedish VAT law matches Articles 62-71 in the BU VAT Directive,

Goods are considered delivered when the right to dispose of the item, as owner has passed from the seller
to the buyer. Services are considered supplied when the supplier has fulfilled his obligation, i.e., when the
customer has approved the completed performance. A service on an item is considered to be provided, when
the customer has collected the item,

Regarding EU trade the obligation to issue an invoice overrules these principles. The VAT becomes
chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice. In case the invoice has not been issued timely, VAT shall
become chargeable on the 15" day of the month following that during which the chargeable event occurs.
The VAT on the purchase of a service, which falls under the main rules of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT
Directive, is nevertheless due at the time the service is supplied.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acqi rer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the payment has been totally or partially received before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acqi rer or the services are supplied.

Sweden | 605

--- Page 106 ---
43 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month, which are dispatched or
transported from Sweden to another EU Member State and which are supplied or transferred VAT exempt,
shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the supply comes to
an end,

Services ongoing over a period of more than | year, that do not give rise to settlements or payments in

that period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of article 196 EU VAT
Directive, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time the provision of
the services ceases.

4.4 Cash accounting

In certain cases, it is allowed to pay VAT to the Swedish State on the basis of cash received during the
respective VAT period as opposed to mak ng payment on the basis of supplied goods and services during the
period. The option of cash accounting is available for taxable persons whose annual sales normally amount to
a maximum of SEK 3 million (approximately EUR 300,000).

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

Bad debt relief is available to taxable persons, provided that they can prove to the satisfaction of the Swedish
Tax Authority that the amount they did not receive from the customer became a bad debt in the relevant
period.

When claiming a bad debt relief, the taxable person deducts the amount of VAT he is reclaiming from the
output VAT amount his supplies in the VAT return for the period in which the loss has arisen,

‘The supplier must be able to show that a bad debt exists, He must have information showing that the customer
lacks the financial means to pay the debt, such as bankruptcy or unsuccessful execution attempts.

It should be stressed that the Swedish Tax Authorities do have a very strict approach towards the application
of a bad debt relief, so in case there is a substantial amount for which a bad debt relief is claimed, please
mak sure to disclose this fact towards the Tax Authorities.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
25% VAT EUR 250
Total to be paid EUR 1,250

If payment is received within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount.”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier has to pay EUR 250 in its VAT return,

The customer can deduct BUR 250 in its VAT return. Therefore, the supplier paid too much VAT and the

customer deducted too much, According to the Swedish Tax Authority, the supplier does not have to send a

credit invoice for the 2% if following criteria are met:

+» the period within which the cash discount is granted is stated on the invoice;

+ ifthe cash discount is used, the change of the tax base and VAT is stated on the invoice; and

+ acopy of the payment document van be presented, showing that the payment was made within the
prescribed time.

606 | Sweden

--- Page 107 ---
5.3 Open Market Value

In the case of certain transactions between related parties the tax basis shall, in certain circumstances, be calcu-
lated on the basis of the market value instead of the compensation that the seller has received or will receive.

In order to counteract the fact that the outgoing tax is reduced due to underpriced transactions, the tax base
must be reassessed in the case of sales and intra-community acyi sitions in the following cases:

+ the compensation is lower than the market value;

+ the buyer does not have full right of deduction/refund of input VAT;

+ the seller and the buyer are connected to each other; and

+ the taxable person cannot mak it lik ly that the compensation is mark t-based.

This provision can be applied, for example, to intra-group transactions where the buyer does not have a
{ull right of deduction, transactions between limited companies and their owners and transactions between.
employers and employees.

In order to prevent the right to deduction or refund of input VAT from increasing for those who conduct both

VAT taxable and non-VAT taxable transactions and provide goods or services at a lower price, a revaluation of

the tax base must be made in the following cases:

+ the compensation is lower than the market value and refers to a transaction that is VAT exempt;

+ — the seller is not entitled a full deduction/refund of input VAT;

+ the seller’s deduction/refund of input VAT regarding on purchases attributable to both the VAT taxable
and non-VAT taxable transactions, are determined by the total annual turnover;

+ the seller and the buyer are connected to each other; and

+ the seller cannot mak it lik ly that the compensation is mark t-based.

Sweden has also implemented rules in order to prevent the right to deduction or refund of input tax from
increasing for those who carry out both VAT taxable and non-VAT taxable transactions and provide goods or
services at a premium. However, so far these rules have never been applied.

The concept of open market value is defined as the amount that the buyer would have to pay to an
independent seller within the country for a comparable supply. If there is no comparable supply of goods or
services, the mark t value is made up of the costs for the supply.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

In Sweden the consideration for the returnable packaging is invoiced together with the contents of the
pack ging and thus, the consideration for the returnable pack ging is charged with the same VAT rate as its
contents, .g. 12% VAT on soft drink .

When the company responsible for the returnable pack ging system, pays out the consideration to the
company selling e.g. the soft drink , the consideration is seen as payment for a service provided by that
company and thus subject to 25% Swedish VAT.

6. VAT rates and exemptions

61 Standard rate
The standard rate is 25%.

6.2 Reduced rate

In Sweden there are two reduced rates for VAT i.e. 12 % and 6 %.

The reduced rate of 12 % applies to:
+ foodstuffs (including beverages but excluding alcoholic beverages);
* accommodation in hotels and inns as well as camping grounds;

+ anartist’s supply of own work of art and imports from outside the EU of work of art, collectors’ items

The reduced rate of 6 % applies to:

literary or artistic work

63  Super-reduced and zero rate

Sweden does not have any super-reduced rates and Sweden does not apply a zero rate. Sweden applies an
exemption with right to deduction instead (see further below).

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

In order to mak. it lik ly that the VAT exemption for intra-Community supply of goods is applicable, the
seller must, among other things, have documentation showing that the goods have been transported to another
EU Member state.

In Sweden, free examination of evidence is applied, and thus an assessment of whether the documentation
shows this, must tak place in each individual case based on what can reasonably be reyi red of the seller in
normal business conditions. Only information on an invoice about e.g. terms of delivery and delivery address
relating to the delivery of the goods, do not normally show that a cross-border transport has tak n place.
Common examples of documents that, together with an invoice, can show evidence of a cross-border
transport are transport and shipping documents. When assessing whether the documentation shows the cross-
border transport, detailed information about the goods (type, weight, qa ntity, etc.), delivery address, time of
transport, mode of transport and route can be important. Other information in the documentation may also be
of importance.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to mak it lilt ly that the VAT exemption for export supplies of goods is applicable, the seller must,
among other things, have documentation showing that the goods have been transported out of the EU.

In Sweden, free examination of evidence is applied, and thus an assessment of whether the documentation
shows this, must tak place in each individual case based on what can reasonably be reyi_ red of the seller
in normal business conditions. Only information on an invoice about ¢.g. terms of delivery and delivery
address relating to the delivery of the goods do not normally show that a cross-border transport has tak n
place. Common examples of documents that, together with an invoice, can show evidence of a cross-border
transport are export (including the confirmation of exit) and import documents from customs authorities.
When assessing whether the documentation shows the cross-border transport, detailed information about the
goods (type, weight, ya ntity, etc.), delivery address, time of transport, mode of transport and route can be
important. Other information in the documentation may also be of importance.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT Warehouse

‘The supply of certain goods (primarily raw materials such as metals, cereals and oils) & pt in special
warehouses (VAT warehouses) is exempt from VAT. Services related to goods held in a VAT warehouse are
also exempt from VAT.

A condition for the exemption from tax liability to be applicable is that the delivery of the goods is not
intended for final use or consumption. The Swedish Tax Authority considers that it is in the chain leading
up to the final consumer that the final use or consumption takes place. A delivery that constitutes a direct or
indirect cost in an activity conducted by the acqi rer that fully or partially entails the right to deduction or
reimbursement, does not constitute a delivery for final consumption.

In the case of services, it is a question of final consumption, if the services are performed on a good ina
bonded warehouse, if the good is not a commodity to be delivered.

VAT is due as soon as goods are released from the VAT warehouse. The person releasing the goods from the
VAT warehouse becomes responsible for the payment of the VAT.

7,

It is possible for a legal entity or private individual to store excisable goods with the immediate postponement
of excise duties, This suspension scheme is granted to a person that fulfils certain criteria mentioned in the
law. In order to obtain this license, the taxpayer must apply for the license at the Swedish Tax Authority.

2 Excise warehouse

7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Itis possible to put all k nds of goods in a bonded (customs) warehouse. The storage time is unlimited. A
precondition for tax exemption is that the supply of the product is not aimed at final use or consumption.

Sweden | 609

--- Page 110 ---
If the goods are placed under a customs warehouse regime, no customs duties or VAT become due. When the
goods are released from the customs warehouse for free circulation, duties and VAT become due. A customer
who declares the goods into free circulation becomes responsible for the payment of the VAI.

The release of goods from a bonded warehouse regime does not constitute an importation, if the goods are
subsege ntly placed under another customs regime,

The sale of goods while still in bond is not subject to Swedish VAT. Evidence that the sales toolp! ace in the
bonded warehouse should be kept in the files (i.e. list of the transactions from the warehouse keeper, customs
documents, the agreements, the invoices, etc.).

Services related to bonded goods are exempt from VAT.

7.3 Option for taxation

For the letting of immovable property, the landlord may opt to tax the lease for VAT purposes. The option for
taxable lease is only possible, if the lessee carries out VAT taxable business activity, in full or partially, in the
rented premises/property. In addition, the rental relationship must also last for 12 months or longer.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In Sweden, there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities with a Swedish VAT number
established in Sweden. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles 44 and
196 EU VAT Directive. In addition there is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity
supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in Sweden.

8.1.1 General reverse charge — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

General reverse charge applies to:
+ all supplies of (im)movable goods;

+ the supply of services related to immovable property; and

+ installation/assembly supplies

made by a non-established taxable person in Sweden to customers that are registered for VAT in Sweden. A
taxable person with a fixed establishment in Sweden is deemed to be a foreign taxable person where general
reverse charge may apply, provided that the Swedish establishment does not intervene in the supply.

If a foreign non-established taxable person is obliged to register for VAT, due to e.g. B2C supplies, it remains
subject to the general reverse charge procedure on its B2B supplies. However, it can opt for VAT liability by
indicating on its VAT registration form that it wants to charge VAT on all taxable supplies, including supplies
that fall under the general reverse charge mechanism with regard to B2B supplies.

If general reverse charge applies, no VAT should be mentioned on the invoice. The VAT identification number
of the customer must be mentioned on the invoice. The invoice should, among other information, also refer
to the application of the reverse charge mechanism by using the following text on the invoice: “omvand
betalningsskt dighet”

Please note that the general reverse charge does not apply to the letting out of immovable property (see
chapter 7.3 above) or services of travel agencies, entertainment, sport and culture.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

‘The reverse charge applies for certain construction services. According to these rules, a subcontractor!

middleman that purchases and sells construction services should report and account for the VAT in Sweden.

This type of reverse charge is mandatory. Examples of construction services that fall under the reverse charge

mechanism are:

+ land and foundation work (e.g. demolition of houses and land investigations);

* construction and plant work e.g. construction of houses, erection of other buildings and road
construction);

* construction of installation work (e.g. electrical installation, insulation, water and ventilation work);

+ completion work on buildings (e.g. rendering and facade work, carpentry, floor and walls coverings,

+ painting and glazing work):

+ clearing afier construction; and

+ leasing of construction and plant machinery with operators and leasing of personnel for services listed above.

The domestic reverse charge also applies to the following transactions:
+ certain transactions involving gold; and
* certain transactions involving scrap of metal etc.

Since the 1 April 2021, domestic reverse charge also applies on the domestic sale of certain goods between
taxable persons. These goods are:
+ mobile phones;
+ integrated circuits;
+ game consoles;
* tablets; and
+ laptops.
Sweden | 611

--- Page 112 ---
Businesses exempt from VAT will not be subject to this reverse charge. Moreover, there is an invoice
threshold of SEK 100,000 (approximately EUR 10,000), which will determine whether reverse charge is to be
applied or nol, This domestic reverse charge was introduced as a consege nce of substantial VAT Craud.

A supplier must refer to the reverse charge. The following text could be mentioned on the invoice: “reverse
charge — Article 199 of the EU VAT Directive 2006/112/EC”.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Due to Swedish rules, managing directors of businesses or members of the board, can face personal
conseye nces for unpaid taxes in case of fraudulent behavior or criminal proceedings. It is not necessary that
the fraudulent behavior is in the hand of the taxpayer itself, we have seen cases where this personal liability
hits persons that may be involved (innocently) in VAT fraud schemes such as MITC schemes or “cross-
invoicing” schemes.

9. Recovery of input VAT

oO Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is not recoverable in case of;

* where it is not possible to obtain an income tax deduction for business entertainment (except for meals for
representing purposes where VAT is recoverable up to a taxable base of SEK 300 (approximately EUR 30));

+ purchase or lease of a permanent dwelling: and

+ acqi sition of passenger cars (or motorcycles), except in certain cases (e.g. passenger cars purchased by
taxi companies and driving schools).

9.2 Excess input VAT
Excess input VAT reported in the VAT return is normally immediately repaid by the Swedish Tax Authority,

9.3 Adjustment periods

‘The adjustment period for immovable property is 10 years following the year of first use. The adjustment
period for movable property is 5 years following the year of first use.

9.4 VAT refund for foreigners

Foreign taxable persons can obtain a refund of VAT on purchased goods and services provided that:

+ the VAT relates to an acquisition or import attributable to a supply in a business conducted abroad;

+ the supply, if made within the EU, would have been taxable or entitled a refund in the EU Member State
where the supply was made; and

+ the supply would have been subject to VAT or the VAT would have been refundable if the supply would
have had been carried out in Sweden.

9.4.1 VAT refund to EU taxable persons
A taxable person resident within the European Union must deliver an electronic application to the competent
authorities where the taxable person is resident in order to obtain a VAT refund, The competent authorities
must receive the application at the latest by 30 September of the calendar year following the refund period.

The application must cover at least 3 and no more than 12 consecutive calendar months. The application may
cover less than 3 months if it relates to the remainder of a calendar year.

If the refund application relates to at least 3 months, but not a full calendar year, the VAT due for refund must

exceed SEK 4,000 (approximately EUR 400). [f the application covers a full calendar year or the remainder
of it, the VAT due for refund is regi red to exceed SEK 500 (approximately EUR 50).

612 | Sweden

--- Page 113 ---
An application should contain the following information:

+ applicant’s name and full address;

+ address for contact by electronic means;

+ applicant's VAT identification number or tax identification number,

+ applicant's bank details;

+ adescription of the business for which the goods and services have been purchased;

+ the refund period in question; and

+ an explanation that the applicant has only supplied refundable goods and services in Sweden in the
current period.

For each invoice or importation document the following information is regi red:

+ name and full address of the supplier;

+ the VAT ID number of the supplier;

+ the prefix for Sweden under Article 215 of the EU VAT Directive;

+ the date and number of the document;

+ the taxable basis and the VAT amount;

+ the deductible share as percentage if the applicant has made both deductible and non-ceductible
transactions;

* the VAT amount that gives a right to refund in SEK; and

+ the type of the purchased goods and services must be described by codes. The applicant must provide
farther information regarding the codes if needed.

A decision shall be communicated to the applicant within 4 months from the date the application was
submitted to the Tax Authority, if no further information has been reye sted. If further information is
rege sted the period in which the authorities must mak a decision is extended up to 8 months.

The refunded amount shall be paid no later than 10 work ng days from the deadline to issue a decision.
An appeal must be received no later than the 6 year after the end of the calendar year covered by the

application,

If the refund application relates to at least 3 months, but not a full calendar year, the VAT due for refund must

The applicant must enclose original invoices to the application specifying the VAT. When an invoice refers to

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

According to the invoicing regi rements in the Swedish VAT Act, an invoice must contain the following
information:
+ date of invoice;
* invoice number;
+ the seller’s VAT registration number;
+ the buyer's VAT registration number, if the buyer is liable for VAT for the supply or provision or i
is an exempt intra-community sale of goods;
+ seller’s full name and address;
+ the buyer’s full name and address;
+ the quantily and nature of the goods or the scope and nature of the services;
+ — date of delivery of goods or performance of services or date of payment for advances or on account;
+ the tax base for each tax rate;
+ VAT rate:
» the amount of VAT to be paid;
+ ifthe buyer issues the invoice, the information “self-invoicing”;
* incase of exemption from tax liability, a reference to the relevant provision in the Swedish VAT Act or in
the VAT Directive or another information about the exemption from tax liability;
+ if the buyer is obliged to pay the value added tax, the information “reverse charge”;
+ when delivering a new means of transport to another EU country, information about the new means of
transport;
+ when applying the special regime for travel agencies, the information “marginal profit taxation for travel
agencies”; and
+ when applying the special scheme for used goods etc., the information “marginal profit taxation for used
goods”; and “marginal profit taxation for works of art” or “marginal profit taxation for collectors’ items or
antige s”.

10.1.2 Time limits

An invoice needs to be issued according to generally accepted accounting principles. When it comes to intra-
Community transactions an invoice must be issued no later than the 15" day in the month after the goods
were delivered or services supplied.

An invoice relating to building and construction services, or goods that are supplied in connection with

such services, can be issued by the end of the second month following the month in which the services were
provided or the goods were delivered.

614 | Sweden

--- Page 115 ---
10.1.3 Curreney

A taxable person who issues invoices in a currency other than SEK must specify the VAT also in SEK. A
taxable person with EUR as the accounting currency and invoices in EUR can state the Swedish VAT in EUR
instead of in SEK.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

One of the following exchange rates must be used to convert foreign currencies into SEK: a joint MID-PRICE
established by Nasdaq) MX Stockol m AB, published on the website of Central Banlof Sweden (Sveriges
Riksbank), www.riksbank.se at the time of the chargeable event; or the latest exchange rate published by the
European Central Bank t the time of the chargeable event.

Conversion via the European Central Bankw ith other currencies than EUR must be made with the conversion
rate of EUR for each respective currency.

10.1.5 — Self-billing

Under Swedish VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed provided that the following pre-requisites are fulfilled:
+ there is a pre-arranged agreement on self-billing between the supplier and the purchaser;

+ itis clear from the invoice that it was issued by the customer; and

+ the customer and the supplier have procedures for approval of issued invoices.

There are no special formal regi rements for the self-billing agreement. The supplier is always responsible
for the content of the invoice even when the invoice is issued by the customer. A procedure of approving the
invoice could be that the supplier does not object to an invoice issued by the customer. Please note that if self-
billing is used, a separate seye ntial number chain must be put in place for each supplier.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ identity of the supplier of the goods and/or services;

+ type of goods or services supplied;

+ the VAT amount payable or the information needed to calculate it;

* specific, unambiguous reference to the initial invoice and the details that are being amended (on a credit note).

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if the amount of the invoice is not higher than 4,000 SEK
(approximately EUR 400) inclusive of VAT, if the invoice issued is a credit note or if the commercial use in
the relevant business sector, administrative practice or the technical conditions for issuing the invoice mak it
difficult to comply with all the information mandatory in a full invoice (see section 10.1).

It should be noted that for certain supplies a full invoice must always be issued irrespective of amount to
invoice etc.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices
Invoices need to be stored for 7 years

Accounting information that a company receives must, as a starting point, be preserved in the form the
information had when it arrived at the company.

Accounting information may however be transferred in a form other than the form in which the information
was originally received. This applies both if the original document is in paper or electronic form and
regardless of the form in which the information is transferred. If the transfer is made in a secure manner,
from 1 July 2024, the company no longer needs to & ep the accounting information in the original form. A
company may thus destroy the original document if the information that constitutes an invoice is transferred
to another paper document or electronic document. A prereqi site for this is that the transfer does not involve
any rislof the information being changed or lost.

According to the Swedish VAT Act, the transfer to another form must be done in such a way that the
requirement for securing the invoice can still be fulfilled.

Sweden | 615

--- Page 116 ---
10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoicing is only allowed, if the customer agrees to receive the invoice electronically. Apart from
this reyi rement, there are no conditions for applying electronic invoicing. The electronic invoice can be sent
in any format, including pdf.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

The standard reporting period for VAT is a calendar ya ter. If an annual turnover exceeds SEK 40 million
(approximately EUR 4 million), the VAT return must be submitted on a monthly basis. The VAT may be
reported once a year if the annual turnover does not exceed SEK | million (approximately EUR 100,000).

‘Taxable persons with an annual turnover less than SEK 40 million (approximately EUR 4 million) may apply
for monthly filings by submitting a request to the Swedish Tax Authority.

Electronic filing is also possible but not compulsory.

‘There are no differences regarding VAT retum period and electronic filing for non-established taxable persons
as compared to resident taxable persons.
10.2.2. Filing of VAT return and payment

The VAT retums should be filed and paid to the Swedish Tax Authority on the following dates. The VAT payment
is duc on the same day. The deadlines are applicable for both resident as well as for foreign taxable persons.

+ Quarterly VAT returns:

Reporting period Due date

10.2.3 European sales listing

According to the main rule, the EC sales listing shall be submitted every calendar month for goods and
every calendar quarter for services. If the European sales listing includes both goods and services, it shall be
submitted on a monthly basis.

In addition to the other regi rements that apply for intra~Community sales of goods within the EU, the
following applies from January 1, 2020. The purchaser is regi red to have a valid VAT registration number in
another EU Member State in order for the supplier to be able to mak _ an intra-Community EU sale of goods.
When acyi ring the goods or service, the purchaser must state his VAT registration number to the supplier.
10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

There are no national recapitulative statements in Sweden.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period in Sweden is 7 years (including the present year). This means that in the year 2025,
the last open year for assessments is the year 2019,

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

There are no penalties for late registration, However, interest charges will be assessed in cases of late
registration for failure to report VAT on time.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or an European sales listing on time

If the VAT return is not filed on time, the late filing penalty is 625 SEK (approximately EUR 62).

The late filing penalty for European sales listings is SEK 1,250 (approximately EUR 125).

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the VAT authorities

If the VAT was reported in the wrong reporting period, the tax penalty is 2% of the VAT amount provided
that the late filing does not exceed 4 months. In other cases, the tax penalty is 5% of the VAT amount for late
filing,

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correetly

If the taxable person has submitted incorrect information regarding VAT, a general tax penalty of 20% may be
levied on the incorrectly reported VAT amount.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

‘The Swedish Tax Authority may decide to mak a criminal complaint.

10.4 Intrastat
In Sweden, a separate Intrastat return needs to be submitted to Statistics Sweden (SCB).

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

The Intrastat threshold for dispatches arrivals is SEK 12,000,000 (approximately EUR 1,200,000). The
Intrastat threshold for arrival is SEK. 15,000,000 (approximately EUR 1,500,000). The liability to file the
Intrastat returns begins in the month in which the threshold is exceeded. The taxable person will then be
contacted by Statistics Sweden which will inform the taxable person of the liability to file Intrastat returns.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which needs to be reported for both the arrival and the dispatches statistics is:
+ EU Member State of origin/destination;

+ Partner ID (the recipient’s VAT number/VAT registration number)

* nature of the transaction;

+ 8-digit commodity code of the goods;

+ net weight of the goods; and

+ invoice value of the goods in SEK.

Sweden | 617

--- Page 118 ---
For some goods, a supplementary unit, e.g. number of units, must be reported.

‘The rules on Intrastat reporting apply in the same way for companies that engage in distance selling, i.e. an
intrastat needs to be submitted to SCB once the Intrastat threshold has been exceeded.

Switzerland

The most important Swiss laws in the field of the VAT are the following:

+ Bundesgesetz liber die Mehrwertsteuer (Mehrwertsteuergesetz, MWSTG), hereafter referred to as the
VAT Law;

+ Mehrwertsteuerverordnung (MWSTV);

+ Verordnung des EFD iiber die Steuerbefreiung von Inlandlieferungen von Gegenstinden zwecks Ausfuhr
im Reiseverkehr;

+ Verordnung der ESTV iiber die Hohe der Saldosteuersitze nach Branchen und Tatigkeiten;

+ Verordnung des EFD itber die steuerbefreite Einfuhr von Gegenstiinden in kleinen Mengen, von
unbedeutendem Wert oder mit geringfiigigem Steuerbetrag; and

+ Verordnung des EFD iiber die Verzugs- und die Vergtitungszinssiitze.

VAT is referred to as “Mehrwertsteuer”or “MWSt”.

The VAT Law has been revised by the Swiss Parliament in a number of areas and put into force on 1 January
2018, 1 January 2019, 1 January 2020 and | January 2025,

1. Taxable persons

11 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

In principle, any business having its registered office, its domicile or its permanent establishment in
Switzerland is subject to Swiss VAT, if it

+ independently carries out a professional or commercial activity,

+ acts externally under its own name and

+ has the aim of sustainably generating revenue from supplies of goods and services.

However, as far as the global annual turnover does not exceed the threshold of CHF 100,000 (approximately
EUR 105,000), the business is exempt from the tax liability. A voluntary registration is possible.

A higher threshold of CHF 250,000 (approximately EUR 262,500) is applicable to non-profit, voluntarily-run
sports or cultural associations and charitable institutions.

No VAT registration is required, respectively is possible, if the business provides exclusively services exempt
from VAT (without right to deduct input VAT).

In order to get a Swiss VAT number, an online questionnaire has to be completed and submitted to the Federal
Tax Administration (FTA).

The mailing address of the FTA is:
Eidg. Steuerverwaltung
Hauptabteilung Mehrwertsteuer
Schwarztorstrasse 50

CH - 3003 Bern

Switzerland

The German version of the online questionnaire can be downloaded at the following link: hitps./tinyurl.com/3vv2rrjt

1.2 _ Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

In principle, the same rules apply to non-resident taxable persons. Thus, a business with a global annual
turnover of CHF 100,000 (approximately EUR 105,000) or more is liable for Swiss VAT, if it makes domestic
supplies regardless of the volume of the local turnover.

Switzerland | 619

--- Page 120 ---
However, a taxable person established abroad is not obliged to register for VAT purposes in Switzerland, if it
exclusively performs supplies that are subject to the reverse charge mechanism (Article 10 paragraph 2 lit. b
in conjunction with Articles 45 - 49 of the VAT Law).

The mentioned exemption from the tax liability does not apply in case the non-resident taxable person renders
telecommunication services or electronic services to non-taxable persons (¢.g. a private person).

In order to obtain a Swiss VAT number, an online questionnaire must be completed. Moreover, a proxy duly
signed by the taxable person as well as a declaration of acceptance duly signed by the fiscal representative
(see chapter 1.4) must be submitted to the FTA. The mailing address is the same as for domestic businesses
(see also chapter 1.1). In the past, non-resident taxable persons had to furnish a bank guarantee in favour of
the FTA, issued by a bank established in Switzerland. Alternatively, the amount of security could be remitted
to the account of the FTA. Since 2025 the FTA abandons the right to request a security, In particular cases, the
FTA may ask for additional information (e.g. articles of association, an excerpt from the commercial register
and a certificate of status as a taxable person).

Generally, the FTA assigns the VAT number within 3 weeks after receipt of the required documents.

Regarding the need to appoint a fiscal representative, see also chapter 1.4. The link to the German version of
the online questionnaire is as follows: https://tinyurl.com/3vv2rrjt

13. VAT grouping

Legal entities (in particular, legal entities, partnerships and natural persons) established in Switzerland or
having a branch in Switzerland can apply to be considered as a single taxable person (so-called VAT group)
for the imposition of Swiss VAT provided that all the entities are under the uniform control of one legal entity
(Article 13 of the VAT Law). Also legal entities without business activity can be included in the VAT group.
Legal entities established outside Switzerland cannot be part of the VAT group. The termination of the VAT
grouping is possible per end of the tax period.

All supplies of goods and services of the VAT group members performed to third parties are treated as being
carried out by the VAT group. On the other hand, all the supplies of goods and services within the VAT group
will not be subject to Swiss VAT.

As long as the members belong to the VAT group, they are jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT
debts of the group.

14 Fiscal representative

For non-resident taxable persons a direct registration is not possible. Thus, in order to become registered for
VAT purposes in Switzerland, the foreign taxable person must appoint a fiscal representative domiciled in
Switzerland. No special license is needed in order to act as a fiscal representative.

For the VAT registration, a proxy must be submitted together with the declaration of the acceptance of the
fiscal representative and the documents mentioned in chapter 1.2.

As of | January 2025, electronic platforms might apply for a direct registration. In this case it has to be
ensured that the taxable person will comply with the procedural obligations.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

The VAT Law provides for a mandatory application of a specific procedure in case of a transfer of the totality
or part of the assets. If the VAT, calculated on the sales price at the statutory rate, exceeds CHF 10,000
(approximately EUR 10,500) or the sale is made to an affiliated person, the taxable person must fulfill the
reporting and tax payment obligation by a notification procedure which applies in the following cascs to:

620 | Switzerland

--- Page 121 ---
*  reorganisations under Articles 19 and 61 of the Federal Law of 14 December 1990 governing the Direct
Federal Tax; or

+ other transfers of a totality of assets or part, thereof, to another taxable person in the context of an
incorporation, a liquidation, a reorganisation or of another legal transaction foreseen in the Merger Law
dated 3 October 2003.

The notifications are to be made in the course of ordinary reporting.

The notification procedure also applies if the transferee only becomes a taxable person as a result of the
transfer of the totality or part of the assets. If the notification procedure is applied, the transferee takes over
from the transferor the pro rata for input VAT deduction and the assessment basis for the assets transferred.
The invoice will not show any VAT but a corresponding reference that the notification procedure has been
applied. Although being mandatory, if the notification procedure was not applied and the parties have
(erroneously) applied VAT on the transaction, although the legal preconditions were fulfilled, the FTA will not
force the parties to retroactively apply the notification procedure as long as the VAT has been paid to the FTA.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

In Switzerland supplies free of charge are only taxed if the recipient is closely linked (Article 24 para 2 of the
VAT Law). Closely linked persons are the owners of at least 20 percent of the share capital of a company or of
a corresponding share in a parinership or persons closely associated with them. The tax base for such supplies
free of charge is the value that would be agreed between independent third parties.

Apart from that, the VAT deducted has to be corrected under certain conditions. This is the case if goods or
services are used for non-business purposes, especially for private use or for transactions that are exempt

from VAT (without a right to deduct the input VAT).

However, in case of gifts of up to CHF 500 (approximately EUR 525) per person and year, as well as
promotional gifts and samples of goods to generate taxable turnover, the business purpose is presumed by
law. Thus, no correction of the VAT deduction is required.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply
As Switzerland is not within the EU, there is no import followed by an intra-Community supply.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

In connection with importation, the import VAT can be postponed by the centralized billing procedures of the

Federal Customs Administration (FCA). This procedure is recommended for all importers and shippers who

pay regular import taxes on merchandise. The advantages of the centralised billing process for the account

holder are:

* cashless customs clearance;

+ — shorter waiting periods at the customs offices. The shipments will be released after adoption of the
customs declaration and after any revision of the goods; and

+ the payment period for VAT is 60 days.

‘The applicant needs to complete an application form and send it to “Bundesamt fiir Zoll und Grenzsicherheit,
Abteilung Finanzen, Taubenstrasse 16, CH - 3003 Bern”.

In addition, a security must be granted in one of the following two ways:

+ general guarantees by banks or insurance companies with headquarters in Switzerland, which are under
the supervision of the Swiss Financial Market Supervisory (FINMA), respectively, are mentioned on
the list of the Federal Supervision of private insurances. The application must be filed with form 22.10.
Such guarantees have to be sent directly to the Federal Customs Authorities mentioned above. For the
acceptance of the guarantee the customs administration will charge a fee deposit of collateral securities

Switzerland | 621

--- Page 122 ---
(i.e. good Swiss bonds or medium-term notes, denominated in bearer form) issued in favour of the
customs administration on a toll depot established by the Swiss National Bank in Bern; or

+ cash deposit (without interest) by payment to the postal account of the Federal Customs Authorities. The
account will only be opened after both the membership application, and the security have arrived at the
Federal Customs Authorities.

The amount of security is calculated as follows:

50% of the average charges accrued in 2 weeks; and

+ fora taxable person: at least 20% of the VAT charges accrued during a period of 60 days; and
+ for non-taxable persons: 100% of the VAT charges accrued within a period of 60 days.

Once the application form and, depending on the chosen method of payment, the security has been rendered,
the applicant is informed by the Federal Customs Authorities about the account number assigned. ‘This
number will be communicated in writing, Only after receiving this confirmation the applicant can use the
centralized billing account.

To ensure a smooth process, it is important that the account number assigned is mentioned on the customs
declaration, the documents of payments and in the correspondence with the customs authorities, Therefore,
it is advisable to inform the involved shipping company about the procedure in place, as they should also be
empowered to make severance payments on the account.

The granted authorization is subject to random checks by the customs office.

3. Place of supply

3 Consignment and call-off stock

The simplification for consignment stocks applies to the import of goods. The simplification for consignment
stocks provides that the foreign supplier does not need to register for VAT in Switzerland, if the import VAT
for the goods, which shall be supplied within Switzerland to a consignee, is assessed on the value of the
consideration to be paid by the consignee for the goods purchased from the consignor. Under this condition
the supply of the goods to the consignee within Switzerland, is deemed to take place abroad (see Article 4
Swiss VAT Ordinance).

The simplification rule can, therefore, only be applied, if the consideration to be paid is known at the time

of importation, Otherwise, the simplification cannot be applied and the consignor must register for VAT in
Switzerland, The same would apply, if at the time of import the buyer of the goods is unknown as the goods will
only be sold after being placed in the stock. In case the preconditions for the simplification are fulfilled, the import
VAT due on the value of the goods transferred into the stock can be deducted as input VAT by the consignee.

No time limit applies in relation to the simplification. The preconditions must be fulfilled prior to the first
importation.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

As per the very broad definition of a supply of goods according to Article 3 lit. d of the VAT Law, any supply
of goods with installation or assembly performed in Switzerland is treated as a domestic supply.

Thus, any (foreign) entrepreneur with a global annual turnover of CHF 100,000 (approximately EUR
105,000) or more is liable for Swiss VAT, if it makes domestic supplies regardless of the volume of the local
turnover. Thus, the supplier is obliged to register for VAT in Switzerland (see also chapters 1.1 and 1.2).

The tax liability of foreign entrepreneurs begins with the first provision of a supply with installation in
Switzerland (Art. 14 para. | lit. b VAT Law). However, in case of an advance payment, the date of the invoice
is determinant or, if no invoice is issued, the receipt of the consideration.

622 | Switzerland

--- Page 123 ---
‘The domestic reverse charge procedure does apply in cases where the foreign supplier is not registered for
VAT purposes - rightly or wrongly - and only supplies with installation connected with immovable property
are concemed. It covers inter alia: excavation and earthworks, construction works, erection and dismantling
of prefabricated elements, alterations, renovations, repairs, dismantling and demolition works, maintenance,
servicing (painting and cleaning works) and refurbishments.

The reverse charge mechanism also applies in case the customer is a non-taxable person in Switzerland, e.g.
a natural person or a legal person who does not perform economic activities. However, if the customer is not
a taxable person, the VAT liability is only shifted to him, if the supplies received from the foreign supplier
exceed CHF 10,000 (approximately EUR 10,500) in a calendar year (see also chapters 1.1 and 1.2).

3.3 Distance sales threshold

3.3.1 Ingeneral
As Switzerland is not within the EU, there is no distance sales threshold. Due to this fact, the goods will be
taxed when imported into Switzerland. In principle, the Swiss private individual is the importer of record.
However, the seller might apply for a special procedure as well as a Swiss VAT number in order to be able to
act as importer of record. If that is done, then Swiss VAT must be invoiced to the Swiss private individual.

Since 1 January 2019, in case of distance sales the place of supply will be also shifted to Switzerland (Art. 7

para. 3 lit. b of the VAT Law) provided that

+ goods are physically supplied from abroad to Switzerland;

* the import of the goods is VAT-exempt according to Art. 53 para. 1 lit. a. This is the case if the value
of the goods imported (including transport costs) amounts to less than CHF 62 (for goods taxed at the
standard rate of 8.1 %) respectively less than CHF 193 (for goods taxed at the reduced rate of 2.6 %);

+ the goods are transported / dispatched by the supplier; and

+ the respective turnover exceeds the threshold of CHF 100,000 (approximately EUR 105,000).

The shipment of the place of supply results in the tax liability of the (foreign) distance-selling company and
Swiss VAT must be invoiced to the Swiss buyer.

3.3.2 Sales via electronic platforms

From 2025, electronic platforms will, under certain conditions, be treated, as if they themselves had supplied
the goods for the sales they facilitate. Thus, if the place of supply is in Switzerland, they are liable for VAT on
these sales.

If the electronic platform is registered for VAT purposes, the first (fictitious) supply between the seller and the
platform is either taxable abroad or exempt from VAT (without the right to deduct the input VAT). Thus, the
seller established abroad and doing business only via such platforms, will not be subject to Swiss VAT anymore.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

As Switzerland is not a Member State of the EU, no EU simplified triangulation applies. The basic rule for
the place of supply according to Article 7 of the VAT Law must be applied, even in case of a chain supply.
However, if goods are transported from the first supplier in Switzerland directly to the final recipient outside
Switzerland (j.¢. exported), the zero rating for export applies to all foreign or domestic suppliers in the chain
supply. The same applies in case the goods are picked up by the last supplier in the chain supply, provided
that the goods that are exported are not being used in Switzerland in accordance with Article 23 paragraph 3
of the VAT Law.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

As Switzerland is not within the EU, there is no threshold for the intra-Community acquisition of goods by
non-taxable persons.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Switzerland has not implemented any effective use and enjoyment rules.

Switzerland | 623

--- Page 124 ---
4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

‘As regards (he lax poinl, the Swiss VAT legislation makes a distinction depending on the accounting method.
In case the taxable person applied for the standard accounting method (i.e. reporting on the basis of agreed
considerations), the VAT becomes chargeable at the time the invoice has been issued. The entitlement to input
VAT deduction arises on the date of receipt of the invoice.

In case the taxable person applied for the special accounting method (i.e. reporting on the basis of collected
considerations), the VAT becomes chargeable at the time the payment has been received. The entitlement to
input VAT deduction arises at the time of payment of the invoice.

42 Advance payments

In case of advance payments, the VAT becomes chargeable at the time the payment has been received. This
applies regardless of the accounting method.

4.3 Continuous supplies

In principle, the same rules are applied in case of continuous supplies. Moreover, the Swiss VAT legislation
does not provide a time limit before which an invoice must be issued (cf. chapter 10.1.2).

4.4 Cash accounting

The cash accounting regime is available to microenterprises having mainly cash transactions (e.g. take-away
shops, hairdressing salon, kiosk). The cash accounting regime requires that the taxable person has a cash hook
in which the revenue and expenditure are entered in a timely manner.

The VAT becomes chargeable when a payment has been received from the contracting party. The entitlement
to input VAT deduction arises at the time of payment of the invoice.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

In case of a correction of the consideration paid or agreed by the customer of the supply, the VAT due must
be adjusted at the time when the correction is booked or the corrected consideration is collected (Article 41
paragraph | of the VAT Law).

Until now, no code of practice has been published by the FTA in regard to the mentioned provision, especially
ag to how the bad debt rule is to be applied. However, in the former “Instructions 2008 regarding VAT”
applicable before 2010, the FTA defined that in case of a loss the consideration can be corrected in the VAT
return, only at the time when the supplier qualifies the outstanding debt as a bad debt and, therefore, cancels
the outstanding debt definitely from the books (Instructions 2008 Z 252). It is not necessary for the supplier to
have received an official notice of loss.

No correction can be done in case the supplier books an allowance for a bad debt (i.e. a deleredere risk) but
eaves the outstanding debt in the books.

‘Tax base: Special cases

624 | Switzerland

--- Page 125 ---
5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:
Supply of goods — CHF 1000
8.1% VAT CHF 81

Total to be paid — CHF 1981
If payment is received within the next 14 days,a 2% cash discount is granted.

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. Thus, in the supplier’s VAT return CHF 20 can be deducted as
payment reduction from the total sales. On the other hand, the customer is only entitled to deduct CHF 79,40
in his VAT return. It is not required that the supplier issues a credit note for the remaining amount.

5.3 Open market value

‘The Swiss VAT Law stipulates explicitly that in case of the supply of goods and services to closely related
persons, the consideration is deemed to be the amount that would be agreed between independent third parties
(Art. 24 paragraph 2 of the VAT Law). This rule applies not only if a (reduced) consideration has been agreed,
but also in case of a service free of charge.

5.4 Returnable packaging

The Swiss VAT Law stipulates explicitly that deposits, namely on returnable packaging and containers, are
not part of the consideration because of a lack of supply.

6. VAT rates and exemptions

6.1 Standard rate
‘The standard VAT rate in Switzerland is 8.1%.

6.2 Reduced rate

A special rate of 3.8% applies to the supply of accommodation services.
In addition, a reduced rate of 2.6% applies to the supply of:

+ food:

* water;

+ agricultural products;

+ medical products;

+ newspapers, magazines, books;

+ electronic newspapers, magazines and books with no promotional character;
+ some cultural services; and

+ sanitary protection products for women (as of 1 January 2025).

6.3 Zero rate

The supply of goods by a vendor via an electronic platform, provided that the person enabling the supply is
deemed to be the supplier according to Art. 20a of the VAT Law and registered for VAT purposes.

1 Exemptions

7.4 Exemptions with the right of deduction

‘The following supplies are zero rated (VAT exempt with a right of deduction):
+ export of goods;
+ leasing or chartering of goods which are transported or dispatched directly abroad and are used
predominantly abroad by the recipient;
Switzerland | 625

--- Page 126 ---
+ — supply of goods which are under customs control in Switzerland (e.g. goods under a transit procedure, a
bonded warehouse procedure or a temporary customs procedure);

+ — supply of goods which are stored in a bonded warehouse;

+ transport or dispatch of goods in connection with the import of goods;

* transport or dispatch of goods in connection with the export of goods;

» rendering of transport scrvices and ancillary logistic activities, such as loading, unloading, transshipment,
clearing or temporary warehousing, abroad or in connection with goods that are under customs control;

+ — international flights and related services;

+ services of intermediaries acting expressly in the name of and for account of others, if the brokered supply
is either exempt from the tax under this article or is physically carried out exclusively abroad; and

+ services rendered in their own name by travel agents and organisers of events, to the extent they draw on
deliveries and services of third parties, which are carried out by them abroad;

Under the current VAT Law, the exporter can supply any evidence in order to prove that the goods supplied
have been exported. There is no legal obligation anymore to furnish an (electronic) export declaration issued
by the customs authorities, However, the export declaration is still taken as the most appropriate proof that the
goods have left Switzerland.

The tax exemption for goods hought by touri

s only possible if all of the following conditions are met:

+ retail price of the goods must be at least, including VAT, CHF 300 (approximately EUR 315). The retail
price excluding VAT must be indicated in the export document;

+ customer may not be domiciled in Switzerland (cf. Article 3 lit. a of the VAT Law). Moreover, the
nationality of the customer is irrelevant;

+ goods must be intended for the personal use of the customer or as gifts;

+ goods must be exported to a foreign customs territory by the customer within 30 days of reccipt;

+ proof of exportation can be provided either with a confirmed export document or with an unconfirmed
export document together with an import assessment issued by a foreign customs authority; and

+ the export document must be drawn up in the name of the customer and may contain solely goods
supplied to this person. Joint declarations with goods for various customers are not permitted.

If one or more of the mentioned conditions are not met, the supplier must tax the goods supplied.

Forms no. 11.49 and no. 11.49 [A] remain valid, but they are no longer printed. Instead, the FTA has drafted a
sample export document, which can be downloaded from its website.

7 Warehousing

721 VAT warehouse
‘There is no such arrangement available in either the VAT Law or Swiss Customs Law.

7.2.2 Bonded (customs) warehouse

The Swiss Customs Law provides for a number of bonded warehouses that have been drafted reflecting the
provisions of the European Customs Code.

A supply of goods stored in a bonded warehouse can be zero rated, provided that it can be proven to the
satisfaction of the FTA that the goods were under customs control when being supplied. The proof is normally
furnished by the stock accounting.

The term customs debtor is broadly interpreted by the FCA, Anybody who is involved in the movement of
the goods (supplier, customer, freight forwarder, customs agent etc.) can be held liable for payment of the
customs debt (i.e. import VAT and customs duties) irrespective of whether or not he is mentioned on the
customs declaration,

626 | Switzerland

--- Page 127 ---
73 Exemptions without right to deduction

The following supplies are VAT exempt without the right to deduct the input VAT:
public postal services according to Article 3 of the Postal Law;

+ hospital and medical treatment and closely linked activities by hospitals, medical and diagnostic centers
in the field of human medicine;

+ human medical care provided by doctors, dentists, psychotherapists, chiropractors, physiotherapists,
naturopaths, midwives and similar medical professionals;

+ nursing care services provided by nursing staff, nursing organisations and home aid (Spitex) or in homes
if prescribed by a doctor;

+ supply of human organs by recognised medical institutions / hospitals and of human whole blood by a
licensed organisation;

+ supply of transport services for sick, injured or disabled persons in vehicles specially designed for this
purpose;

+ _ supply of services and goods closely linked to the child and youth care by institutions specially designed
for this purpose;

+ _ provision of children’s or young people's education as well as a broad range of training such as courses,
examinations and organisational services with the exception of restaurant services and accommodation:

+ supply of staff by religious or philosophical non-profit organisations in the field of human medical care,
social assistance and security, child and youth care, education and training as well as charity;

+ supply of services and goods to their members in their common interest in return for a subscription fixed
in accordance with their rules by non-profit-making organisations with aims of a political, trade-union,
economic, religious, patriotic, philosophical, philanthropic, ecological, sporting, cultural or civic nature;

+ cultural services provided directly to the audience for which an entrance fee is due (e.g. theater, music,
film, library);

* entrance fees for sporting events and fees for taking part in them;

+ cultural services and delivery of works by their creators, such as authors, composers, film makers,
painters, sculptors and publishers;

+ insurance and reinsurance transactions, including related services performed by insurance brokers and
insurance agents;

+ abroad range of money and payment transactions such as granting and negotiation of credits, transactions
concerning deposit and current accounts, transactions concerning currency and bank notes, transactions in
shares, interests in companies ete.;

+ transfer of real property rights linked to immovable property;

+ leasing or letting of immovable property and parts thereof. However, the provision of accommodation,
the letting of premises and sites for the parking of vehicles, the letting of permanently installed equipment
and machinery and the hire of safe-deposit boxes are taxable;

+ supply at face value of postage stamps valid for use for postal services in Switzerland;

* _ betting, lotteries and other forms of gambling as far as subject to a special tax; and

* travel services resold by travel agencies and services of travel agencies linked to such services (as of 1
January 2025).

7.4 Option for taxation

For most of the VAT exempted transactions the taxable person can opt to apply VAT; there are no specific
conditions for exercising the option.

No option to tax is applicable for insurance and reinsurance transactions as nor for financial services.
The option for the transfer of rights in rem linked to immovable property and the leasing or letting of

immovable property and parts thereof, is only possible if the recipient does not use the goods exclusively for
residential purposes.
